# Cello Cool Club — All English Texts

## 001. Monday

On Sunday evening around eleven o'clock Monday already arrives in the suburbs of the week It makes no noise It waits on the doormat of time With its gray briefcase full of duties And its rain-colored coat
At dawn it enters the city without knocking It is a zealous civil servant First it repaints the sky With a thick coat of administrative gray Then it turns off the last lingering dreams And it turns on the alarm clocks All at the same time A siren's cry to announce the end of recess
Monday walks in the streets And everything stands at attention It steals the taste from the coffee It puts sand in the eyes of children It replaces kisses with handshakes It teaches people to walk in a straight line With heads bowed and shoulders slumped It checks that the clocks are on time On the time of sadness and efficiency
Under its reign the city is a large waiting room One waits for lunch One waits for the end of the day One waits for Friday Laughter is forbidden before nine in the morning Sighs are tolerated but not encouraged Even the birds sing a little less loudly As if they were afraid to disturb
But Monday is not all-powerful Look closely In the metro a young girl is reading a poem A small act of colorful disobedience On the fogged-up window of a bus A child's finger has drawn a mocking sun A man and a woman send each other a message Just one word "courage" A small lifebuoy in the ocean of the day
In the evening Monday is tired Its work is done It packs up its gray and its obligations It leaves again on tiptoe Leaving behind an exhausted city But where in the black sky The first star lights up Like a stubborn promise That all is not yet lost.

---

## 002. Jupiter, the Stormy King

After the red soldier and his silence of rust
I am the king, the giant, the big gentleman
I am not made of earth nor of regrets
I am made of gas, of anger, and of solitude
I have no ground to place my feet on
My only land is my own immense weight
I am a painter who paints only with storms
My colors are ocher, brick, and old gold
My brushes are winds of a thousand kilometers per hour
I paint ribbons of clouds that never dry
And in the middle of it all, I have my masterpiece
My great red wound
A cyclone's eye open for centuries
That stares into the void without ever blinking
It is the signature of my melancholy
I have a large family of little moons
My court of silent and faithful servants
They dance around me to try to calm me
They polish my great ball of gas with their little light
They do not understand
That you cannot calm a storm
That is inside of yourself
I am the big protective brother of the family
The goalkeeper of the solar system
With my immense body, I catch the stray stones
The drunken comets that would like to do harm
To my fragile little sisters, Earth or Venus
It is a thankless and solitary job
They are too busy with their little lives
To see the dangers I divert for them
So I turn heavily in the dark
The beating heart of the great celestial clockwork
A king without a castle, a god without prayers
A father who protects his children from afar
And who can only show them
His tormented and magnificent face
I am the great storm that will never end.

---

## 003. Mars, the Old Soldier

After my sister Earth, noisy and full of life,
I am the fourth, the tired soldier
They call me the God of War
A ridiculous old name for an old man like me
My great wars have been over for millions of years
My armor is covered in rust
And that rust is my only color
I remember a time when I was blue
A time when I wore rivers on my face
Tears of clear water that flowed in my great canyons
Perhaps I remember the taste of rain
And the murmur of a wind that was not made of dust
That was before the great drought
Before the great silence
Today my kingdom is a red desert
My mountains are volcanoes asleep forever
My army is a storm of fine sand
That marches with no purpose and no enemy
My only treasures are two small pieces of ice
At my poles like two cold medals
I am the king of an immense silence
The guardian of a dead world
Sometimes I receive visitors
Little metal insects that my sister Earth sends
They roll slowly over my skin
They take pictures of my old scars
They search for signs of life with their little glass eyes
Like children searching for treasure in an empty attic
I let them be
I am too old for anger
Their curiosity is a small, touching thing
In my great solitude
I look at them and I look at my sister in the distance
That blue and white marble so full of noise and fury
She does not yet know
How lucky she is to have water to cry with
I have nothing left but red dust
So I wrap myself in my thin, cold atmosphere
Like in an old military blanket
And I wait
I don't know for what
I just wait.

---

## 004. Mercury, the Hurried Messenger

I am the first the closest
The little messenger of the great king Sun
I run I run I never stop
My race is my prayer and my madness
I carry an urgent message
But I have no mouth to speak it
And there is no one to hear it
I have two faces like a liar or a lover
One face turned toward him
Burned with love and with light
A land of molten rock and of despair
Where silence boils under a sky of fire
It is the face of the eternal "yes"
My other face is turned toward the void
Frozen by solitude and by night
A plain of black ice and absolute silence
Where the stars are cold pinpricks
It is the face of "never"
And I live ceaselessly between these two extremes
Between the kiss that burns and the absence that freezes
The other planets watch me from afar
They turn slowly in their queenly gowns
They see me as a little fool a nervous spark
They do not understand my haste and my devotion
They do not know what it is
To love a fire that devours you
On my skin I have scars
The memories of an old war of pebbles
Each crater is a letter of an unknown alphabet
That tells the story of my terrible birth
My message is written on my face
But no one knows how to read it
So I run
With one side of my body on fire
And the other of ice
The first word in the great king's sentence
The first servant and the first prisoner
I am the spinning top that the Sun has launched
And that will never stop turning.

---

## 005. Neptune, the Lighthouse Keeper

After the rebel lying on his side
I am the last, the distant, the forgotten
The final blue period at the end of the Sun's sentence
They gave me the name of the god of the sea
What a cruel irony
I who reign over a frozen ocean
My kingdom is a world of eternal twilight
Where the Sun's light is just one star among others
A cold and distant pinhead
My color is the deepest blue
Not the blue of life or of hope
But the blue of a deep bruise on the soul
The blue of the great void
I am the king of the wildest winds
Of drafts of two thousand kilometers per hour
My invisible and howling subjects
They are the only music in my icy silence
They tell stories of darkness and speed
The only ones who know the secrets of my surface
I once had a great dark eye
A storm to keep me company
But even it has abandoned me
It dissolved into my blue atmosphere
Leaving me even more alone
With my memories of whirlpools
My job is to be the lighthouse keeper
The last one before the great night between the stars
I keep my blue lantern lit
Not to guide ships, for there are none
But simply to say
"Here our world ends"
"Here infinity begins"
I look at my brothers and sisters
Little lights so distant
They could be memories
I am the king in exile, the guardian of the border
The god of an ocean that no longer remembers water
And I listen to the song of my furious winds
The only proof that I am not
Just a cold and blue dream.

---

## 006. Saturn, the Sad Ballerina

After my brother king Jupiter
And his great uproar of storms
I am the queen of silence
The great melancholy lady
I am not heavy I am light
They say I could float on the ocean
What a funny idea
I only dream of stopping floating
Around my waist I have my adornment and my prison
My beautiful rings of ice and dust
The other planets look at them with envy
They do not know what it is
To eternally wear the debris of a broken love
The frozen memory of a lost moon
I am a sad ballerina in a giant music box
I turn slowly on my axis
An endless pirouette without applause
My dress is a hoop of diamonds and frost
A silent carousel for the ghosts of light
A great necklace of frozen tears
That will never fall
My little moons are my only spectators
They watch me turn with faithful eyes
But they are far away
On the other side of my beautiful barrier of ice
I am the most beautiful perhaps
But I am the loneliest
Beauty is a cold thing that creates distance
I do not have the anger of Mars nor the life of Earth
I do not have the fire of the Sun nor the poison of Venus
I have only my cold and perfect beauty
And my eternal circular motion
I am the great white sigh of the solar system
The forgotten pearl at the bottom of the night's jewelry box
The dream of a prisoner
Who has decorated the walls of his cell.

---

## 007. Uranus, the Silent Rebel

After the sad dance of Saturn
I am the seventh, the strange one, the eccentric
All the other planets stand up straight
Like well-behaved soldiers or obedient spinning tops
I have lain down on my side
It was not an accident, a cosmic shoving match
It was a choice
I had had enough of looking up and down
I wanted to look at the world from the side
To see if the universe was different from that point of view
It is my small, silent revolution
My act of laziness and of disobedience
I do not spin, I roll
Like a child in the grass or a barrel of blue wine
My kingdom is of a pale and quiet color
A blue-green of a frozen lagoon
I do not have the great angers of Jupiter
Nor the ostentatious jewels of Saturn
My beauty is discreet and uneventful
I am a great calm eye floating in the dark
My few rings are so thin
They look like forgotten spider threads
And my moons have names from poetry
Miranda, Titania, Oberon
They are characters from old, sad plays
My only companions in the great cold
They understand my melancholy
From afar they look at me with curiosity
The tilted planet, the anomaly of the system
They wonder if I am sick or tired
They do not understand that rest can be a form of protest
That silence can be a strong word
So I continue my long and slow roll
In the distant suburbs of the Sun's kingdom
I ask nothing of anyone
I am at peace with my own strangeness
And I simply prove
That there is more than one way to dance
Around the great fire of life.

---

## 008. Venus, the Veiled Beauty

After the little mad messenger
I am the second
The most beautiful, they say
The shepherd's star, the evening star
The lovers on Earth look at me and sigh
They give me the name of their most beautiful goddess
Venus
They do not know
They see only my great cloak of clouds
My gown of white and golden silk
They do not see what lies beneath
My true face
If a poet came here seeking love
He would find an oven, a hell
My air is not air, it is a poison that crushes
My rain is not water, it is acid that burns
My kisses are bites of fire
I am the great queen of solitude
My kingdom is a desert of red rocks
Where volcanoes shout their stony anger
There is not a single bird, not a single flower
The silence here is not the silence of peace
It is the heavy silence of death
Nothing can live beneath my beautiful poisoned gown
They call me Earth's twin sister
A sister who went wrong
A warning, a terrible mirror
Of what love can become
When it locks itself in its own heat
And forgets to breathe
So I turn slowly on my axis
In my magnificent prison of gas
I am the brightest in the night sky
All eyes turn to me
And no one sees my sorrow
I am the promise of an impossible beauty
The love that one can only admire from very, very far away.

---

## 009. Earth, the Noisy House

After my sisters, the burned and the burning,
I am the third, the lucky one
The blue marble in the great silent game
I am not made of fire nor of poison
I am made of water, of earth, and of patience
And of a very strange thing called life
I carry on my back oceans that dream
Forests that murmur old secrets
And mountains that scratch the belly of the clouds
I have thousands of creatures
Those that crawl, those that swim, those that fly
And then I have my strangest children
Men
They have a mad talent for beauty
They know how to make a stone or a piece of wood speak
They write poems that make me weep with joy
They invent music that makes the stars dance
They know the taste of warm bread
The warmth of a friendly hand
And the sweetness of a kiss in the rain
They are my magnificent children
But they also have a mad talent for foolishness
They draw lines on my skin and call them borders
They wage war over pieces of land that belong to me
They build factories that make me cough up black smoke
They cover my meadows with cement and sadness
They speak of love and they make bombs
They are so intelligent
And so incredibly stupid
They are my despairing children
So I turn in the great silence
The only inhabited house on the street of the sun
The only planet with laughter and wars
With songs and lies
With libraries and prisons
My distant sisters are clean and tidy
They are perfect in their hell or their solitude
I am messy, I am full of scars
I am noisy and dirty and magnificent and sick
I am their mother, their home, their ship
And I wait
I wait for them to grow up a little
I wait for them to understand
That the only border that matters
Is the one between tenderness and cruelty.

---

## 010. March, the Brawler

February left the door ajar
March kicks it down with a great thrust of his shoulder
He enters shouting and laughing
He is a young rascal full of energy
A brawler with a tender heart
His first act is to throw his gloves of rain
Cold slaps on the cheeks of the city
He whistles in the chimneys
He slams the poorly closed shutters
He fights with the last remnants of winter
A street fight with no rules and no referee
Then suddenly he stops fighting
He sits on a cloud and he smiles
And his smile is the sun
A sun still pale but warm and full of promises
That melts the ice in people's hearts
And dries the tears of the last downpour
March is an impatient painter
He can no longer stand the gray and the white
With large buckets of green paint
He daubs the lawns and the branches of the trees
He yells at the flowers "Get up you lazy things"
And the crocuses and the daffodils stick their heads out
A little scared but curious
He is the great alarm clock of nature
His breath is a powerful and damp wind
That carries the smell of wet earth
The smell of life beginning again
He shakes people by the collar of their coats
And tells them to open their windows and their souls
At the end of the month he is exhausted
He has spent all his anger and all his joy
He has won his fight against winter
The world is clean and muddy and full of disorder
A magnificent battlefield full of life
So March leaves, staggering a little
Making way for the gentle hand of April
Who will come to tidy and polish his giant's work.

---

## 011. July, the Blacksmith of Summer

June fell asleep in a hammock of light
So July the blacksmith sets up his workshop
The sky is his anvil
The sun is his burning forge
And the song of the cicadas is the sound of his hammer
He takes the long and lazy days
And beats them on the anvil of the sky
To make them harder hotter more intense
He hammers the blue until it turns white with heat
He melts the asphalt of the streets
Which becomes a black and sticky molasses
He forges the silence of the crushing afternoons
Where even the birds fall silent
Under his iron hand the city empties
The inhabitants have fled to the sea
Leaving the streets to the lost tourists
And to the cats that sleep in the shade of doorways
The window shutters are closed like eyelids
The entire city is taking a long forced nap
Exhausted by the blacksmith's work
Sometimes his anger erupts
After days of relentless heat
The sky turns black as soot
The thunder is his great hammer striking the anvil
The lightning bolts are the sparks of the furious metal
And the violent rain that falls
Is the cold water where he quenches his burning work
The great hiss of the city cooling down
After the storm July is tired and satisfied
The air is clean and smells of wet dust
The world breathes again
The blacksmith has finished his day
He has created a perfect summer day
Made of crushing heat and sudden relief
He is not a gentle king like his predecessor
He is a brutal and magnificent craftsman
Who forges the strongest memories of the year.

---

## 012. April, the Watercolorist

When March the brawler has gone away shouting
April arrives on the tips of her ballet slippers
She finds the world in a mess and full of mud
A great battlefield after the victory
So she begins by crying a little
Sweet, lukewarm tears that wash the earth
A fine, quiet rain
That smells good of grass and renewal
Then she takes out her box of watercolors
She does not like the bright, garish colors of summer
She prefers pastel tones, delicate nuances
With her finest brush made of a bird's feather
She paints a tender green on the new leaves
A shy pink on the cherry blossoms
A pale yellow on the daffodils
She works with infinite patience
Each petal is a small touch of light
Sometimes for no reason she becomes sad again
She cries a little more over her work
And the people in the street open their umbrellas with a sigh
But suddenly something makes her laugh
Perhaps a child jumping in a puddle
Or a cat chasing its tail
And her laughter is the sun
That breaks through the clouds all at once
And draws a rainbow over her rainy sorrow
April is a capricious and charming artist
She changes her mind like she changes colors
She offers a sunbeam then immediately takes it back
She gives a flower then covers it with frost the next morning
She is the month of uncertainty and fragility
The month of loves that are beginning and still have doubts
At the end of her reign she looks at her work
The world is no longer a battlefield
It is a large, delicate watercolor, still a little damp
The trees are in bloom, the air is soft
She has set the stage with infinite art
Now the great oil painter of summer can arrive
She leaves him a world full of promises
And departs, leaving behind
The smell of rain and of pollen.

---

## 013. May, the Generous Queen

April left a barely dry canvas
Full of shy colors and damp promises
May then arrives like a queen in her kingdom
She does not ask for permission
She takes the world and covers it in gold and perfumes
With a wave of her hand she orders the trees
To put on their most beautiful green dresses
She hangs clusters of purple lilac on the garden gates
And garlands of wisteria on the old stone walls
She unrolls the thick carpet of the lawns
And on it she throws thousands of daisies
Like a handful of little stars
She is the great perfumer of the world
She opens all her bottles at the same time
The heady scent of roses
The innocent smell of lily of the valley
The peppery fragrance of peonies
The air becomes a sweet and intoxicating wine
That makes one dizzy and want to love
Under her reign the days are endless
The sun is her faithful lover
He stays longer in the evening to admire her
People shed their coats and their worries
They lie in the grass
They kiss in the parks without any shame
It is a dangerous month for bosses and governments
For it gives people the dangerous taste of freedom
And the desire to live rather than to work
May is a perfect and slightly sad beauty
For she knows that perfection does not last
She is the summit of the hill of the year
The moment of perfect balance before the long descent of summer
She is the youth of the world in all its glory
When her month ends she does not die
She simply dozes off
Exhausted by her own generosity
She leaves behind a world heavy with sap and pollen
A world full of fruits to be born
And the memory of a beauty so intense
That it makes the heart ache a little.

---

## 014. November, the Stagehand

October has played its great tragic scene
Full of golden leaves and dying light
The curtain has fallen
Then November enters the empty stage
He is not an actor he does not like the show
He is a stagehand a man of the shadows
Dressed in overalls of gray rain
His first job is to sweep up the confetti
The last red and yellow leaves
He makes great damp piles of them that smell of the end
He does not see their beauty he just sees the mess
He takes down the great spotlight of the sun
And replaces it with a small, weak white bulb
That gives an honest and heatless light
Then he sets up the scenery for winter
He pulls the great curtains of fog
He brings down a fine and persistent drizzle
To wash the makeup from the earth
He strips the trees down to the bone
Revealing their true nature
Their black and magnificent skeleton
Drawn against the white sky
The city under his hand is a closed theater
The streets are empty service corridors
People walk quickly with their collars turned up
They are extras in a play with no plot
The wind whistles a sad little tune
In the electric wires and the bare branches
It is a month for seeing the bones of the world
The framework of the houses the structure of lives
But November is not mean
He is just efficient
He prepares for the silence
He prepares for the great white sleep
He makes a void so that the new can arrive
One day
He offers the beauty of the naked truth
A difficult beauty that does not lie
When his work is done
The stage is clean, empty, and ready
He sits in a dark corner in the wings
He rolls a cigarette of fog
And he waits in the silence
For the entrance of the final great actor
The old man in the coat of snow
December.

---

## 015. December, the Old King

November the stagehand has cleaned everything away
The stage is empty and cold
Then December enters
The old king of the dying year
He wears a great coat of snow
And his beard is made of frost
He sits on the throne of silence
And begins his white reign
His first law is the cold
A pure and honest cold that bites the cheeks
And that silences the last complaints of autumn
His second law is the snow
A great white curtain that falls gently
To hide the scars of the world
And to transform the city into a sleeping dream
He hangs chandeliers of ice on the bare branches
And he makes the night sky blacker
So that the real stars shine brighter
But his kingdom is divided
For men are afraid of his great silence
So they light their own small, nervous stars
Their yellow and blue electric garlands
They bring dead trees into their houses
And cover them with glass balls and fake silver
They sing of peace and love
While shoving each other in crowded stores
To buy gifts that will be forgotten in January
The old king watches all this commotion
With his clear and cold eyes
He looks at the fake snow in the shop windows
And he smiles a little from his great coat of real snow
He listens to the recorded Christmas carols
And he prefers the simple music of the wind
But sometimes in the midst of all this noise
He finds things that he likes
The warmth of a real fire in a fireplace
The face of a child watching the snow fall
For the very first time
The taste of oranges and cinnamon
The sharing of a simple meal among friends
These are the small pockets of truth
In the great lie of the holiday
Then comes the longest night of the year
His true king's night
The small lights of men go out one by one
The noise of the party falls silent
And all that remains is his great white and silent kingdom
The city asleep under its blanket of peace
The year is finished
The old king has put everything to sleep
And he watches over the world's slumber
Waiting for the distant return
Of the first green cry of spring.

---

## 016. The Sun, the Lonely King

At the center of everything there is me
The old solitary king dressed in fire
I have no throne no crown
Just my own burning heart
Which serves as my palace and my prison
Every morning I rise with great effort
I push away the heavy blanket of the night
And I begin my eternal work
I am the great alarm clock of the world
I shout in the ear of the roosters
And I paint a fine line of gold under the door of the sleepers
I am the great painter without a brush
Without me the flower is a gray shape
The ocean a large black spot
The face of the beloved a simple absence
I give the red to the cherry
The green to the meadow
And the changing blue of the eyes that look at the sky
I am the patient baker
Who makes the wheat dough rise in the fields
I am the great clothes dryer of the world
Who dries the shirts of workers and the dresses of lovers
I am the companion of the old on public benches
And the warm rug for the cat at the window
I am the excuse for a lunch on the terrace
But I am terribly alone
I see the children playing in the parks
The couples holding hands
The friends sharing a bottle of wine
I warm everyone
But no one can approach me
I give all my warmth
And I remain in my great silence of fire
Sometimes my work exhausts me
My heat becomes an anger
I beat down on the city like a hammer
I melt the asphalt and crack the earth
People complain and hide from me
They forget that without me they would be nothing
In the evening I am tired
I descend slowly behind the horizon
Like a worker going home after a long day
I leave the place to my sister the moon
Pale and discreet
Who comes to console the world from my great light
And to watch over the sleep of my small distant kingdom.

---

## 017. August, the Old Storyteller

July the blacksmith has put away his hammers
Leaving behind a tired and thirsty world
Then August arrives, walking slowly
He is an old storyteller with a beard the color of ripe wheat
And eyes squinted by a thousand suns
He sits in the shade of the great oak of the year
And begins his long story
He does not tell new things
He tells the story of the summer that is already almost past
He remembers the mad rush of March
The shyness of April and the beauty of May the queen
He speaks of the long lazy afternoon of June
And the great fits of anger of July
His voice is the song of the crickets in the evening
A slow, repetitive, and nostalgic song
While he speaks he dips his brush in liquid gold
And gilds the edges of the tree leaves
He ripens the plums and the figs
He gorges them with sugar and heat
His breath is heavy and smells of dust
Of dry grass and overripe fruit
He stretches the days to make them last longer
Especially the evenings
Those long golden twilights where time seems to stand still
The city under his spell is a sleeping beauty
The heat is no longer an enemy but an old blanket
The last vacationers return with brown skin
And the salt of the sea still in their hair
There is a sweetness and a sadness in the air
The perfect beauty of things that are about to end
One knows that summer is still here
But one can already feel on one's back
The small cool breath of September
When his story is finished
The old storyteller falls silent
He has said everything, told everything
He has bottled the summer like a precious wine
He looks one last time at his golden and motionless world
Then he rises heavily
And lets a first yellow leaf fall
Like the final period of his tale.

---

## 018. September, the Melancholy Librarian

The old storyteller August has finished his tale
And September the librarian arrives
He makes no noise he walks on tiptoe
He smells of old paper and the dust of books
His mission is to close the great library of summer
And to arrange the memories on the shelves of time
First he closes the great book of the sun
With pages still warm and a little dog-eared
Between two chapters he slips a last dried flower
A poppy as red as a kiss
He collects the last sounds of summer
The song of the crickets the laughter of children in the water
And he locks them away in little silent music boxes
Then he changes the light of the world
He replaces the heavy gold of summer
With a clearer, cooler, more honest light
A light that hides nothing
Neither the first yellow leaf on the tree
Nor the first sadness in people's hearts
He takes the woolen sweaters out of the mothballs
And the smell of back-to-school
For September is the great month of returns
Children return to school with new shoes
And schoolbags full of empty notebooks and promises
Parents return to the office with skin still salty
And heads full of the sound of waves
The city streets fill up again
The slow rhythm of summer is broken
But September is not a sad month
It is a month of solemn and pensive beauty
The beauty of spiderwebs covered in dew
The beauty of red apples and ripe grapes
The beauty of the first smoke rising from a chimney
It is a month for walking in the woods
And for smelling the scent of leaves beginning to die
When his work is done
The great book of summer is closed and put away on its shelf
The librarian looks out the window
The world is calm, clean, and well-ordered
Ready for the long reading of autumn
Then he takes a new book, a blank one
And on the first page he writes in a beautiful round hand
"October".

---

## 019. February, the Clumsy Lover

After the long white silence of January
February arrives, stumbling a little
He is the shortest of the months, the shyest
He wears a winter coat that is too big for him
And thin shoes that let in water
He would very much like to bring about the spring
But he doesn't quite know how to go about it
He tries to melt the snow
But he only succeeds in making gray puddles
He blows gently on the branches of the trees
To awaken the sleeping buds
"Go on, it's almost time," he whispers to them
But the buds hesitate, they are still a little afraid of the cold
He tries to paint the sky blue
But his paint is pale and watery
A blue of promise, a blue of maybe
His big day is Valentine's Day
The great fair of love in a box
He looks at the florists' windows
With perfect red roses that smell of nothing
He looks at the heart-shaped boxes of chocolates
Made by machines that have never fallen in love
All that business doesn't interest him
He prefers the real things
The love that doesn't need a date on the calendar
The little snowdrop picked in the woods
The poorly written poem full of real words
The warmth of a hand in a coat pocket
The shared silence over a cup of tea
The stolen kisses that taste of rain
It is that kind of love he tries to protect
The simple love of simple people
Then his time is already over
He is so short, poor February
He doesn't have time to finish his work
He leaves as he came
Discreetly, leaving the door ajar
He has not brought the spring
No, not really
But he has left behind
An earth that is damp and ready
A light that is just a tiny bit longer
And in the air, that strange and vibrant feeling
That something is about to begin.

---

## 020. Black, the Color of Secrets

They say I am the color of mourning
The end of everything, the great hole
The twin brother of nothingness
They say I absorb the light
As if it were a crime
But they forget
That I am the rich and deep earth
Where the seed begins its journey to the sun
I am the immense and velvety night sky
Without me one would never see the moon or the stars
I am the pupil of the eye
The little round door through which all beauty enters
I am the ink that gives a body to poems
The musical note on the white page
The respectful silence in the movie theater
Before the dream begins
I am the coal that carries the fire
And the coffee that awakens sad mornings
I am the color of independent cats
Of anarchists and of poets
The color of simple elegance
And of well-kept secrets
I am the refuge of the shadow
Where lovers can hide to love each other
Of course, I am also the color of fear
Of the storm cloud full of anger
Of the locked door
And of the moonless night
But I am not the void
I am the mystery of that which is full
I am not the end of everything
I am just the other side of the light
White is the question
I am the depth of the answer
We are the two sides of the same coin
The day and the night
The poem and the page
And one without the other
Would tell no story at all.

---

## 021. June, the Lazy King

After May the queen has exhausted all her perfumes
June arrives yawning
He is not a decorator nor a revolutionary
He is a lazy king who inherits a perfect kingdom
He sees that the work is already done
The trees are green the flowers are open
So he lies down in the tall grass
And begins his long reign of heat and light
His first royal decree is for the sun
He orders it to climb very high and to stop moving
He dismisses the wind
Except for a little breeze to make the leaves shiver
He slows down time
Especially in the afternoon
So that naps in the shade can last an eternity
Under his golden reign
The colors become deeper
The green of the leaves darkens and gorges on sap
The blue of the sky becomes intense and cloudless
He paints the cheeks of the first cherries
A shiny and sweet red
He sprinkles the fields with the red dots of poppies
And the blue dots of cornflowers
The city also slows its mad race
The asphalt is soft and hot
People seek the shade of the plane trees
They drink cool drinks on café terraces
The only sound is the buzzing of insects
And the distant laughter of children playing with water
The nights are short and warm
Nights of blue velvet where one can sleep with the window open
Listening to the scent of jasmine
June is the guardian of the longest day of the year
The summit of the great mountain of light
A suspended moment where everything is accomplished
Where everything is ripe and heavy with kept promises
There is nothing to do but to be
To breathe the warm air to feel the sun on one's skin
When his month ends
The lazy king does not worry
He knows that the light will now begin to decline
But that is July's job
He has done his part
He has offered the world a moment of motionless perfection
A long golden afternoon
And the memory of a summer that was just beginning.

---

## 022. The Sidewalk

A crushed cigarette butt
A puddle dreaming of the sky
A losing lottery ticket
The hurried step of a man running from his own life
The laughter of a woman who has already forgotten everything
A piece of chewing gum stuck like a stubborn promise
And the shadow of the plane trees
Drawing on the ground
A map to a country that does not exist.

---

## 023. The Businessman

He adds up numbers
He subtracts lives
He multiplies his worries
He divides the world in two
Those who have
And those who have nothing
And at the end of the day
He looks at his reflection in the office window
And nothing is left
Just a tired zero.

---

## 024. The Child's Question

Papa why is the sky sad
It is not sad my child it is blue
But blue is the color of bruises on the soul
Papa why do the birds sing
To forget they are in a cage my child
Even when the cage is as big as the world
Papa why do you work
To feed you my child
And the child looks at his bread
And he is no longer hungry.

---

## 025. The Wall

There was a wall between us
A wall of silence
A wall of averted eyes
A wall of words we never said
One day I drew a door on the wall
With the chalk of hope
You drew a window
With the tears from your eyes
But neither you nor I
Ever dared to open them.

---

## 026. The Minister of Fear

He sells locks for hearts
Barbed wire for dreams
Dark glasses not to see the sun
He talks of security
With the voice of a prison gate
He promises order
And he sows distrust
And people buy
And double-lock themselves in
In their own solitude.

---

## 027. The Dance of the Rain

The rain dances on the zinc of the roofs
A sad and monotonous tap dance
It washes the faces of statues
Who have never cried
It tells stories of clouds
To windows that do not listen
And in my heart
It plays the same song
The one of a love that has rusted.

---

## 028. The Collector

He collects dead butterflies
Stamps of vanished countries
Frozen smiles on photographs
He pins memories with care
He puts time under glass
He has everything
Except the beating of wings
Except the journey
Except the living laughter.

---

## 029. Your Absence

Your absence is an empty chair at the table
A silence in the middle of the sentence
The taste of coffee suddenly too bitter
It is a color missing from the rainbow
A cold draft in a heated room
Your absence is not nothing
It is everywhere.

---

## 030. The Sunday Painter

He paints the sky bluer than it is
He paints the sun yellower than it is
He paints your smile wider than it is
People say
That is not reality
He answers
No
It is better.

---

## 031. The Old Clock

The old clock in the living room
Eats the seconds one by one
With a sound of dry teeth
It does not tell the time
It takes it
And nobody notices
Sauf the sleeping cat
Who dreams of a mouse
Named eternity.

---

## 032. The Station Platform

A suitcase heavy with memories
A train whistling its impatience
A man searching for a face
A woman who found the wrong one
A glove fallen on the tracks
A farewell that was never said
The cigarette smoke
The taste of cold coffee
And life passing by
Without buying a ticket.

---

## 033. The Soldier's Garden

I remember the roses
Red like your mouth
In the garden behind the house
I remember the soft soil under my fingers
Now the ground is hard
And I dig another kind of hole
The sky is full of scrap metal
And the only red I see
Is not the red of flowers.

---

## 034. The Parish Priest's Accounts

Monday he counts the sins
Tuesday he counts the faithful
Wednesday he counts the pennies in the basket
One Our Father for ten francs
One absolution for fifty
A miracle made to order God is a good customer
But he never pays in cash
And at the end of the week
The
Heaven is always overdrawn.

---

## 035. Your Hair

Your hair is not hair
It is a forest at night
It is a river of ink
It is a nest for my tired hands
When I get lost in it
I do not look for my way
I only look to stay there
Forever.

---

## 036. The Monday Meeting

The boss speaks
The words fall from his mouth
Like dead flies
The stapler yawns
The paperclips form a chain
And escape through the window
The boss's tie
Strangles him a bit more with each sentence
And on the wall
The clock melts gently
It is time to be nowhere.

---

## 037. Things Found

A worn subway ticket
An orphan coat button
A sad smile in the crowd
A forgotten promise on a bench
The key to a door that no longer exists
Your name written on a foggy window pane
And my heart
Which I thought was lost.

---

## 038. Those Who Are Hungry

Those who are hungry look at shop windows
Those who have eaten too much look at their watches
Those who are hungry walk slowly
To save up their dreams
Those who have eaten too much walk fast
To digest their regrets
And at night
Some dream of bread
Others are afraid to dream.

---

## 039. Boulevard de Clichy at Night

The neons lie in color
Faces are masks of rain
The music from a bar bleeds onto the sidewalk
The smell of wet asphalt
A couple kissing under a broken streetlamp
And the moon
Which looks like a worthless coin
Tossed in the air
And which never came back down.

---

## 040. Little Truths

A door is a wall pretending to be polite
A cage is a house with too many windows
A clock is a machine for manufacturing the past
And silence
Is a noise that reflects.

---

## 041. The Man at the Window

He looks at the pigeon on the ledge
The pigeon is free
It can shit on the whole world
He is in the office
A cage of glass and concrete
His tie is a leash
He opens the window to breathe
But he has no wings
Just a desire to fall upwards.

---

## 042. The Waiting Room of Shared Solitude

The chairs are bored lined up in a row
The clock nibbles away the minutes with a sharp click
Nobody speaks
People look at their phones
Those little glowing prisons
Where they serve a sentence of shared solitude
A child on his mother's lap
Takes a pencil and on an old magazine
He draws a bird
A very simple bird with large wings
And for a second just a tiny second
The bird flies off the paper
Taking everyone's boredom away with it
Then a voice calls a name
And the room becomes a waiting room again.

---

## 043. The Important Man's Speech

Ladies
Gentlemen and others
We must consolidate the foundations of the future
We must build bridges across deserts
So that our certainties can cross over
We must modernize tradition
And traditionalize modernity
His tie is a well-fed silk snake
His voice comes out of the microphone like learned dust
He speaks of growth and sacrifice
Especially the sacrifice of others
He speaks of trust and effort
Especially the effort of others
And when he has finished saying nothing
Everyone applauds the silence
Relieved that the noise is finally over.

---

## 044. Rain Street

It rains on the street of
Memories
The rain washes the colors of the signs
It makes the traffic lights cry
A man walks in the rain without an umbrella
He does not feel the cold he feels the absence
Years ago under this same rain
There was a woman's laughter
A small portable sun that warmed the street
Now there is only water
Water falling on the asphalt
The asphalt which is the sad mirror of the sky
The man buys a newspaper
Not to read it
But to have something dry in his hands.

---

## 045. The Banker's Prayer

Our
Capital who art in
Stock Markets
May your
Name be made profitable
May your
Kingdom come through shares
May your
Will be done on
Earth as it is in
Heaven
Give us this day our daily interest
Forgive us our profits
As we also forgive those who owe us money
And lead us not into bankruptcy
But deliver us from fiscal evil
For yours is the kingdom the power and the glory
Forever and ever
Amen
And the word
Amen rings like the cash register
Closing shut.

---

## 046. The Dream of the Class Dunce

The teacher speaks with words of chalk
The numbers on the blackboard are prison bars
The child is bored so he closes his eyes
And the blackboard becomes a deep forest at night
The master's voice is a distant wolf calling
The white chalk is a bird of light
Showing him a secret path through the trees of arithmetic
The path leads to a river of ink
Where swim fish made of commas
The child dives into the river
And he swims towards a land
Where additions do not exist
And where two and two make a flower or a boat
As you wish.

---

## 047. The Heart on the Counter

When you left
You forgot a few little things
A blue scarf on a chair
A book with a dog-eared page
The silence your laughter used to make
And your heart
You left it on the kitchen counter
Next to the sugar bowl
It still beats a little
Just a tiny bit
It makes a sound like a tired watch
I don't know what to do with it
I watched it all day long
Maybe I will give it to the cat
Or throw it away with the faded flowers.

---

## 048. The Soldier's Inventory

In my left pocket
A photo folded in four
A letter I will never open
And half a cigarette
In my right pocket
Three cartridges
And a medal of the
Virgin
Which brought no luck to its former owner
In my head
The order to march
The order to shoot
The order to forget my name
Before I was a man with a trade
Now I am a uniform with a rifle
And the mud
I have mud everywhere
Even in my dreams.

---

## 049. Freedom in a Cage

They caught a wild bird
A bird that sang of freedom
They put it in a solid gold cage
With luxury seeds and spring water
And they told it
Sing for us bird sing the beauty of the world
Sing for us the joy of being free
The bird looked at the bars of its cage
It looked at the satisfied faces of its jailers
It opened its beak
And it laid an egg
A very simple very white egg
Then it fell silent forever
And the men never understood
That freedom is not to be sung
It must be hatched.

---

## 050. The Lovers of the Metro

In the subway car
The people are hurried ghosts
Their faces are masks of fatigue
The train screams its metal pain on the tracks
Nobody looks at anyone
Everyone is a deserted island
But in a corner two lovers
They do not touch they do not speak
They only look at each other
And in their eyes the car becomes a garden
The sound of the train becomes sweet music
The tired faces become strange flowers
For three minutes between
Châtelet and
Gare de
Lyon
They repair the world
Then the doors open
And the gray swallows everything.

---

## 051. The God of Forgotten Things

There is a god of war and a god of money
A god of kings and one of official prayers
But there is another god smaller more discreet
The god of single socks
Of keys that no longer open anything
Of nameless telephone numbers
Of whispered promises carried away by the wind
He lives in the dust under the furniture
He asks for neither temple nor sacrifice
He picks up the small pieces of broken life
Lost smiles missed farewells
He performs no miracles
He only remembers
And that is already a lot.

---

## 052. The Weight of a Key

This key in my pocket
It is small and cold
But it weighs heavier than the house
Heavier than the street
Heavier than the city
Because it is the key to a door
Behind which you are no longer there
It no longer opens a house
It opens an absence
And absence
Weighs heavier than the entire world.

---

## 053. The Stock Exchange of Hearts

Today love is on the rise
Trust is stable
Anger has plummeted
Joy makes a spectacular breakthrough
Sadness stagnates
The analysts understand nothing
They talk of an irrational market
But the heart is not a stock
It obeys no law
Except its own
And today my heart has decided
That the value of your smile
Was worth more than all the gold in the world.

---

## 054. The Dialogue of Hammer and Glass

The hammer said to the glass
I am strength I am power
I can break you into a thousand pieces
The glass answered nothing
It just let the light pass through
It reflected the sky
The face of the child who was watching
The hammer struck
The glass shattered into a million shards of light
Each shard continued to reflect the sky
The hammer remained there
Heavy and stupid in its victory
Having succeeded in breaking
Only beauty
Without ever understanding it.

---

## 055. The Revolt of the Billboards

The billboard for the car said
Buy freedom but stay in traffic jams
The billboard for the perfume said
Be irresistible but stay alone in front of your mirror
The billboard for the bank said
Realize your dreams by going into debt for thirty years
One day they got tired of lying
They tore down their own posters
And they showed the gray wall behind
The bare and honest wall
And they wrote on it with broken neon letters
LIFE
IS
ELSE
WHERE
And people looked up from their phones
And for the first time
They saw the sky.

---

## 056. The Weight of a Regret

A regret is not heavy
It is like a grain of sand in the shoe
At first you do not feel it
Then it starts to irritate
Then it hurts
Then you can no longer walk
You sit on the roadside
You take off your shoe
You shake out the grain of sand
But it does not fall out
It is stuck to the skin
It has become a part of us
And you must learn to walk with it
Limping a little
For the rest of your life.

---

## 057. The Streetlamp's Prayer

My God who art electricity
May your light shine
May your kingdom of kilowatts come
May your will be done in my street as in the others
Give me this day my ration of night
Forgive me my shadow zones
As I forgive the dogs who mistake me for a tree
And lead me not into the temptation of a blackout
But deliver us from vandals
For to me belong the lovers the lonely and the stray cats
For the nights of nights
Amen
And may the moths
Serve as my choir.

---

## 058. The Taste of Rain

Rain does not taste the same everywhere
In the countryside it tastes of earth and grass
In the city it tastes of asphalt and sadness
By the sea it tastes of salt and departures
On your face it tastes of your tears
Or of your smile
When you raise your head to drink it
Rain has no taste of its own
It takes the taste of whatever it touches
Like love
Which takes the taste
Of the heart into which it falls.

---

## 059. The Revolt of the Common Nouns

The table said
I no longer want to be a table I want to be a mountain
The chair said
I no longer want to be a chair I want to be a bird
The window said
I no longer want to be a window I want to be the horizon
So they exchanged their names
The table became a mountain where cats went mountaineering
The chair became a bird that flew away over the mountain
The window became the horizon where the bird vanished
The man in the room was left alone
With words that no longer meant anything
And he understood that poetry
Is when you give a chair
Permission
To fly away.

---

## 060. The Collector of Silences

He collects silences
The awkward silence after a bad joke
The heavy silence before the storm
The respectful silence in a cemetery
The complicit silence between two thieves
The blank silence of a page to be written
His collection is invisible and priceless
He keeps it in his heart
In the evening he sits in his armchair
And he listens to his silences
Each one tells a story
Deeper than all words
He knows that noise is easy
But that silence
Is an art
And that the most beautiful silence
Is the one that is full
Of everything we do not need to say.

---

## 061. The Last Traveler

The last bus of the night
Smells of fatigue and solitude
The seats are cold
The windows weep with condensation
There is only one traveler
An old man with a hat
He does not look outside
He looks inside
He looks at the landscape of his life
The mountains of his joys
The rivers of his sorrows
The bus stops at the terminal
Which is called "End of the
Line"
The old man does not get off
He remains seated
He continues his journey
On the only line
That has no terminal
The line of memory.

---

## 062. The Memory of a Wall

I am a wall
I have seen things
I have felt the backs of lovers hiding
I have heard whispered secrets
I have borne the weight of war posters
And of children's drawings
Words of love and hate were written on me
I was covered in paint
I was cracked
But I am still here
I am the skin of the city
The memory of the street
And if you press your ear against me
You might hear
The sound of all the hearts
That beat one day
On my side of life.

---

## 063. The Weight of Words

The word "war" weighs a ton of tears
The word "bread" weighs the weight of hope
The word "always" weighs the weight of a lie
The word "never" weighs the weight of a regret
The word "you" weighs the weight of my entire world
We must be careful with the words we use
They are not toys
They are stones or feathers
Bridges or walls
Poisons or remedies
Before speaking
We should weigh each word
On the fragile little scale
Of our own heart.

---

## 064. The Revolt of the Garden Statues

The garden gnome said
I am tired of smiling stupidly with my wheelbarrow
The
Plaster Venus said
I am cold and I would like a coat
The plastic pink flamingo said
I would like to see the sea for real
So they left the garden
The gnome went to work in a mine
The
Venus registered with a modeling agency
The pink flamingo took a plane ticket to the tropics
The owner of the garden was very angry
He said
Where is the respect for property
He replaced the statues
With surveillance cameras
And his garden became very secure
And very sad.

---

## 065. The Merchant of Gray Skies

Everyone sells blue skies
He sells gray skies
Skies of fine drizzle
Skies of fog
Skies of melancholy
People look at him strangely
Why sell sadness
He answers
The gray sky is not sad
It is restful
It is gentle on the eyes
It makes you want to stay at home
To read a book to drink a tea
To do nothing
The gray sky is the right to idleness
The right to melancholy
And in a world that asks you to be always happy
Maybe that is
The real revolution.

---

## 066. The Road's Prayer

My God who art tar
May my asphalt be smooth
May thy kingdom of cars come
May thy will be done on the right as it is on the left
Give us today our daily white line
Forgive us our potholes
As we forgive the tires that crush us
And lead us not into the temptation of traffic jams
But deliver us from roadworks
For to us belong the speed the distance and the horizon
For miles and miles
Amen
And may the road signs
Serve as my saints.

---

## 067. The Ministry of Complicated Things

Their job is to complicate simple things
They turn a declaration of love
Into a three-page form
They turn a handshake
Into a contract with fine-print clauses
They turn a good idea
Into a six-hour meeting
They speak a language that no one understands
With words like "synergy" "paradigmatic" "implementation"
They create problems
In order to sell the solutions
They are the high priests of confusion
And they are afraid of only one thing
A child who asks
Why.

---

## 068. The Dance of the Dead Leaves

Dead leaves are not dead
They only pretend
When the wind rises
They begin to dance
A last waltz before the long sleep
They dance to remember the summer
Of the sun on their green skin
Of the singing of birds in their branches
They dance to forget the coming winter
The cold the snow the silence
They are not sad
They are wise
They know that falling
Is not dying
It is just another way of traveling
And of preparing
Next spring.

---

## 069. The Thief of Sleep

He does not steal money
He steals sleep
At night he enters bedrooms
Without making a sound
And he takes people's sleep
He puts it in a large bag
In the morning people wake up tired
With dark circles under their eyes
They do not know their rest was stolen from them
The thief goes home
And he gives the stolen sleep
To his sick little girl
Who cannot sleep because of the pain
She finally falls asleep
With a smile
And the thief watches her
With a heavy heart
To be at once
A criminal and a savior.

---

## 070. The Song of the Crack

I am a crack in a wall
A tiny line of nothing at all
People look at me with contempt
They say
That must be repaired
But they do not know
That I am a window
A small window to the other side
I let in a little light
A little wind
A little life
I am proof that nothing is perfect
That nothing is eternal
I am the small necessary wound
That prevents the wall
From being a prison.

---

## 071. The Last Train

The last train of the night
Is full of people who do not want to go home
The student who is afraid of his exams
The worker who is afraid of his wife
The lover who is afraid of solitude
The train rolls slowly
As if it were hesitating
The lights of the houses are judging eyes
The train is an ark of
Noah's ark of sadness
Everyone is alone with their sorrow
The train arrives at the terminus
Everyone gets off
And returns to their life
As one returns to prison
After a short furlough
Waiting for
The last train of tomorrow.

---

## 072. The Museum of Simple Things

In this museum there are no master paintings
There is a chipped cup
A lost button
A dried flower
A smooth pebble
Each object is in a display case
With a small label "Cup in which my father drank his coffee" "Button from the dress of my first love" "Flower he gave me before going to war"
It is the museum of the heart's memory
The museum of invisible treasures
The visitors do not speak
They look
And they remember
Their own cups their own buttons
Their own flowers
And they understand that the true value
Is not in gold
But in the love that settled
For a moment
On the simplest things.

---

## 073. The Revolt of the Punctuation Marks

The period said
I am sick of ending everything
The comma said
I am tired of making pauses
The exclamation mark said
Enough shouting
The question mark said
I no longer want to doubt
So they all resigned
Phrases became endless rivers
Without breath without emotion without question
A long river of gray words
People no longer read
It was too boring
They understood that punctuation
Is not a decoration
It is the breathing of the text
It is its music its heart
And they asked for forgiveness
From the small modest signs
That give to language
All of its life.

---

## 074. The Merchant of Regrets

He does not sell new things
He sells second-hand regrets
The regret of not having said "I love you"
The regret of having taken this job
The regret of not having left
His regrets are well preserved
They still smell of the past
People buy them
To feel less alone with their own
They compare their regrets
Like collectors "My regret is bigger than yours" "Mine is older"
It is a strange trade
A trade of shared pain
The merchant is a wise man
He knows that the only way to get rid of a regret
Is not to forget it
But to transform it
Into a lesson
For the future.

---

## 075. The Sidewalk's Prayer

My God who art concrete
May my name be trampled underfoot
May thy kingdom of passersby come
May their will be done on me as it is elsewhere
Give us today our daily rain
Forgive us our cracks
As we forgive the chewing gum squashed upon me
And lead us not into the temptation of roadworks
But deliver us from dog poop
For to us belong the hurried the flaneurs and the lovers
For streets and streets
Amen
And may the cigarette butts
Serve as my incense.

---

## 076. The Ministry of Normality

Their goal is for everyone to be the same
Same house same car same dog
Same ideas same dreams same fears
They distribute manuals on "how to live"
They organize conformity championships
They punish originality
Their slogan is "Difference is a disease"
They have almost succeeded
The world has become a large mirror
Where everyone sees their own reflection everywhere
But one day a child laughed
A laugh that was not in the manual
A free and wild laugh
And this laugh made a crack in the large mirror
And through the crack
One began to see
A little blue sky.

---

## 077. The Dance of the Shadow Puppets

On the bedroom wall
The father's hands make shadow puppets
A dog a bird a rabbit
The child laughs
He does not see his father's tired hands
He does not see the worries of the day
He sees a magic world
A world where everything is possible
Where animals are born from light and shadow
The father continues his dance
For his child
He becomes a magician a poet
He forgets that he is just a man
With hands worn out by work
And a heavy heart
And for a moment
The father and the child
Are together in the same dream
The most beautiful theater in the world.

---

## 078. The Thief of Voices

He steals people's voices
He captures them in a tape recorder
He has the cracked voice of the old man
The clear voice of the young girl
The loud voice of the orator
The sweet voice of the mother
In the evening he listens to his collection
He gets drunk on all these lives
He is a thousand people at once
But he is no one
Because he lost his own voice
A long time ago
By trying to shout too much
Against the injustice of the world
Now he is mute
And he has only the voices of others left
To remember
That he once existed.

---

## 079. The Song of the Dust

I am the dust
I am the end of all things
The end of empires of loves of lives
I settle gently on the furniture
On the books on the memories
I am the gray patience of time
I am the proof that everything passes
That everything ends
People chase me away with feather dusters
But I always return
For I am the very matter of the world
I am made of dead skin and shattered stars
I am the beginning and the end
And I dance
In a ray of sunlight
To remind you
Of your own fragile beauty.

---

## 080. The Last Kiosk

It is the last kiosk before the desert
It does not sell newspapers
It sells water
Shadow
A story to listen to
A little silence
The travelers stop
They drink the water
They rest in the shadow
They listen to the story
Then they set off again
Toward the great void
The merchant asks them for nothing
He knows that in the desert
A glass of water
Is worth more than all the gold in the banks
And that a good story
Can save your life.

---

## 081. The Testament of a Cloud

When I am dead
When I have fallen as rain
I leave you no will
I leave you the puddle on the sidewalk
In which the sky is reflected
I leave you the flower that will drink my water
And will be a little more beautiful tomorrow
I leave you the damp earth
Which will smell sweet of life
I leave you nothing grand
Just the proof that even in disappearing
One can still give a little freshness
And a little beauty
To the tired world.

---

## 082. The Dialogue of the Hand and the Glove

The hand said to the glove
You protect me from the cold
But you prevent me from feeling
The glove replied
I protect you from dirt
But I prevent you from touching
La hand said
Without you I am vulnerable
The glove replied
Without you I am only an empty piece of leather
So they decided never to part again
The hand agreed to be a little less sensitive
The glove agreed to be a little less clean
And together they faced the world
One giving warmth
The other giving life
A perfect love story
Made of compromises
And of necessity.

---

## 083. The Revolt of the Musical Notes

The
C said
I am sick of always being the start
The
B said
I am tired of always being the end
The
Sharp said
I want to be natural for once
The
Flat said
I would like to go up a bit
So they left the score
They flew about the room
They created a new music
A music without rules without a conductor
A free and wild music
The pianist tried to catch them
But his fingers were too slow too disciplined
He had to stop and listen
And he understood that the most beautiful music
Is not the one that is written
But the one that invents itself
At every moment.

---

## 084. The Merchant of "I Love You"

He sells "I love yous"
Brand new "I love yous"
Second-hand "I love yous"
Whispered "I love yous"
Shouted "I love yous"
He has an "I love you" for every situation
The first shy "I love you"
The last desperate "I love you"
People come to buy them from him
When they no longer have the strength to say it themselves
Or when they no longer mean it
It is a sad trade
A trade of empty words
But sometimes
Someone buys an "I love you"
And says it to someone else
And the other believes it
And the empty word suddenly fills
With all the magic of the world
And the merchant smiles
Because he knows he has sold
A miracle.

---

## 085. The Cigarette's Prayer

My God who art fire
May my name be smoked
May thy kingdom of nicotine come
May thy will be done in my lungs as it is elsewhere
Give us today our daily puff
Forgive us our smoke
As I forgive those who light me up
And lead us not into the temptation of the ashtray
But deliver us from the final drag
For to me belong the pleasure the cancer and the cough
For packs and packs
Amen
And may my butt
Join its brothers on the sidewalk.

---

## 086. The Ministry of Fear

Their job is to manufacture fear
Fear of the other
Fear of tomorrow
Fear of oneself
They spread it on television
In the newspapers
In speeches
Fear is a good product
It sells well
It makes people docile
It makes them accept anything
Surveillance walls wars
The minister of
Fear is a man who is afraid of everything
He is afraid of his shadow
He is afraid of the silence
He is afraid of a laughing child
Because he knows that fear
Is a fortress of paper
And that a single burst of laughter
A single act of courage
A single word of love
Can make it collapse
Forever.

---

## 087. The Dance of the Snowflakes

Every snowflake is unique
A tiny star of ice
A poem of geometry
They fall in silence
They dance a slow and cold waltz
They cover the world
They hide the dirt the ugliness the mistakes
They make everything clean and new
They are a promise of purity
A great white forgiveness
Then the sun returns
And the flakes melt
They become ordinary water again
And the dirt reappears
But for a moment
The world was perfect
And this memory suffices
To give us hope
Of the next snow.

---

## 088. The Thief of Endings

He steals the ends of stories
Just before the "they lived happily"
Just before the "he died in peace"
Just before the "all's well that ends well"
He leaves stories hanging
With a taste of the unfinished
The characters wander aimlessly
The readers are frustrated
He does not do this out of malice
He does this because he hates endings
He thinks that an ending
Is a prison gate
He prefers stories that do not end
Loves that continue
Questions that remain open
He is the great defender
Of the "to be continued"
Which is the most beautiful promise
Of life.

---

## 089. The Song of the Chewing Gum

I was pink and full of sugar
In the mouth of a young girl
I knew the taste of her kisses
Then she threw me onto the sidewalk
I became gray and hard
Glued to the asphalt
Shoes crush me
Rain washes me
I am the sticky memory of a fleeting pleasure
I am the small scar of passing time
I am of no use anymore
Except perhaps to trip
A hurried man
So that he stops for a second
And looks up at the sky
And remembers
The sweet taste of his own youth.

---

## 090. The Prostitute's Last Client

He entered her room
He did not take off his coat
He sat on the bed
And he asked her
Tell me about yourself
She was surprised
No one ever asked her that
She spoke of her childhood
Of her dreams of her fears
He listened without saying a word
For an hour
Then he laid the money on the table
And he left
She looked at the money
Then she looked at herself in the mirror
And for the first time in a long time
She did not see a prostitute
She saw a woman
And she understood that this man
Had not bought her body
He had returned to her
Her soul.

---

## 091. The Testament of a Blade of Grass

I am a blade of grass
I am not much
But I have seen the sun
I have felt the rain
I have danced with the wind
One day a cow will eat me
Or a lawnmower will cut me
I will leave no name
I will leave no trace
Except perhaps
In the memory of an ant
That sheltered beneath me during a storm
I leave you my green color
The green of hope
And my capacity to always grow back
Even when I am stepped on
That is all
And that is already
A lot.

---

## 092. The Dialogue of the Heart and Reason

The
Reason said to the
Heart
You are reckless you are crazy
You get excited over nothing
You suffer for a single word
You are illogical
The
Heart replied
And you are cold you are calculating
You put everything into an equation
You fear everything that cannot be proven
You are a prison
The
Reason said
Without me you would be only a chaos of passions
The
Heart replied
Without me you would be only a lifeless machine
So they continued to argue
Like an old couple
Who cannot live together
But who knows very well
That they cannot live
One without the other.

---

## 093. The Revolt of the Fairy Tales

The
Little
Red
Riding Hood said
I am sick of the wolf I want a prince charming
Cinderella said
Enough of the prince I want to start a union for scullery maids
The
Sleeping
Beauty
Woke up all by herself
And she said
A hundred years of sleep is enough I want a coffee and a newspaper
The
Prince
Charming was unemployed
The wolf became a vegetarian
The wicked queen opened a beauty salon
Everything was topsy-turvy
The children understood nothing anymore
But they found it much more amusing
For the characters had finally decided
No longer to be prisoners
Of their own story.

---

## 094. The Merchant of "If Only"

He sells "if onlys"
In small glass bottles "If only I had dared" "If only I had known" "If only I had left"
It is the most powerful perfume in the world
The perfume of regret
People put a drop of it behind their ear
And they spend the day dreaming
Of the life they did not have
It is a sweet and terrible drug
Which prevents one from living in the present
The merchant is a rich man
Because "if only"
Is an inexhaustible resource
In the hearts of men.

---

## 095. The Book's Prayer

My God who art paper
May my title be read
May thy kingdom of readers come
May thy will be done in my pages as it is elsewhere
Give us today our daily reading
Forgive us our typos
As I forgive those who dog-ear my pages
And lead us not into the temptation of the library
But deliver us from oblivion
For to me belong story imagination and dreams
Forever and ever
Amen
And may my last page
Be the most beautiful.

---

## 096. The Ministry of the Obvious

Their job is to repeat the obvious
The sky is blue
Water is wet
Fire burns
They write very long reports
Give very boring lectures
To prove that two and two make four
They are paid very highly
To say nothing new
They are the guardians of the status quo
The enemies of surprise
Their greatest fear
Is that one day someone will stand up and say
What if the sky were green
What if water were dry
And what if two and two
Made a bird.

---

## 097. The Dance of the Strangers

In the subway at rush hour
Bodies touch brush press together
It is a strange and involuntary dance
A forced intimacy
One feels the warmth of others
Their smell their breathing
One is closer to them
Than to one's own family
But no one looks at each other
No one speaks to each other
Each is an island of solitude
In an ocean of closeness
Then the doors open
And the dance stops
Each returns to their own solitude
Carrying on their skin
The phantom memory
Of an unknown contact.

---

## 098. The Thief of Time

He does not steal watches
He steals time itself
He steals the minute when you hesitate
The second when you dream
The hour you spend doing nothing
He puts them in a large clock
That turns backward
He says he wants to catch up with his past
Repair his mistakes
But by stealing the present of others
He only creates an empty future
A future without surprise without the unforeseen
A future where everything has already happened
He is the greatest of criminals
For he does not steal what people have
He steals what they could
Have.

---

## 099. The Song of the Chipped Cup

I am a chipped cup
I am put at the back of the cupboard
No one wants to drink from me anymore
I have a small porcelain wound
A memory of a clumsy hand
Or of too sharp an anger
I am no longer perfect
But I am more interesting
My chip is a mouth
Which could tell stories
The story of coffee too hot
Of tears fallen inside
Of kisses exchanged above me
I am a cup with a past
And that is much better
Than a new cup
Which still has
Nothing to say.

---

## 100. The Last Piano in the Bar

The piano is old and out of tune
Its keys are yellow like a smoker's teeth
It remembers all the songs
Sad love songs
Joyful drinking songs
It remembers the hands that played it
The expert hands of the professional
The clumsy hands of the lover
The drunk hands of the late-night customer
The bar is going to close
It will be sold for wood
It will play one last song
For itself
A song without notes
Made of all the silences
Of all the laughter
Of all the tears
It accompanied for fifty years.

---

## 101. The Testament of a Star

I am an old star
My light takes millions of years to reach you
When you look at me
You are looking into the past
Perhaps I am already dead
But my light continues to travel
To tell you that I existed
I leave you no will
I leave you my light
A fragile little light in the dark night
A small point of hope
To guide you
To remind you that even after death
Something of us continues to shine
And to travel
Toward unknown eyes
That have need
Of a little light.

---

## 102. The Newsstand

The newsagent is a tired man
He sells the news of the world
Wars crises catastrophes
The glossy paper smiles of celebrities
He sells words printed in black on gray paper
He sees life pass before his kiosk
Loves that are born loves that die
People running after time
He does not run
He waits
He is the guardian of bad news
The motionless lighthouse in the city storm
In the evening he closes his shop of misfortunes
And he goes home
To read a book
Where there is mention of nothing
Only of a bird singing on a branch.

---

## 103. The Unemployed Man's Prayer

Our Employer who art nowhere
Cursed be thy name
May thy kingdom never come
May thy will be done neither in the office nor elsewhere
Give us today our absence of bread
Forgive us our anger
As we do not forgive those who laid us off
And lead us not into the temptation of hope
But deliver us from the job interview
For to you belong the contempt the pity and the indifference
Forever and ever
And after.

---

## 104. The Words You Don't Say

The words you do not say
Are the heaviest
They sit between us at the table
They sleep in the middle of our bed
They have the shape of your silence
The color of your evasive looks
I hear them shouting at night
When you think I am asleep
They are terrible words
Words that are afraid of themselves
Words that could break everything
Or save everything
I would like to take them in my hand
To caress them reassure them
And tell them
Do not be afraid
Come out
Even if you must kill me.

---

## 105. The Drawing on the Wall

The wall was gray and sad
A wall of prison or hospital
A wall that expected nothing
A child passed by
With a piece of white chalk
He drew a door
Then he drew a key
Then he drew a path
Starting from the door and climbing toward the sky
The wall said nothing
But it felt something stir within itself
A crack of light
A desire to open
The child left
But the door remained
And at night the prisoners and the sick
Dream that they take the key
And set off on the path
Leading to the land where walls
Do not exist.

---

## 106. The Dream of a Hammer

I am a hammer
My life is to strike
Striking nails
Striking walls
Striking heads
I am brute force violence
But at night I dream
I dream that I am a bird
A tiny light bird
I dream that my iron head becomes a head of feathers
And that my wooden handle becomes a wing
I fly above houses
I sing sweet songs
I strike no more
I caress the wind
Then the morning arrives
And I become a hammer again
Heavy and full of hatred
Waiting for the night
To be able to dream
That I am something other than a tool of misfortune.

---

## 107. Grammar Is a Prison

The subject must agree with the verb
The past participle must obey the direct object
The sentence must have a beginning a middle an end
Grammar is a barracks
With its rules its orders its punishments
Me I am a deserter
I make dream agree with reality
I let the verb love conjugate all subjects
My sentences begin in the middle and never end
I put adjectives of color on nouns of sadness
I am a syntax terrorist
A dictionary anarchist
For poetry
Is when a word
Finally decides to be free.

---

## 108. The Bridge of Sighs

It is not the bridge of
Venice
It is a little bridge of nothing at all
Over a dirty canal
But people call it the
Bridge of
Sighs
Because all the sighs of the city
Come to seek refuge there at night
There are the sighs of separated lovers
The sighs of tired workers
The sighs of old people who remember
The sighs of bored children
The bridge is made of wood and iron
But above all it is made of sighs
It is heavy with all these sadnesses
And when the wind blows
One hears a long lament
The song of all hearts
That have a little too much weight.

---

## 109. The Statesman's Suit

The suit is impeccable
Cut from the finest fabric
Gray like a certainty
The tie is a silk knot
That strangles questions
The shoes shine like promises
The suit speaks on television
It speaks of the future of the nation
Of growth and security
It uses important words
Responsibility solidarity trust
But if one looks closely
Inside the suit
There is no one
Just a cold draft of air
And the echo of its own voice
Resounding in the void.

---

## 110. The Scar

I have a scar on my hand
It is not a war wound
It is not a dog bite
It is the memory of a shard of glass
The day you broke a vase
Because I had said one word too many
The blood flowed
You cried
You took my hand in yours
And you kissed the wound
The wound healed
But the scar remained
It is white and thin
It is the map of our love
A map that shows the exact place
Where passion almost destroyed everything
And where forgiveness
Saved everything.

---

## 111. The Dialogue of the Rain and the Window

The rain said to the window
Why are you so cold and so hard
The window replied
Why are you so sad and so wet
The rain said
I weep for the sorrow of the world
The window said
Me, I reflect its solitude
The rain said
I would like to pass through you to comfort the people
The window said
Me, I would like to break to let them out
So they made a pact
The rain struck so hard
That the window shattered into a thousand pieces
And the rain entered the house
And the people went out into the street
And for the first time
They danced together in the rain
Free and wet.

---

## 112. The Cloud's Profession

My trade is to be a cloud
I have no boss no timetable
I travel with the wind
I change shape whenever I please
Sometimes I am a great white castle
Sometimes a threatening dragon
Sometimes a face that nobody recognizes
I watch the world from above
The restless little lives of men
Their wars their loves their buildings
I do not judge
I pass by
My only job
Is to cast some shade
Or to weep a little
So that the flowers can grow
And so that the sky
Will not always be
Desperately blue.

---

## 113. The Message in a Bottle

I wrote a poem
On a little scrap of paper
A poem that said
I am alone
Love me
I put it in an empty bottle
And threw it into the sea
The sea carried it away
For years it traveled
It saw whales and storms
One day it washed up on a deserted beach
Nobody found it
The sun erased the ink
The paper turned back to white
The bottle remained there
Empty
Like the heart of the one who had cast it
Proving that hope
Is the most beautiful form
Of despair.

---

## 114. The Animal Court

One day the animals put man on trial
The lion said
He kills for pleasure not for hunger
The bird said
He cages the sky
The horse said
He whips me when I am tired
The lamb said
He eats me while talking of peace
The tree said
He cuts me down to print his stupid laws
The river said
He turns me into a dustbin
So all the animals condemned man
To the worst of punishments
Solitude
They left him alone on the earth
With his money his machines and his intelligence
And man looked around him
And for the first time
He was afraid
Of his own victory.

---

## 115. The Color of Silence

Silence is not colorless
It has a thousand colors
There is the white silence of snow
Which smothers every sound
There is the black silence of the deep night
Full of fears and mysteries
There is the gray silence of boredom
In a waiting room
There is the red silence of anger
Just before the explosion
And then there is the golden silence
The silence between two lovers looking at each other
A silence so full of words
So full of music
That it is the most beautiful of all languages
A silence that has no need for color
For it is
Light itself.

---

## 116. The Collector of First Times

He does not collect stamps
He collects first times
He has in a box
The memory of a child's first step
In another
The taste of the first kiss
He has the sound of the first word
The fear of the first day of school
The pain of the first heartbreak
His collection is the most precious in the world
For it is made of unique things
Which will never return
In the evening he opens his boxes
And he relives those moments
He is not nostalgic
He knows that life
Is a collection of first times
And that the most important one
Is always the one yet to come
The first time
That we will see the day break
Tomorrow.

---

## 117. The Revolt of the Question Mark

The question mark had had enough
Enough of always asking questions
Enough of always doubting
Enough of being the symbol of uncertainty
One day it stood up straight
It lost its curve
It became an exclamation mark
It began to assert things
The sky is green
The fish sing
Love is simple
It was wonderful
The world was full of joyful certitudes
But people began to get bored
There was no more mystery
Nothing left to discover
So the exclamation mark regretted its curve
It understood that asking a question
Is often more beautiful
Than knowing the answer
And it gently bent back again
Happy to be once more
A fishhook to catch dreams.

---

## 118. The Umbrella Merchant

He does not like the sun
He likes the rain
When it rains he goes out into the street
With his umbrellas of every color
He does not sell shelters
He sells portable skies
A red umbrella for singing in the rain
A yellow umbrella to make a sun
A blue umbrella for dreaming of the sea
A large black umbrella for two lovers
People buy an umbrella from him
And they begin to love the rain
They walk smiling under the drops
