They look at the world through a colored filter
The umbrella seller
Is a practical poet
He knows that to change life
Sometimes it is enough
To change the color
Of the sky above our heads.

---

## 119. The Secret Life of Proper Names

Proper nouns have a secret life
The street of
Peace dreams of silence
Mister
Leblanc has a black heart
Miss
Legrand feels very small
The city of
Brest is always in mourning
The
Seine is full of sorrow Paris is a bet lost in advance
Words lie to us
They wear masks
They make us believe in things that do not exist
We must listen to them with suspicion
We must look behind
To see the truth
Which often hides
In common nouns
Like "pain" "joy" or "love".

---

## 120. The Streetlamp Blues

I am a street lamp
I am paid to light up others
I see everything at night
Stolen kisses
Hidden tears
Solitary steps
Shady deals
I am the silent confidant of the city
I know all the stories
But I cannot speak
And I am always alone
My only friend is the moon
But she is so far away
Sometimes a moth comes to burn its wings
On my hot bulb
It seeks the light
Like everyone else
Me, I give the light
But I am in the dark
The dark of my own solitude
And I wait for the morning
To be able at last
To go out and sleep.

---

## 121. The Revolt of the Ellipses

We are the three ellipsis points
We are the silence after the sentence
The hesitation the subtext
We are what is left unsaid
But today we have had enough
We want to speak
We want to shout
We want to finish the sentences
We want to say that love is not always beautiful
That hope is sometimes a lie
That the end is not always happy
We are not an open door
We are a wall
The wall against which words come to break
So the next time you use us
Remember
That our silence
Is full of terrible words
Just waiting for a sign
To make everything explode.

---

## 122. The Collector of Farewells

He collects farewells
He records them in his head
The quick farewell on a station platform
The whispered farewell in an unmade bed
The silent farewell of a look
The farewell of a child leaving for school
Every farewell has its music
A sad little music
Which speaks of separation and of passing time
He has the largest collection of farewells in the world
He is the richest man in departures
But he is also the poorest
For he has never collected
A single hello
A single return
His life is a long succession of closing doors
And he waits
The final farewell
His own.

---

## 123. The Poem That Was Looking for a Reader

A poem escaped from a book
It was completely naked without a title without an author
It wandered through the streets
It knocked on the doors of hearts
But the hearts were closed
Or busy with something else
The poem grew cold
It sat down on a bench
It began to weep words of rain
An old lady passed by
She picked it up
She put it in her pocket
Against her heart
She did not read it
She just kept it warm
And the poem understood
That it had no need to be read
In order to exist
It only needed
A little human warmth.

---

## 124. The Revolt of the Still Lifes

In the museum at night
The apple of
Cézanne said
I am fed up with being an apple
I want to roll on the ground
The violin of
Picasso said
I want to play some wild music
Not be a wooden jigsaw puzzle
The pipe of
Magritte said
This is a pipe and I want to smoke
So all the still lifes came to life
The fruits organized a food fight
The instruments played a cacophonous symphony
The objects danced on the walls
The night watchman thought he was going mad
He called the police
But when the police arrived
Everything had become calm and motionless again
Everything in its place
Except that the apple of
Cézanne
Had a tiny bite
And that the pipe of
Magritte
Still smelled a little of stale tobacco.

---

## 125. The Merchant of Good Excuses

He has a small very discreet shop
He sells good excuses
The excuse of "I didn't have time"
The excuse of "it's not my fault"
The excuse of "I'll do it tomorrow"
He has excuses for all situations
For failed loves promises unkept
Ordinary cowardices
His excuses are made with very simple words
But they are very effective
They allow one to sleep at night
They allow one to look at oneself in the mirror
They are the little anesthetic of the conscience
His business is very prosperous
For people will always prefer
To buy a good excuse
Rather than face a bad truth.

---

## 126. The Tree's Prayer

My God who art the earth
Hallowed be my roots
Thy kingdom of sap come
Thy will be done high in my branches as it is below
Give me this day my daily rain
Forgive me my shadow over the little flowers
As I forgive the birds that nest in me
And lead me not into the temptation of the axe
But deliver me from the woodcutter
For mine is the wind the sun and the passing time
For the centuries of seasons
Amen
And may my dead leaves
Nourish the earth from whence I came.

---

## 127. The Ministry of Forgetting

It is a very important ministry
With large offices full of drafts
The civil servants work there with erasers
They erase awkward memories
The promises of politicians
The mistakes of history
The loves that hurt too much
They work day and night
So that memory is a blank page
Clean and neat
They say it is for the happiness of people
So they can move forward without the weight of the past
But sometimes an eraser is worn out
And a memory reappears
An image a smell a song
And people remember
And they understand that forgetting
Is not happiness
But just another form
Of prison.

---

## 128. The Geometry of Love

Love is not a straight line
It is a labyrinth
With dead ends and secret paths
Our two lives were two parallel lines
That were never meant to meet
But love is a poor student in geometry
It folded the sheet of reality
And our two lines crossed
At a single burning point
Now we are a circle
Without beginning or end
A perfect and fragile circle
Containing our whole universe
And we turn together
Hoping that the compass of destiny
Will never slip.

---

## 129. The Thief of Faces

He walks around at night with a strange camera
He does not take pictures
He steals faces
He steals the smile of a happy woman
The tired look of an old man
The wonder of a child
Then he goes back home
And he wears these faces
Like masks
Today he wears the woman's smile
And he feels happy
Tomorrow he will wear the old man's look
And he will feel wise
He lives a thousand lives
But he has none
For he forgot his own face
So long ago
In the mirror of a lost love.

---

## 130. The Song of Things That Fall

The rain falls
The dead leaves fall
The tears fall
The night falls
The governments fall
The masks fall
Lovers fall
In love
Everything falls sooner or later
It is the law of gravity
And of passing time
There is no need to be sad
One must learn to fall
With grace
Like a dead leaf
Dancing with the wind
Before touching the ground
And becoming the earth
From where other things
Will rise.

---

## 131. The Last Dream

When I am very old
And my memory is an empty house
I would like to have a final dream
A simple dream
I will dream that I am a child
And that I am running in a field of poppies
The sun is warm
The wind is gentle
My mother calls me for my snack
Her voice is young and full of love
I do not want a dream of paradise
With angels and music
I just want that little dream
That little piece of lost happiness
And I will wake up
Or I will not wake up
And in either case
It will be fine
For I will have carried with me
The red color of the poppies
And the warmth of the sun
On my child's skin.

---

## 132. The Wind Merchant

He sells wind in a bottle
Offshore wind that smells of salt
A little spring breeze that smells of flowers
A great autumn wind that blows away leaves
A freezing winter wind
People buy it from him
When life is too heavy too motionless
They open a bottle
And the wind comes out
It sends the papers on the desk flying
It makes the doors slam
It brings a smell from elsewhere
A taste of freedom
It does not last long
Just a moment
But it is enough
To remember that the world is large
And that walls are not eternal.

---

## 133. The Revolt of the Furniture

The table said
I am fed up with bearing the weight of meals and elbows
The chair said
Enough of supporting the weight of backsides and troubles
The bed said
I am tired of restless sleep and sad loves
The wardrobe said
I no longer want to keep skeletons and old secrets
So they moved out all on their own
The table left for the countryside to become a picnic table
The chair went to the theater to listen to stories
The bed settled on a beach to watch the stars
And the wardrobe opened wide in a forest
So that birds could build their nests in it
The apartment was empty
And the man who lived there
Had to learn to sit on the floor
And to sleep standing up
And to live without owning anything
And he found that it was
Much lighter.

---

## 134. The Lost Poem

I wrote a poem on a subway ticket
I lost it
Perhaps someone found it
A tired worker
A sad lover
A hurried banker
Perhaps they read it
And that it put a little sun into their day
Or a little sadness
Or just a question mark
Perhaps they threw it away
And that the wind makes it dance now
With the dead leaves
I will never know
And that is the beauty of poetry
It does not belong to us
It is like a bird
We open our hand
And we let it fly away
Toward unknown skies.

---

## 135. The Tool Conference

The hammer said
More strength is needed
The screwdriver said
More precision is needed
The saw said
One must know how to cut
The tape measure said
Everything must be measured
They talked all night long
Each praising its own qualities
Then the nail spoke up
A tiny nail
It said
You are all very useful
But without me
You are useless
For it is I who hold things together
I am the bond the goal the final end
The tools fell silent
They understood that strength precision measurement
Are nothing
Without a little nail of love
To join everything together.

---

## 136. The Unknown Soldier's Prayer

My God if you exist
And if you are not too busy with the generals
Tell me why
Why I died in the mud
For a piece of flag
For a word in a speech
I had a wife children a trade
I loved red wine and the sun
Now I am a name on a monument
An excuse for speeches on the eleventh of November
I do not ask you for paradise
I ask you for just one thing
Make my death
Serve at least for something
Make children learn at school
That war is a dirty business
And that the only enemy
Is the one who gives you a gun
And who tells you to kill your brother.

---

## 137. The Merchant of Silences

In the noisy city
He sells silence
He catches it at night in closed parks
He gathers it in the morning in empty churches
He picks it up in the look of statues
He has silence for every need
A little silence to concentrate
A great silence to forget the world
A complicit silence for lovers
A deadly silence for enemies
People queue in front of his shop
They are tired of the noise
Of the noise of machines of cars of televisions
Of the noise of their own thoughts
They buy a little silence
As one buys medicine
And they consume it in secret
To try to hear themselves
Live.

---

## 138. The Revolt of the Adjectives

The adjective "beautiful" said
I am fed up with qualifying sunsets and women
I want to qualify a just action
The adjective "great" said
Enough of qualifying generals and bankers
I want to qualify a small act of kindness
The adjective "true" said
I refuse to let myself be coupled with the word "lie"
So they left their nouns
They floated freely in the air
And they settled where they wanted "Beautiful" settled on the gesture of a worker sharing his bread "Great" settled on the silence of a man who listens "True" settled on a child's tear
The world became more just
And more difficult to describe
For beauty and greatness
Were no longer where we were used
To looking for them.

---

## 139. The Collector of Ends of the World

He collects ends of the world
Those announced by prophets
Those predicted by scientists
Those hoped for by the desperate
He has a large map
With dates and places
The end of the world by fire by water by ice
By human stupidity
Each time an end of the world does not take place
He puts a small cross on his map
And he smiles
His collection is a collection of non-events
A collection of ridiculous fears
He is not cynical
He is optimistic
He knows that the world is solid
And that the only end of the world that really counts
Is the one that arrives each evening
When we fall asleep
And that the only thing that matters
Is to know if we will wake up
The next day.

---

## 140. The Lock Song

I am a lock
I am the guardian of the inside
I know the secret of keys
The key that opens gently
The key that forces with anger
The key that hesitates
The key that can no longer find its way
I am the border between outside and inside
Between the world and intimacy
I am a small metal hole
But I contain universes
Laughter tears secrets
I am a small cold and hard thing
But I protect what is most fragile
The warmth of a home
The security of a refuge
And the necessary silence
To be oneself.

---

## 141. The Last Journey

He packed his bag
He did not take much
A childhood memory
An adult regret
A burst of laughter
A sad song
He closed the door of his life
Without making a sound
He did not take a ticket
He did not know where he was going
He just began to walk
Toward the horizon
Which is not a line
But a promise
The promise that something else exists
Beyond what we know
Beyond what we have been
He walked light
For he had finally understood
That the only baggage needed
For the last voyage
Is to have none at all.

---

## 142. The Weight of Clouds

They say that clouds are light
It is a lie
Clouds are heavy
Heavy with all the rain they contain
Heavy with all the unwept sorrows
Heavy with all the storms to come
They float above our heads
Like continents of sadness
Sometimes a cloud can take no more
It bursts into tears
And it rains on the earth
People open their umbrellas
They protect themselves from this sadness falling from the sky
They do not know that this rain
Is necessary
To wash the world
And to allow the sun
To shine again
On a clean and peaceful earth.

---

## 143. The Revolt of the Odd Numbers

The
One the
Three the
Five the
Seven the
Nine
Had had enough
Enough of being odd
Enough of not being able to be divided in two
Enough of being the symbols of solitude and imbalance
They decided to unite
They formed a large strange and magnificent number
A number that meant nothing
A number that obeyed no logic
The even numbers looked at them with contempt
You are not rational they said
The odd numbers replied
So what
Life is not rational
Love is not rational
Poetry is not rational
We are the numbers of life
And we are proud
To be a little twisted.

---

## 144. The Merchant of First Words

He sells the first words of children
In small music boxes "Mama" "Papa" "Again"
Every word has the music of the voice that spoke it
A pure music full of wonder
Old people come to buy them from him
When their memory fades
They open a box
And they hear the first word of their child
Who is now fifty years old
And who no longer talks to them
It is a sweet little torture
A small happiness that hurts
The merchant is a sad man
He sells the beginnings of stories
But he knows that all stories
End one day
In silence.

---

## 145. The Saints' Strike

Saint
Anthony said
I am fed up with searching for others' lost objects
I would like to lose something too
Saint
Christopher said
Enough of carrying people on my back
I would like someone to carry me a little
Saint
Rita patron saint of hopeless causes said
My own cause is hopeless I resign
So all the saints left their churches
They went to have a drink at the café opposite
They talked about their lives of sacrifices
Of their miracles and their doubts
People entered the empty churches
They prayed in the silence
They prayed not to saints
But to their own hopes their own strengths
And for the first time
Some prayers
Were answered.

---

## 146. The Tattooed Poem

He had a poem tattooed on his heart
A poem that no one could read
Except the woman he loved
She would lay her head on his chest
And she would read the words with her fingers
With her lips
It was their secret their garden
One day the woman left
The poem remained
But it had become illegible
The letters had jumbled up
The words no longer made sense
It was no longer a love poem
It was a scar
A confused map of pain
And the man spent the rest of his life
Trying to decipher
The language of his own sorrow.

---

## 147. The Revolt of the Red Lights

The red light said
I am fed up with being the symbol of forbidden things
The amber light said
I am tired of being the symbol of hesitation
So they exchanged their roles
The red light became green
The green light became amber
The amber light became red
The motorists went mad
They speeded up at red
They braked at green
They honked at amber
It was chaos
But a joyful chaos
A chaos where rules no longer existed
Where everyone had to trust
Their own judgment
And that of others
And that day
Nobody arrived late
For the destination
Was no longer the most important thing.

---

## 148. The Taste of Absence

Your absence has a taste
It is not a bitter taste
It is not a sweet taste
It is the taste of unsalted food
The taste of water when one is not thirsty
The taste of bread when one is not hungry
It is a taste of nothing
But a nothing that fills everything
A nothing that weighs heavy on the tongue
A nothing that prevents tasting
The rest of the world
Your absence
Is the only dish
That I eat every day
And that never nourishes me.

---

## 149. The Sunset Collector

He does not collect objects
He collects sunsets
Every evening he sits facing west
And he watches
He watches the sky catch fire
The red the orange the violet
He records every shade in his memory
He has an immense collection
The sunset on the sea
On the mountain
On the city
The sunset on a day of happiness
A day of sadness
He does not photograph them
He lives them
He knows that every sunset is unique
And that it is a gift
A free and magnificent show
Offered by the departing day
To comfort us
For the arriving night.

---

## 150. The Dance of Passwords

My first password was my dog's name
My second the name of my first love
My third a birth date
Now my passwords are monsters "Zk78!bX_p"
Soulless combinations
That protect my digital life
My bank my emails my photos
I am a prisoner of my own secrets
I spend my time inventing passwords
Forgetting them
Resetting them
I have a fortress around my life
But inside there is not much left
Just a man who is afraid
And who has forgotten the password
The most important one
The one that opens the door
Of his own heart.

---

## 151. The Last Tree in the City

It was the last tree
An old chestnut tree on a concrete square
Birds no longer dared to perch there
For fear of being the only ones
Lovers no longer carved their hearts on it
For fear it would bring bad luck
The tree was alone
It watched the cars the buildings the noise
It remembered the time when there was a forest
Animals rivers
It was the last witness of a vanished world
One day men came with saws
They said
It is dangerous it is going to fall
They cut it down
And in its place they put
A bronze statue
Representing a tree
And everyone found it
Very modern
And very safe.

---

## 152. The Souvenir Seller

On the
Pont des
Arts there is a man
Who sells neither padlocks nor portraits
He sells memories by calling out
A kilo of first love for ten francs
A very ripe regret a little bitter
A handful of children's laughter guaranteed fresh
He has second-hand hopes in good condition
Comfortable silences for two people
Blind rages almost brand new
A tear of joy in a small glass vial
The police pass by and say nothing
Memories are not subject to
VAT
A young woman approaches
I would like to forget she says
The seller shakes his head
Sorry madam I don't do buybacks
I only sell what people have lost
Not what they would like to throw away.

---

## 153. The Clocks' Strike

One Tuesday morning at exactly eight o'clock
All the clocks in the town stopped
The one at the station the one at the church the one at the post office
The wristwatches the alarm clocks the Swiss cuckoo clocks
They had had enough of slicing life into pieces
Enough of running after a future that never arrives
Enough of eating seconds like dry insects
The bosses shouted
The teachers screamed
The generals stamped their feet
But time moved no longer
People went out into the streets
They looked at each other for the first time
They talked
They had a coffee that lasted for hours
Children played on the highways
It was the most beautiful day in the world
A day that never ended
For no one ever knew what time it was to end it.

---

## 154. The Wind's Speech

The wind wears no tie
The wind makes no official speeches
But when it speaks it tells the truth
It passes through the fancy streets
And whispers at the windows of the rich
Your safes are full of tears
Your carpets are woven with silence
It passes through the factories
And shouts to the workers
Your lungs are grey with the dust of your lost lives
Your fatigue is the fuel of their luxury cars
It passes over the battlefields
And blows upon the wooden crosses
It speaks the names of the dead whom everyone forgot
The wind is neither of the right nor of the left
It is just
And when it falls silent
It is because the world has grown so heavy
That even the wind no longer has the strength to lift it.

---

## 155. The Lesson of Silence

The new teacher said
Today a lesson in silence
He wrote
SILENCE on the blackboard
And he sat down
The children had never seen anything like it
At first they whispered
Then they grew quiet
And in the silence they began to hear
The sound of their own beating hearts
The sound of a fly bumping against the windowpane
The sound of a cloud drifting across the sky
The sound of an idea born in a neighbor's head
Silence was not empty
It was full of small secret tunes
The teacher smiled
He erased the word
SILENCE
And wrote instead
LISTEN
The bell rang
But for the first time
Nobody wanted to go out.

---

## 156. The Flat-Pack Heart

I bought my heart in a department store
It was a flat-pack kit in a cardboard box
With a very complicated instruction manual
Part
A: tenderness
Part
B: passion (handle with care)
Part
C: jealousy (manipulate with gloves)
Screws of trust and nuts of patience
I assembled it all as I was supposed to
But there was always one part left over
A strange little part without a name
I put it aside
My heart worked well
It loved it suffered it hoped
But one day it stopped
The repairman came
He looked at my heart and said
Ah but the most important part is missing
The strange little part without a name
It is the part of forgiveness
Without it no heart can ever truly restart.

---

## 157. The Poet and the Accountant

The accountant said to the poet
You are not profitable
Your words bring in nothing
Your dreams pay no taxes
The poet looked at the accountant
And he said
And you what do you do
The accountant replied proudly
I count
I count other people's money
I put life into columns
Debit
Credit
The poet smiled
I count too
I count the stars in the sky
I count the tears on a cheek
I count the beats of a heart in love
And at the end of the day
It is I who am the richest
For these numbers of mine
Can never be stolen.

---

## 158. The City That Dreams

At night when men are sleeping
The city wakes up and dreams
Statues climb down from their pedestals
And go for a drink at the corner café
The traffic lights play cards
Red always loses
The subway mouths sing sad operas
Of loves lost in the tunnels
The buildings tell each other stories
Of all the people they have sheltered
The joys the sorrows the births the deaths
The river dreams it is a great silver serpent
Dancing under the moon
And the public benches dream
That one day lovers will come and sit
And stay there forever
Then the first garbage truck passes by
And the city pretends to go back to sleep.

---

## 159. The Atheist's Prayer

Oh
Nothingness who art nowhere
Hallowed be thy absence
Thy silence come unto us
Thy emptiness be done on
Earth as it is in
Heaven
Give us this day our bread of chance
Forgive us our certainties
As we also forgive those who believe in thee
And lead us not into the temptation of eternity
But deliver us from priests
For ours is the doubt the question and the beauty
For the little time we have left
Here and now
Amen
And let the great silence be our answer.

---

## 160. The Last Lover

In a world of faces lit by screens
In a world of conversations made of thumbs up
There was a man and a woman
Who met in a library
They did not exchange profiles
They exchanged books
Then they sat on a bench
And they talked
With their mouths their eyes their hands
They talked about the weather
About the color of the sky and the taste of coffee
They did not take a photo
They made a memory
From the fabric of the present moment
People walked by and looked at them
Like animals of an extinct species
Strange creatures from another time
The man and the woman smiled
They were the last lovers
The only survivors
Of the great digital shipwreck.

---

## 161. The Factory of Sorrows

There is a factory that runs day and night
It makes neither cars nor shoes
It makes sorrow
Sad workers in grey coats
Mix regrets with tears
They add a pinch of solitude
And a healthy dose of bad luck
The sorrow comes out on a conveyor belt
Wrapped in pretty boxes with ribbons
There is love-sorrow for the young
The sorrow of passing time for the old
The little Sunday night sorrow for everyone
The boss is a very rich and very unhappy man
He keeps the most beautiful sorrows for himself
Those that are so pure so perfect
That they look almost like happiness
And he looks at them and weeps
Proud of his production.

---

## 162. The Ghost of the Metro

I am the ghost of the station
Châtelet
I am not scary
I am just transparent
I walk through people
And feel their thoughts
The young girl afraid of her exam
The worker with a sore back
The banker thinking of his money
The lover thinking of their date
I am a sponge for lives
I absorb everything
Fatigue hope boredom joy
I know all the secrets of Paris
But I can tell them to no one
Sometimes I sit next to a musician
And try to sing with him
But no sound comes from my mouth of fog
So I watch the trains come and go
Full of lives I will never touch
And that is my hell
Not fire
Just solitude.

---

## 163. The Revolt of the Statues

One night the statues had had enough
The general on his bronze horse
The poet with his stone pen
The king with his marble sceptre
Sick of standing in the rain and the sun
Sick of being a perch for pigeons
Sick of representing a dead glory
So they stepped down from their pedestals
The general charged the cars
The poet wrote insults on the walls of banks
The king tried to sell his crown for a coffee
It was a great night of disorder and freedom
In the morning the police arrested them all
For vandalism of public property
And put them back on their pedestals
But something had changed
In their stone eyes
There was now a tiny spark of defiance
And they were just waiting
For the next night.

---

## 164. The Thief of Colors

There was a very sad man
Who hated colors
One day he built a large machine
A color vacuum cleaner
He sucked up the red of the poppies
The blue of the sky
The green of the trees
The yellow of the sun
The world became grey
Grey like his heart
People were sad and walked with heads bowed
But a child had kept a small box of colored pencils
He sat on the ground
And began to draw
A big yellow sun a red flower a blue sky
The colors were so bright so strong
That they blew up the thief's machine
And the world recovered its colors
And even the thief
Got a little spot of red on the tip of his grey nose.

---

## 165. The Song of the Ink Stain

I am an inkblot on a white page
I am not a mistake
I am a possibility
I can be a black bird taking flight
A hole in reality to peek through to the other side
The shadow of a word that didn't dare write itself
The eye of a gentle monster
The poet looks at me
He doesn't reach for his eraser
He reaches for his pen
And around me he draws a world
He draws mountains rivers houses
And suddenly I am no longer a blot
I am the lake in the middle of the landscape
I am the black and deep heart of his poem
And I am happy
For even a mistake
Can become the center of the universe.

---

## 166. The Menu of the Day

At the restaurant 'At the Sign
of Life'
The menu is written in chalk on a slate
For starter
Salad of simple little pleasures
Or
A piping hot soup of nostalgia
For main course
Stew of passion long simmered
Accompanied by its potatoes of patience
Or
April fool fish grilled on a bed of doubts
For dessert
Chocolate mousse from childhood
Or
Fruit salad of forgetfulness
The wine is a wine of friendship
That never gives you a headache
And the coffee is black coffee
Black like a well-kept secret
The bill is never the same
You pay with a smile a memory or a tear.

---

## 167. The Asphalt Gardener

There was an old man
Whom everyone called the asphalt gardener
He grew neither roses nor tulips
He cultivated the cracks in the pavement
Every morning he went out with a small watering can
And watered the wild little weeds
The stubborn dandelions
The small nameless flowers growing between the cobblestones
People made fun of him
But he smiled
He said
Life is stronger than concrete
Beauty is more stubborn than ugliness
One day the town hall decided to repave the walk
They covered everything with new smooth asphalt
The old man was sad
But the following spring
A small yellow flower broke through the tar
Right in front of his door
And the old man began to water again.

---

## 168. The Memory of the Staircase

I am an old wooden staircase in a Parisian building
I creak under footsteps
It is my way of speaking
I know the memory of feet
The heavy steps of the worker coming home tired
The light steps of the lover taking them two at a time
The hesitant little steps of the child learning to walk
The slow steps of the old lady afraid of falling
I have felt the weight of moving days
Furniture going up full of hope
Furniture going down full of sadness
I drank the tears that fell on my steps
I heard the laughter echoing in my stairwell
I am the silent witness to a hundred years of life
And if one day they destroy me
Every piece of my wood
Will keep within it the scent of a step
The ghost of a story.

---

## 169. The Trial of the Sun

One day they put on trial the
Sun
The prosecution said
It is too hot it causes heatstroke
It is too bright it hurts the eyes
It reveals the dust on the furniture
And the wrinkles on the faces
It is guilty of too much truth
The defense called a witness
A sunflower
The sunflower said nothing
It just turned its head slowly toward the
Sun
And smiled
The judge looked at the sunflower
Then he looked at the
Sun
And he said
The accused is acquitted
Those who do not like the light
Can just stay in their cellar
The court is adjourned
And everyone went to have a drink on the terrace.

---

## 170. The Letter Never Written

My dearest
No that's not right
My love
That's too cliché
I just wanted to tell you
Tell you what
That the sky without you is a blank page
That my bed is a continent of solitude
That I miss the sound of your steps more than music
That I tried to write to you a hundred times
And a hundred times the words hid under the bed
Like children afraid of the dark
I wanted to tell you that I love you
But the word
LOVE is a garment too big or too small
That never fits me
So I do not write to you
I take the sheet of paper
And look at it
And on this blank page
Is everything I did not know how to tell you
And it is the most beautiful love letter in the world.

---

## 171. The Last Word

The old man was on his deathbed
His family was around him
The priest spoke to him of paradise
The doctor spoke to him of morphine
Everyone was waiting for his last word
The word that would sum up his life
The word of wisdom for posterity
The old man opened his eyes
He looked at his wife his son his daughter his dog
He looked at the priest and the doctor
He took a deep breath
And he said
Damn
Then he died
The family was shocked
The priest was scandalized
The doctor noted the time of death
Only the dog understood
He wagged his tail
For 'Damn' was the word the old man said
When he was truly happy
And couldn't find a word big enough to say it.

---

## 172. The Office of Lost Things

At the lost and found office
There are umbrellas that have forgotten the rain
Orphan gloves looking for a hand
Keys that have lost the memory of their lock
And then there are other things
Those without a name on the label
A burst of laughter abandoned in a draft
A promise made one summer evening and never kept
A piece of courage left on a station platform
The comfortable silence between two old friends
The first glance of a love that never happened
The clerk with his grey mustache
Notes everything down in a large ledger
But he never returns anything
For to find these things
You do not need an address
You need a heart.

---

## 173. The Geography Lesson

The teacher points at the map with his pointer
The
Seine is a blue line that does not move
The
Alps are brown triangles standing very well-behaved
The ocean is a big blue spot without waves
Repeat after me says the teacher
France
Is shaped like a hexagon
But a child at the back of the class
Sees the
Seine overflowing from the map
Flooding the classroom with barges and fish
He sees the
Alps shaking their snow onto the master's desk
And the Atlantic
Ocean coming in through the window
With its waves its salt and its great birds
The teacher shouts a little louder
THE HEXAGON
But the child is already gone
He is sailing on the
Seine in a boat made of blotting paper
And skiing on
Mont
Blanc
Which is the bald head of the teacher.

---

## 174. The General's Sunday

On Sundays the general does not wage war
He reads his newspaper
The news is bad
There are no new battles
He carves the roast chicken
With surgical precision
The left thigh for
Madame
The right thigh for him
The enemy is defeated
Then he goes down to the cellar
Where he plays with his toy soldiers
He organizes grand maneuvers on the ping-pong table
He shouts orders to his lead regiments
Forward charge fire
The cat watches the general
The cat doesn't care
The cat is not a soldier
The cat is an anarchist sleeping in the sun.

---

## 175. The Color of Your Absence

Since you went away
The sky has one color less
The coffee tastes like ashes
Silence has taken up all the space in the house
It sits in your armchair
It reads your books
It wears your dresses
Sometimes I speak to it
I ask it if it has seen you
Silence does not answer
It looks at me with your eyes
Your absence is not an emptiness
It is a solid and heavy thing
A statue of sorrow that I must carry every day
And which has the exact shape of your body.

---

## 176. The Flea Market

On the stalls of the flea market
The lives of others are for sale for next to nothing
A porcelain doll with a broken eye
That has seen too many family secrets
A toy soldier who lost his war
A chipped coffee cup
That remembers a thousand mornings
A violin without strings
Keeping inside all the music it never played
The merchant shouts
Everything must go
But nothing really disappears
Memories are stubborn
They cling to rust and dust
And wait for a new hand
To tell the same story.

---

## 177. The Living Statue

By dint of taking the same bus at the same time
Sitting at the same desk in front of the same wall
Eating the same sandwich at the same break
The man became a statue
One morning he did not get up
He remained sitting on the square's bench
Motionless and grey
Pigeons poop on his head
Lovers carve their names on his back
Children play hide-and-seek behind him
He feels nothing
He has become a monument to boredom
Sometimes a stone tear flows down his cheek
When he remembers a time
When he was made of flesh and blood
And when he still knew how to run.

---

## 178. The Worker's Prayer

Our Boss who art in the office
Forgotten be thy name
Thy kingdom end
Thy will no longer be done in the factory or anywhere else
Give us this day our daily bread
And a little wine to go with it
Forgive us our urges to rebel
As we forgive your indecent profits
And lead us not into the temptation of overtime
But deliver us from the machine
For ours is the sweat the fatigue and the life
For the few years we have left
Amen
And shit.

---

## 179. The Words on Strike

One day the words had had enough
The word
LOVE no longer wanted to be in the mouths of tyrants
The word
FREEDOM no longer wanted to be on prison walls
The word
TRUTH was tired of lying
So they went on strike
They marched into the streets
The politician opened his mouth for a speech
And nothing came out
The general wanted to shout an order
And made the sound of a duck
The advertiser wanted to sell soap
And only stammered
It was a great silence
A magnificent and terrible silence
And in this silence
We finally heard the sound of the wind in the trees
And the beating heart of a sleeping child.

---

## 180. The Gardener Who Watered Names

The cemetery gardener
Does not plant flowers
He waters the names on the gravestones
Good day
Monsieur
Dupont your regrets are a bit dry today
Watch out
Madame
Durand your solitude is growing crooked
He speaks to the dead as to friends
He tells them the news of the village
The loves of the living
The foolishness of children
The dead do not answer
But they listen
Their roots of silence drink his words
And sometimes at night
A marble rose blooms on an old grave
Just to say thank you to him.

---

## 181. The Recipe for Happiness

Take a large blue sky
Add a ray of morning sun
Mix with a child's laughter
Fold in a look of love
Let it rest in the hollow of a knowing silence
Sprinkle with a few useless and tender words
Add neither reason nor logic
It would ruin the taste
Serve immediately
This dish cannot be preserved
It must be lived
And do not ask the neighbor for the recipe
Everyone must invent their own
With the ingredients they have at hand
And in their heart.

---

## 182. The Mirror's Confession

I am the mirror of a cheap hotel room
I have seen faces tired from the road
Faces illuminated by a one-night love
Faces hollowed out by solitude
I have seen silent tears
Smiles that lied
Suitcases full of hopes
And suitcases full of regrets
I do not judge
I reflect
I am the cold and faithful memory of passing lives
I keep within me all these reflections
And sometimes at night when all is dark
I project them on the wall
And watch my own movie
Which is the movie of humanity.

---

## 183. The Last Tree

In the city of concrete and steel
One tree remained
An old plane tree in a small square
Developers wanted to chop it down
To build a parking lot
But the people of the neighborhood said no
Children formed a ring around it
Lovers carved their hearts on its bark
The old folks came to sit in its shade
The tree was no longer a tree
It was the heart of the neighborhood
So the developers built their parking lot elsewhere
And the tree remained
It gives neither fruit nor flowers
It gives only shade and hope
And that is much more important.

---

## 184. The Cloud Factory

In a large factory near the sky
Workers in grey coats manufacture the clouds
There is the cumulus department
For lazy afternoons
The stratus department
For melancholy mornings
And the nimbus department
For when the world needs washing
The foreman shouts
Faster more clouds
The boss wants rain on the competitor's shares
But a young dreamy worker
Secretly makes clouds in the shape of a horse
Of a face or a boat
He releases them into the sky
And watches the children below
Who point at them laughing
It is his small act of poetic sabotage.

---

## 185. The Liar

There was a man who lied all the time
He said he had seen fish fly
Cats speak Latin
Honest politicians
Nobody believed him
But he lied so well
With so much detail and passion
That his lies began to come to life
One day a goldfish flew through his window
His cat asked him the time in Latin
And the town mayor confessed all his sins on TV
The man was terrified
His world of lies had become reality
He tried to tell the truth
He said
The sky is blue
And the sky turned green with pink polka dots
He was a prisoner of his own talent.

---

## 186. The Laundry of Worries

Every Monday morning
She does the big wash of the week's worries
Into the washing machine
She puts her husband's shirt of anger
The tablecloth stained by mean words
The dirty socks of fatigue
She adds a dose of forgiveness
And a bit of softener of forgetfulness
The machine spins
It washes whiter than white
It erases the stains of the soul
And when she hangs the laundry on the line
The wind carries away the last remains of sadness
And the week can start again
Clean and fresh
Until next Monday.

---

## 187. The Collector of Sounds

He collected neither stamps nor butterflies
He collected sounds
In well-labeled glass jars
He had the sound of a first kiss
A shy and wet little sound
The sound of the silence of a snow-covered forest
The sound of a door slamming in an argument
The sound of a popping champagne cork
The sound of a pencil on paper
In the evening he opened his jars
And listened
He re-listened to the symphonies of life
The false notes and the harmonies
It was his own way
Of forgetting nothing.

---

## 188. The Passport

A passport is a sad little book
Full of stamps saying where you have been
But saying nothing of what you saw
There is the stamp of the border where you were afraid
The stamp of the country where you were in love
The stamp of the airport where you waited for nothing
Your ID photo looks at you
With the look of a criminal
You do not recognize yourself
It is not you
It is just a number a birthdate a nationality
A passport is not a travel journal
It is a criminal record of your travels
Proving that you belong to a country
Before you belong to yourself.

---

## 189. The Revolt of the Vegetables

One morning on the market stall
The carrot said
I am sick of being grated
The potato said
Enough of being mashed
The pea said
I refuse to be squashed by a fork
So they organized a revolt
The tomatoes bombarded the merchant
The onions made her cry
The leeks fought like sabres
It was a great vegetable revolution
Customers were scared and ran away
And the vegetables won
They set up a free and independent republic
On the marketplace
Until the goats arrived.

---

## 190. The Harbor Siren

It is not a woman with a fish tail
The harbor siren
It is a great hoarse and sad voice
That sings when the fog rolls in
It sings for the boats that get lost
It sings for the sailors who are homesick
It sings for the women waiting on the dock
Its song is made of salt rust and departures
It is the lament of all loves separated by the sea
It is the blues of the open sea
And when it falls silent
The silence is even heavier
For we know that the fog has swallowed everything
And that we must wait for morning
To count the boats that have returned.

---

## 191. The Blind Photographer

He was a photographer but he was blind
People laughed at him
How can he take photos
If he sees nothing
But he did not photograph with his eyes
He photographed with his hands his nose his ears
He took photos of the warmth of a hand in his
The smell of warm bread coming out of the oven
The sound of a child's laughter
The silence of a flowing tear
His photos were all black
But when you looked at them with the heart
You saw everything
You saw life
As it really is
Invisible to the eyes.

---

## 192. The Postman of Words

The mailman does not deliver letters
He delivers words
In his bag he has heavy words
Like stones of sorrow
Words light as feathers of laughter
Words that sting like nettles
Words that caress like velvet
There are love words he slips under doors
Angry words he throws at the windows of the powerful
Forgiveness words he leaves on public benches
The mailman never rings
He knows that every word
Will always find its recipient
Even if it is twenty years later
In the hollow of a memory.

---

## 193. The Meeting of the Shadows

At midnight when the city sleeps
Shadows detach themselves from the walls
And meet in the Place de la République
The banker's shadow is long and thin
The worker's shadow is bent by fatigue
The lover's shadow seeks the shadow of their love
They do not speak
They dance
A slow and silent waltz
They exchange their shapes their secrets their regrets
The dog's shadow pees on the general's shadow
Nobody is offended
Here there is no hierarchy
Just the grand democracy of the night
Then the first ray of sun arrives
And each shadow returns obediently
To cling to the feet of its owner
Waiting for the next night.

---

## 194. The Sky on Sale

I saw a strange shop
With a sign that said 'The
Sky on
Sale -
Total
Clearance'
Inside an old man was selling pieces of sky
A yard of blue sky for gloomy days
A small white cloud to make it pretty
A sunset slightly damaged but cheap
A storm in a jar for thrill-seekers
A handful of stars for dreamers
I asked
Do you not have the moon
The old man smiled
The moon is not for sale sir
It belongs to everyone
Like love
And like death.

---

## 195. The Colors' Strike

Red
Said
I am tired of being the color of blood and war
I want to be the color of cherries and kisses
Black
Said
Enough of being the color of mourning and misery
I want to be the color of the night's velvet and cats' eyes
White
Said
I no longer want to be the color of surrender
But that of the page where everything remains to be written
So all the colors went on strike
The world became grey
A great grey boredom
Generals could no longer decorate their chests
Advertisers could no longer sell their lies
People stopped watching television
And began to look at themselves
It was so terrifying
That they begged the colors to return
Promising to use them better.

---

## 196. The Mechanic of Time

He has his workshop at the corner of Forgetfulness Street
He does not repair watches
He repairs time
People bring him their broken memories
A lost hour of happiness
A missing minute of courage
A second of inattention that changed everything
He pulls out his small tools
A pair of regret pliers
A nostalgia screwdriver
An oil can of hope oil
He works patiently
He resolders the broken moments
He resets the hands to the heart's time
He cannot change the past
But sometimes he manages to make it
A little less heavy to carry.

---

## 197. The Soup Kitchen

They queue in the cold
Their faces are winter landscapes
Their hands are knotted roots
They do not speak
Their stories are frozen in their eyes
The woman who serves the soup
Has the tired smile of an angel working overtime
She does not only give soup
She gives a spoonful of warmth
A bowl of dignity
The soup is hot
It has the simple taste of survival
It warms the belly
But it does not warm the soul
For in the soup there is no justice
There is only pity
And pity
Is the tip that the rich leave to misery.

---

## 198. The Confessional of a Park Bench

I am an old green wooden bench
I am the confessional of the churchless
I have heard more secrets than all the priests of Paris
The promises of lovers
That last only for the length of a season
The whispers of the lonely
Who talk to the pigeons
The plans of rebels
Who want to remake the world
The sobs of the hopeless
Who have nowhere left to go
I do not give absolution
I do not judge
I keep it all
My wood is soaked in all these lives
And if one day they burn me
My smoke will tell the sky
All the prayers of the asphalt.

---

## 199. The Architect of Ruins

He does not build houses
He does not build bridges
He is an architect of ruins
He chooses a beautiful place
A clearing a cliff
And he builds a wall that is already collapsing
A tower that leans
A staircase leading nowhere
He works with tired stones
Bricks that have already lived through a lot
Then he goes away
And he lets time do its work
The wind the rain the ivy
His ruins are poems of stone
Teaching men
The beauty of what ends
The wisdom of what falls
And the patience of what begins again.

---

## 200. The Flying Lesson

The teacher said to the children
Today we are going to learn to fly
Forget gravity
Forget that two and two make four
Forget your name your address
Close your eyes
Think of something light
A feather a laugh a secret
Now jump
The children jumped
Some of them fell
Those who had learned their lessons too well
But others floated
They floated in the classroom like happy balloons
They flew out the window
And they went to play with the birds
The teacher remained alone
He had never managed to forget
That his own heart was heavy.

---

## 201. The Ferryman of Dreams

He has a boat made of silence
His oars are moonbeams
Every night he waits on the shore of sleep
People arrive tired from their day
They get into his boat
And he takes them to the other side
To the islands of dreams
There is the island of rediscovered loves
The island where you can fly
The island where the dead return to speak to us
The ferryman asks for no money
His only pay
Is seeing the peaceful faces of the sleepers
When he brings them back in the early morning
To the grey shore of reality
Leaving them a little dream sand
At the bottom of their pockets.

---

## 202. Canned Sadness

In a grey factory by a dead river
They put sadness in cans
There is ordinary sadness for everyday consumption
Luxury sadness with a taste of nostalgia
The little Sunday evening sadness on special offer
The workers are ghosts
They do not speak
They sort tears
They label sighs
The boss is a man who has never cried
He says that sadness is a market of the future
He exports worldwide
And people buy
They open the cans at night in front of the television
And consume their own misfortune
Finding that it has a familiar taste
And almost comforting.

---

## 203. The Rain's Speech

I am the rain
I make no speeches
I fall
I fall on the rich and on the poor
On the statues of heroes and on the graves of the unknown
I wash the cars of ministers
And the faces of the homeless
I have no party
I have no god
I am the great democrat
I reveal the truth
I show the cracks in the walls
The lies that run with makeup
The sad colors of the world when there is no sun left to lie
People say I am sad
But it is not I who am sad
It is the world
I only give it
A liquid and honest mirror.

---

## 204. The Collector of Echoes

He lives in a silent valley
And he collects echoes
He has the echo of a child's laughter lost in the mountains
The echo of an "I love you" whispered a hundred years ago
The echo of a soldier's last cry
The echo of a forgotten song
He keeps them in deep caves
Each echo has its own color its own temperature
In the evening he sits at the entrance of a cave
And he listens
He listens to the sound memory of the world
He never feels alone
For he knows that nothing is truly lost
That every word every sound
Continues to vibrate somewhere
And that you only need to know how to listen closely
To hear eternity.

---

## 205. The Revolt of the Road Signs

The
STOP sign said
I'm sick of stopping everyone
Today I say
GO AHEAD
The
NO
ENTRY sign said
I'm tired of forbidding
Today everything is allowed
The
GIVE
WAY
sign said
Today I give up nothing
Take what you are owed
It was a monstrous mess
Cars bumped into each other laughing
Pedestrians crossed dancing
The police tried to restore order
But the station sign
Had replaced its cap with a straw hat
And it played the accordion
It was the first day of road anarchy
And strangely
There was no accident
Just lots of people happy to be finally lost.

---

## 206. The Merchant of Maybe

He has his little stall in an alley
He sells nothing solid
He sells maybes
A maybe yes
A maybe no
A maybe if you dare
A maybe if you wait
People come to see him when they are at the end of their rope
When life has left them nothing but grey certainty
They buy a little maybe
Not expensive
Just enough to hold on one more day
Just enough to open a small window
In the wall of their despair
The merchant never grows rich
But he has the finest of trades
He is a sower of fertile doubts
In the arid field of reality.

---

## 207. The Cemetery of Forgotten Words

There is a secret place that only poets know
It is the cemetery of forgotten words
There rest magnificent words
That no one uses anymore "Désuet" "Coquecigrue" "Nitescence"
Each word has its little grave of dust
With its etymology carved upon it
Sometimes a word rises
And comes to haunt a poem
Bringing with it a scent of yesteryear
A strange music
The other words look at it with suspicion
But the poet welcomes it
For he knows that words never truly die
They sleep
And that a little love is enough
To wake them up
And restore their place in the world.

---

## 208. The Dance of the Littered Papers

When the wind rises in the city
The scrap papers begin to dance
An old candy wrapper
A worn subway ticket
A newspaper page talking about a forgotten war
They swirl on the asphalt
A sad and light-hearted waltz
They are the ghosts of our consumption
The souls of our indifference
They tell the story of our rushed lives
A child watches them from the window
He does not see garbage
He sees paper butterflies
Birds of misery
And he finds it beautiful
Because his heart has not yet learned
To tell the difference between what has value
And what has none.

---

## 209. The Tailor of Silences

He has his shop in the street of
Calm
He does not sew suits
He sews custom-made silences
He takes your measurements with a wind tape measure
He makes you choose the fabric
Silence of snow
Silence of libraries
Silence after love
He cuts with scissors of the moon
He sews with a spider's thread
He delivers a perfect silence to you
A silence that fits you like a glove
A silence where you can finally hear
The sound your soul makes
When it breathes
And when it is happy
To be finally alone with itself.

---

## 210. The Station of Farewells

It is a station where trains never arrive
They only depart
The tracks are made of regrets
The carriages are full of ghosts
On the platform people do not kiss
They shake hands a little too tightly
Their smiles are masks of rain
The stationmaster is an old man
Who lost his own love here fifty years ago
He whistles the departure with a whistle of tears
The train goes away
Carrying with it halves of lives
And on the platform only silence remains
And the cold draft
Of someone who has just left
Forever.

---

## 211. The Clock That Ran Backwards

In an antique shop window
There was an old clock
Whose hands turned backward
People passed by and laughed
What a stupid clock
But an old man went inside
He bought the clock
He put it in his living room
And his life began to go back in time
His wife returned from the dead
His wrinkles disappeared
His hair turned black again
He relived his first kiss
His first heartbreak
His first day of school
He became a child again
Then a baby
Then nothing at all
The clock stopped
It had finished its work
It had erased a life
With all its joy and all its sorrow
And no one ever knew if it was a gift
Or a curse.

---

## 212. The Painter of the Invisible

He had his studio on the rooftops of Paris
He did not paint landscapes
He painted the invisible
On his canvases one could see
The smell of coffee in the morning
The sound of a heel on the cobblestones
The warmth of a sunbeam on the skin
The sadness of a distant song
The silence between two words of love
The art critics said
It looks like nothing
It is abstract
But simple people understood
A woman wept before the painting of solitude
A child laughed before the painting of a burst of laughter
For the painter did not paint what he saw
He painted what everyone feels
Without ever finding the words
Or the colors to say it.

---

## 213. The Library of Unlived Lives

It is a secret library in the heart of the city
The books have no title
They just have a name on the cover
Yours mine that of the neighbor
Each book tells the life you could have had
If you had said yes instead of no
If you had taken that train
If you had dared to kiss her
There are lives of an adventurer of a poet of a king
Lives of simple happiness in the countryside
People come here in secret
They read their other life with tears in their eyes
They see the happiness they missed
The person they could have become
Then they close the book
And return to their real life
A little sadder
And a little wiser
Wondering if somewhere
Someone is not reading
The life they are currently living.

---

## 214. The Meeting of the Street Names

The street of
Peace said
I am sick of military parades
The street of
Martyrs said
I would really like a little joy for a change
The street of
Commerce said
People only pass by they do not look at me anymore
The dead-end of
Desire said
No one ever comes to the very end
The square of
Holidays said
I am tired of speeches and fireworks
I would just like a little silence
So they decided to change their names
The street of
Peace was called the street of the
Kiss
The street of
Martyrs was called the street of
Laughter
And the dead-end of
Desire became the avenue of Mad
Love
People were lost
Their
GPS went crazy
They had to raise their heads
And ask for directions
And that is how
They began talking to each other again.

---

## 215. The Tamer of Nightmares

He has a little circus that only sets up at night
He does not tame lions
He tames nightmares
People bring him their worst fears
The fear of falling
The fear of being alone
The fear of death
He enters the cage of sleep
With his whip of light and his chair of courage
He faces the monsters of the unconscious
He speaks to them softly
He teaches them to be scary no more
He transforms the monster chasing you
Into a little dog that licks your hand
He transforms the void sucking you in
Into a large soft bed
At the end of the show
People leave with their tamed nightmares
Sleeping quietly at their feet
Like old household pets.

---

## 216. The Cat's Prayer

My master who is on earth
May your name be easy to ignore
May your reign be comfortable
May your will be done especially when it involves caresses
Give me today my daily nap
Forgive me my claws on the sofa
As I forgive you your useless noises
And lead me not into the temptation of the bath
But deliver me from the neighbor's dog
For to me belong the sun warmth and laziness
For the nine lives of the nine lives
Amen
And now let me sleep
Please.

---

## 217. The Ministry of Grayness

It is a large building without windows
Where faceless bureaucrats work
Their mission is to make the world grey
They forbid laughter that is too loud
They tax bright colors
They subsidize boredom
They organize yawning contests
The Minister of
Greyness
Is a man who has never seen the sea
He signs decrees with an invisible ink pen
His slogan is "A happy people is a dangerous people"
But one day an employee brought back a little red flower
He hid it in his drawer
And its scent began to spread
The scent of disobedience
And of joy
And the whole ministry began to smell of
Spring.

---

## 218. The Taste of Words

The word "freedom" tastes like wind on the sea
The word "love" tastes like warm bread and cherries
The word "sadness" tastes like rain on a windowpane
The word "hate" tastes like cold metal
The word "hope" has the fragile taste of a bud in winter
Politicians serve us tasteless words
Plastic words
Words that taste of nothing
They make us eat wooden language
The poet himself is a cook
He chooses his words with care
He mixes them lets them simmer
And he serves us a simple and true dish
A dish that tastes of life
With all its salt and all its sugar
And sometimes
All its bitterness.

---

## 219. The Thief of Horizons

He would arrive at night with large fog scissors
And he cut out the horizon
He folded it carefully
And carried it away in his sack
In the morning people woke up
And the sky touched the earth
There was no far away anymore
No elsewhere
The world was a little box with no way out
Birds flew in circles
Boats spun around in place
Dreams died of claustrophobia
People stopped looking out the window
They looked at their feet
And they found that life was very small
Until the day a child took a blue pencil
And drew a line
A simple line between the earth and the sky
And the horizon returned
And the thief died of hunger
For he fed only on lost hopes.

---

## 220. The Song of the Drying Laundry

On the line in the courtyard
The drying laundry tells stories
The worker's shirt tells of sweat and fatigue
The lover's dress tells of stolen kisses
The bedsheet tells of dreams and nightmares
The child's little sock tells of games and wild runs
The old lady's handkerchief tells of tears and memories
The wind passes by and reads all these stories
It mixes them up
And makes a great song of them
The song of ordinary life
A simple and complicated song
A song that smells good of laundry
And of passing time
Drying in the sun
Like all of us.

---

## 221. One Last Poem

I will write one last poem
A poem so light
That it will fly off the paper
A poem so transparent
That you will not see the words
Just what they hide
A poem that will say nothing
But will make you feel everything
It will have the smell of rain after the storm
The sound of silence
The color of a happy memory
You will read it
And five minutes later
You will have forgotten it
But it will remain in you
Like a little warmth in the pit of your stomach
Like a little light behind your eyes
And it will be my most beautiful poem
The one that does not need memory
To go on living.

---

## 222. The Maker of Tears

In his workshop smelling of salt and rain
He makes tears to order
He has crocodile tears for politicians
Tears of joy for weddings
Tears of rage for rebels
Little tears of nothing at all for sad movies
He distills them with care
From regrets memories and onions
He puts them in small vials
Each tear is unique
It contains a story
A sorrow or a joy
He is the only artisan of his guild
For everyone knows how to make their own tears
But no one wants to admit
That they need them
So people come to see him in secret
Like going to a poison merchant
Or a seller of remedies.

---

## 223. The Wall That Spoke

It was an old wall in an alley
Full of graffiti cracks and moss
One day it began to speak
In a voice hoarse and full of dust
It told the story of the street
Stolen kisses in its shadow
Conspiracies whispered against it
Children who played ball and grew old
It told of wars revolutions holidays
It knew everything
It had seen everything
People came from far away to listen to it
The mayor wanted to class it as a historic monument
But the wall said
Leave me alone
I am not a monument
I am just a memory
And one day I will collapse
With all my secrets
And it will be just fine that way.

---

## 224. The Commas' Strike

The commas had had enough
Tired of separating tired of making pauses
Tired of being the little maids-of-all-work of the sentence
So they left
All the commas of the world disappeared
It was a terrible chaos
Sentences had no breath left
Ideas collided
Lists became single and monstrous words
Lovers no longer knew where to stop to breathe in their letters
Politicians could no longer catch their breath to lie better
The world had become one single long incomprehensible sentence
People begged the commas to return
They promised to respect them
To give them importance
The commas returned
And the world could breathe again
And take a pause
To think.

---

## 225. The Merchant of Spare Skies

He pushes a little cart full of skies
There are skies for every taste
A storm sky for days of anger
A pink and tender sky for lovers
A sky full of stars for sleepless nights
A grey and low sky for when you want to stay at home
A sky of perfect blue that makes you want to live
People buy a sky from him
And paste it above their heads
It doesn't change the weather outside
But it changes the weather in the heart
The merchant does not sell weather reports
He sells states of mind
He is the official supplier
Of the color of our days.

---

## 226. The Loneliness of the Number One

The digit
One is always alone
At the beginning of everything
In front of the others
He is the first cry the first step
He is the beginning of solitude
The
Two has someone to talk to
The
Three can have a party
But the
One is alone
He watches the other digits having fun together
Forming complicated numbers
Additions multiplications
As for him he remains
One
Indivisible and straight
Like a soldier standing at attention
Like a telegraph pole in an empty field
Sometimes he dreams of being a
Zero
To be able to disappear
Or stand next to another digit
To finally count for ten.

---

## 227. The Dust Ball

In a sunbeam crossing a dark room
The dust motes dance
Each grain of dust is a world
A piece of dead skin
A grain of pollen
A fiber of clothing
A cigarette ash
A dried tear
They dance a silent and eternal waltz
They tell the story of the house
The story of the people who lived there
They are the invisible memory of our lives
We vacuum
We make them disappear
But they always return
For you cannot get rid of your past
You can only watch it dance
In the light.

---

## 228. The Walker Through Walls of Feeling

People build walls around their hearts
Walls of fear
Walls of pride
Walls of silence
As for him he has a strange gift
He can pass through these walls
He enters people's hearts without making a sound
He sees the sadness hidden behind a smile
The love that does not dare to speak
The childhood wound that never healed
He steals nothing
He changes nothing
He only watches
And when he leaves
He leaves the wall's door ajar
Just a little
So that the air can enter
And maybe one day
Someone else.

---

## 229. The Revolt of Reflections

One morning your reflection in the mirror
Got tired of imitating you
When you raised your right hand
It raised its left hand
When you smiled
It made a face
When you wanted to shave
It put on lipstick
You were afraid
You broke the mirror
But all the pieces on the ground
Continued to show you another you
A you who no longer obeyed
A you who was free
You spent the day locked in the dark
For fear of seeing yourself in a shop window
Or in a puddle of water
And you understood
That your worst enemy
Your most ferocious jailer
Was yourself.

---

## 230. The Fisher of Stars

He does not fish for fish
He fishes for stars
At night he gets into his boat
And he oars on the great black lake of the sky
He casts his net of silence
And he waits
Sometimes he catches a little shooting star
Which struggles and which he releases at once
Sometimes he catches an old tired moon
Which he helps to fall back asleep
But what he seeks
Is the shepherd's star
The star of lovers and poets
One day he will catch it
And he will put it in a lantern
To light the way
For all those who are lost
In the night of their own lives.

---

## 231. The Clinic for Sick Words

It is a very special clinic
There they treat sick words
Words worn out by politicians
Words tortured by advertisers
Words emptied of meaning by habit
The word "always" is in depression
The word "love" suffers from inflation
The word "freedom" is in intensive care
The nurses are poets
They make dressings of silence
Infusions of new meaning
Massages of unexpected rhymes
They give back to the words their color
Their weight their music
And when a word is cured
They release it into the world
Hoping it will not fall sick again
Too quickly.

---

## 232. Breath on the Windowpane

A child is bored on the bus
It is cold outside
He presses his mouth to the window
And makes fog
A little ephemeral cloud
On this cloud with his finger
He draws a sun
A heart
The name of his secret love
Then the fog disappears
The drawing fades away
The sun the heart the name
Everything is gone
The child is not sad
He knows that the most beautiful things
Are those that do not last
Those that exist just for an instant
In the warm breath of life
Against the cold windowpane of the world.

---

## 233. The Catalog of Useless Things

A mismatched button at the bottom of a sewing box
A rusty key that no longer opens any door
A movie ticket for a film that everyone has forgotten
A dried flower between the pages of a book
A pebble picked up on a distant beach
A song hummed without knowing the lyrics
The list is long
The list of useless things
That we keep without knowing why
Perhaps because they are the true treasures
The silent guardians of our memories
The little white pebbles of our lost path
Perhaps the useless
Is just another way of saying
Essential.

---

## 234. The Sadness of the Shop-Window Mannequin

I am made of plastic
I am perfect
My smile is frozen for eternity
I wear the most beautiful dresses
I never grow old
I am the dream of all women
But at night when the store is closed
I am sad
I see people passing in the street
I see them laughing crying kissing arguing
I see them living
As for me I do not live
I pose
I am a beautiful empty thing
A promise that will never be kept
My only desire
Would be to have a single wrinkle
A single tear
A single imperfection
Which would prove that I existed
Truly.

---

## 235. The Speech of the Commander's Statue

Yes it is me the
Commander
The one whom
Don
Juan invited to dinner
Everyone says that I am the symbol of divine justice
The punishment of the sinner
What a joke
The truth is that I was sick of it
Sick of being on my pedestal
Sick of being a cold and dignified statue
I saw
Don
Juan living
Loving seducing laughing
I was jealous
So yes I came to his dinner
Not to punish him
But to feel the warmth of a fire
To drink a glass of wine
To listen to music
I dragged him to hell
Because it was the only place
Where a stone statue could finally
Feel something
Even the burning.

---

## 236. The Ghosts' Afternoon Tea

At four o'clock in the afternoon
In the attic of abandoned houses
The ghosts have their afternoon snack
They do not eat cakes
They eat memories
They bite into a burst of a child's laughter
They spread a slice of nostalgia
They drink cups of silence
Their conversations are made of whispers
Of regrets and old songs
They talk of the good old days
When they still had a body
When they could still cry for real
Then the sun sets
And the ghosts return to their solitude
Leaving on the table
A few crumbs of what has been
And what will never be again.

---

## 237. The Anger of a Drop of Water

I was born in a clean cloud
I was pure and full of light
I dreamed of falling on a flower
Or in the hand of a child
But I fell on the city
I rolled on a roof full of soot
In a gutter full of spit
I joined a river that smelled of poison
I saw dead fish belly up
I carried factory waste
I am no longer a drop of water
I am a drop of anger
A drop of shame
And when I evaporate
I will go up to the sky
And I will form a black cloud
A cloud of vengeance
And I will rain down on men
An acid rain
A rain that will remind them
Of what they have done to me
And to the world.

---

## 238. The Merchant of Sleep

He spends the night in silent streets
With his suitcase full of sleep
He has light sleep for those who fear the dark
Deep sleep for those who want to forget everything
Restless sleep for lovers
Childlike sleep for old men
He sells his sleep by the spoonful
To insomniacs staring at the ceiling
To the anxious listening to their hearts beating too fast
He takes no money
He takes in exchange a little piece of their day
A worry a joy a secret
With that he fills his suitcase
For sleep feeds on life
And the merchant knows
That he is the most essential of shopkeepers
The only one selling what money
Will never be able to buy.

---

## 239. The Dance of the Lost Keys

At night in the large warehouse of lost and found
The lost keys organize a ball
There is the key to the childhood home
The key to the first car
The key to the heart of a forgotten love
The key to a cage from which the bird has flown
They clink softly against each other
A music of metal and nostalgia
They dance a rusty jig
They tell each other of the doors they opened
The secrets they guarded
They are not sad
They know that a lost key
Is not the end of a story
It is just the beginning of another
That of the door
Which will remain forever
Open or closed.

---

## 240. The Café's Last Customer

The waiter puts the chairs on the tables
It makes a sound of climbing skeletons
The coffee machine sighs one last time
Outside night has eaten everything
There remains a man alone at a table
He looks at the bottom of his glass
As if he were looking for an answer there
He thinks of nothing
He is just there
In the silence that settles in
In the smell of cold coffee and cigarettes
He is the last survivor of the day
The guardian of a moment that no longer exists
The waiter waits
He says nothing
He knows you do not disturb
Someone who is becoming
A memory.

---

## 241. A Poet's Testament

When I am dead
Do not put my name on a stone
Let the wind carry it away
I leave you neither gold nor silver
I leave you the red of poppies
The blue of the sky on very windy days
I leave you the sound of rain on rooftops
The smile of a stranger in the street
I leave you the words I tried to bend
To make them say a little truth
Take them use them discard them
Do what you want with them
My only heritage
Is to have tried to show you
How to look at the world
With eyes that have just been born
Every morning.

---

## 242. Blue, the Color of Dreams

I am not a color that screams
I am a great murmur, a sigh
The color of that which is far away
The horizon, the sea, and the vein on a hand
I am the color of escape
The immense sky that belongs to no one
And where the birds draw poems of freedom
I am the deep ocean full of voyages and monsters
The liquid road to unknown islands
I am the uniform of the sailor who is leaving
And the color of the eyes in which one drowns in love
I am the color of dreams and of hope
The blue smoke of a wood fire rising to the stars
The ink of the letter one is waiting for
And the little flame of the gas in the silent night
But I am also the color of sorrow
The "blues" of the black musician in a Chicago bar
The color of lips when one is cold
The terrible blue of a bruise on the skin and on the soul
I am the color of the work uniform
The "bleu de travail" that smells of sweat and fatigue
The faded blue of a life spent in a factory
Looking at the sky through a dirty window
I am the greatest of paradoxes
The color of the cage and of freedom
I am the blue of the worker's overalls
And the blue of the sky he looks at while dreaming
I am the distance between what one is
And what one would like to be
I am the color of the human soul
A great sea of desires and of melancholy.

---

## 243. Orange, the Color of Twilight

I am not the scream of red
Nor the great laughter of yellow
I am their friendly conversation
A kind color that doesn't want to make a scene
I am the warmth of a wood fire in the fireplace
I am the color of simple and good things
The carrot that one pulls from the earth
The apricot full of sun
And the orange, that little sun to be peeled
Full of a sweet juice that sticks to the fingers
I am the round belly of the pumpkin
Waiting quietly for autumn in its field
And the color of the fox disappearing into the woods
I am the color of the end of the day
The great magnificent farewell of the sun
Its golden wound on the belly of the sky
I am the light of the little lamp on the table
When the night is already behind the window
I am a promise of warmth and of refuge
But I am also the color of warning
The flashing light of the road workers
The plastic cone that says "Caution, danger"
The jacket of the garbage collector that makes him visible in the night
I am the color that forewarns
The color of prudence and of change
For I am above all the color of autumn
The sad beauty of the leaf that is about to fall
The last warmth before the great cold
I am not an eternal color
I am the color of a moment
A perfect and fragile moment
Between fullness and the end
I am the color of a friendly "goodbye for now"
Never that of a "farewell".

---

## 244. Green, the Color of Hope

I am not a hurried color
I am patience, I am perseverance
The little blade of grass that grows through the concrete
The ivy that slowly climbs the wall of time
I am the great quiet breath of the forests
I am the color of hope
The first bud on the dead tree of winter
The four-leaf clover found by a child
The green light at the crossroads that says "Go, live your life"
I am the fresh mint in a glass of water
The little motionless lizard on the warm stone
And the deep green of a mountain lake
I am the repose of the eye and the calm of the heart
The promise that even after the harshest of winters
Life always returns
But men have invented another life for me
Another color
They took my name to put it on their money
That paper that buys everything except happiness
They put me on the uniforms of their soldiers
And on the gambling table where fortunes are lost
I have become the color of jealousy
The murky green of envy in the eyes
The color of absinthe that drives one mad
And of the mold on forgotten bread
They have created a green that is the enemy of life
A green of poison, a green of dollars, a green of profit
A green that builds walls
While my own true green
Only dreams of covering them
So look closely
There are two greens in the world
The one that liberates and the one that imprisons
And every day you must choose
Between the grass in the park where you can lie down to do nothing
And the banknote you must earn to survive.

---

## 245. Red, the Color of the Heart

After the silence of white and black
I arrive making noise
I am the first color, the great scream
The color that even the blind can feel
The warmth of blood on the skin
I am the color of life and of desire
The cherry that one steals from the tree
The lips of a woman waiting for a kiss
The fragile and stubborn poppy in the wheat field
The heart drawn in chalk on a gray wall
I am the wine of lovers and the blush of shyness
The blood that runs in the veins
The proof that one is alive
But I am also the color of anger and of war
The fire that devours the house
The blood that flows on the pavement
The face of an angry man
The teacher's pen that crosses out mistakes
The stop sign that kills movement
And the flag of the revolution
That promises a better world
With the blood of others
I am a paradox, a wound and a flower
The most joyful and the most terrible of colors
I am the velvet curtain of the theater
And the stain on the butcher's apron
I am the candy apple of the funfair
And the light of the brothel in the night
You cannot ignore me
I am the color of all passions
From love to hate there is only one step
And that step is always red
A world without me would be a reasonable world
A gray and polite world
A deathly boring world.

---

## 246. Yellow, the Color of Laughter

I am the color of laughter and of the sun
The not-so-rich cousin of gold
I am simple joy, a color without manners
That bursts forth in the middle of the serious green of the meadow
A million dandelions applauding the spring
I am the sharp and sour taste of the lemon
The heat of mustard that rushes to the nose
The liquid heart of a fried egg
I am the naive sunflower that follows its god the sun
And the raincoat of a child jumping in puddles
I am a party, a surprise, an exclamation
But you must not trust my smile
I am also a color that lies
The color of sickness on a man's skin
The yellow and feverish light of a tired lightbulb
The pages of an old book that are slowly dying
I am the color of betrayal and of cowardice
At the crossroads I am the middle light
The one that is neither yes nor no
The one that says "Careful, maybe danger"
I am the desert sand where one dies of thirst
And the fake gold of a cheap ring
That leaves a green trace on the finger
I am joy, but a joy that knows it does not last
The beauty of summer just before autumn
A laughter that is on the verge of tears
I am the most cheerful and the most unsettling of colors
A flash of light
Before the shadow comes to take it all back.

---
