For all the ones the children
never meant to let go
his flies off
up into the dark
to catch the others

Up there by now there must be
a whole flock of them
red yellow blue
full of the crying of children
that turns up there into stars

and somebody has to look after them
so why not him
the shepherd of runaway balloons
who every evening
adds one more to the flock

---

## 344. THE DENTIST

The dentist looks into mouths all day long
open mouths
mouths full of fear
and he knows the pain
before the x-ray shows it

he knows it by the eyes in the waiting room
by the hands that clutch the handbag
by the foot that taps against the floor
open wide please
and talk to me

all day he looks into the pain
he drills it fills it pulls it out
and the pain goes home cured
home to its lunch
home to its children

but there is one pain
his surgery cannot cure
it arrives with healthy teeth
it is an old woman
she comes every month

she sits down opens her mouth
and he looks
your teeth are fine madam
teeth like porcelain
there's nothing wrong

but she stays sitting
in the chair as if in church
so he says
open your mouth
and talk to me

and she talks
of the husband who left by the door
of the son who left by the train
of the cat who left by the window
and he holds the little mirror and listens

the worst pains in his surgery
have healthy teeth
and the only cure is
open your mouth madam
and talk to me

---

## 345. THE LITTLE TREE IN JANUARY

In January the trees lie by the bins
on the pavement on the snow
still with their silver threads
still with one forgotten bauble
looking out at the street like an eye

a week ago they were family
standing warm in the living room
they shone they smelled sweet they held the gifts
the children danced around them
and said our little tree our own

now they lie by the bins
and the children walk to school
and wave to them like old friends
bye little tree bye
the way you wave to an uncle at the station

the trees do not wave
the trees lie
shedding needles along the pavement
small green footsteps
the way Christmas walked out

the bin men come on Tuesday
and carry off the whole of December
loaded in a single heap
the silver threads and all the rest
into the big dark lorry

but one neighbour
had bought a tree with its roots
in a pot like a dog
and Christmas never choked it in a stand
it simply lived by the window

and in January he took a spade
and planted it in the yard
the only man on the street
who for the holidays had invited not a guest
but a neighbour

---

## 346. THE DRIVING INSTRUCTOR

All day he teaches strangers to drive
into first gear
let the clutch out slowly
mirror signal and off we go
all day the same

he taught the butcher's daughter to reverse
he taught the baker to brake
he taught the barber to turn left
he taught the whole town to park
he taught everyone every road

he sits on the right with his second pedal
his hand near the handbrake
and a little nervous he says
easy now just easy
you've almost got it

down at the crossing a policeman waves
and greets him like an old friend
because he taught him twenty years ago
that policeman too
and the one who drives the ambulance now

the whole town knows him at the wheel
the whole town he has carried
hand to hand year to year
the tram driver the bus driver the cabbie
all of them his pupils

in the evening he parks the school car
locks it checks the handbrake
and walks home
and they all honk and wave at him
maestro get in we'll give you a lift

he shakes his head he smiles
and goes on foot
because he has no car himself
he never had one
whoever taught the town to drive
knows best how the town drives

---

## 347. THE PROMPTER

All her life she whispers
from the little box below the footlights
hidden under the boards of the stage
only her head between two worlds
a voice without a face

she knows every part in every play
Juliet and Hamlet
the king and the beggar
she knows the first line and the last
and above all the one the actor forgot

the actors stand in the light
in costume and in applause
and beneath them she whispers
the next word please the next word
and they speak it as their own

no one has ever seen her
no one claps for the prompter's box
the programmes do not name her
she plays in a murmur madam from nowhere
all her life in the third person

she loves the ones who do not quite hear her
and saves them every evening
when they fall silent and fear the silence
she is always there
prompting them to go on living

and at her last curtain call
the old actors stand in a row
and one of them turns
points his hand down to the prompter's box
and the spotlight drops into it for the first time

and there she sits dazzled
seen for the first time after so many years
a whole career in a whisper
so she takes a bow
and whispers to the audience thank you

---

## 348. THE WELL

In the middle of the square stands a well
and on it hangs a sign
WATER NOT DRINKABLE
and across the bucket a grate
and across the grate a lock

the council closed it
with a stamp and a by-law
the well is forbidden
the well is dangerous
the well is officially dead

but people still come
and slip their fingers through the grate
and drop a coin down
and over this water no one may drink
they whisper their wishes

let him come back
let her get well
let him say yes to me
let the children write from abroad
let this at last pass over

the coins fall into the dark
they chime on the stone and on each other
a small rain of copper and silver
wish after wish year after year
at the bottom a whole town piles up

and one day the council comes
with a stamp and a pail
to remove that improper water
and hauls up the bucket full
of coins instead of water

and counts them from the bottom up
and finds there is just enough
exactly enough to mend the well
so it pays for a new pump
out of the wishes it had banned

a wish cannot be forbidden
not by a sign nor by a lock
it falls into the dark patiently
and in the end
it saves itself

---

## 349. THE TWO DOORBELLS

On the flat's door two bells
one new and white
NOVAK in big letters
and the other beside it
faded past reading

the new tenant moved in
with his boxes and his bed
and he saw the other bell
that name that is no longer a name
only a grey shadow where the letters were

he could have unscrewed it
he could have pasted his own over it
he could have changed the whole plate
but he left it there
not even knowing why

and people rang for NOVAK
friends and the neighbour and the postman
and he opened and he laughed
and the other bell stayed silent
a bell for no one

until one day the postman rang
holding a letter in his hand
and reading the faded name
hesitant syllable by syllable
does he still live here?

and the new tenant looked
at the envelope and at that name
he could have said it's a mistake
he could have said he doesn't live here anymore
and shut the door

but he said yes
give it to me
I'll take it
whoever inherits an address
inherits its past as well

and someone must take in the mail
even for those who have gone
even for those who no longer have a bell
that anyone could ring
only a faded name at the door

---

## 350. THE SWEATERS

The grandmother knits sweaters
by the window by the lamp
stitch after stitch row after row
she knits for her grandchildren abroad
last seen when they were small

she knits a green one for Tony
a blue one for Annie
and into each she knits
stay warm don't you catch cold
write and tell me how you are

then she wraps the sweater in paper
ties it with string
writes the address in big letters
and the post carries it across the borders
across the mountains across that whole piece of world

but the grandchildren grow
they grow faster than she knits
while the needles click
the boys shoot up a hand's width
the girls a whole sleeve

and so the sweaters always come
a size too small
the sleeves too short the collar tight
the grandmother had counted last year's child
and this year's child arrived

and the parents say never mind
next time we'll send her the measurements
next time let her knit a size up
and the children squeeze into the tight wool
and grumble and laugh

but they wear them all the same
tugging at the too-short sleeves
because a grandmother's warmth
should be felt right where it pinches
and what is a little too small
is worn closer to the heart

---

## 351. THE STATION CLOCK

Under the great station clock
people wait
they wait for loves
they wait for trains
they wait for homecomings

they stand and look up
at the big white face with its hands
when now when now when now
and the clock goes on
the clock alone does not wait

a man waits for a woman with a suitcase
a woman waits for the man who promised
a mother waits for her son from the army
and they all look up
at the clock instead of at each other

the hand jumps and the heart jumps with it
four minutes more
two more
one more
and under the clock two hundred solitudes

the clock knows exactly what time it is
how much delay how much life
how much waiting in a single station
it ticks above the heads
indifferent and precise

and one day it stops
it simply stops at half past two
and before the stationmaster comes with his ladder
the people below no longer know the time
so for the first time they look down

and for the first time they look at each other
at that woman beside them that man
who has been waiting here just as long
and they say are you waiting too?
and time had stopped just in time

and two waitings met
under the stopped clock
and when the stationmaster set it going again
it was already too late
they were already looking at each other

---

## 352. THE SWIFTS

The building firm is to repair the school
the façade the gutters the plaster
the crane has come and the cement mixer
the workmen have come with their helmets
and the papers with every permit

but up there under the cornice
where the new plaster was meant to go
there are nests
small mud nests of swifts
and inside them summer is peeping

and so the work stops
because the law is the law
and the swift is protected
and the work must wait
until the young ones have flown

the crane stands with its hook in the air
the mixer is silent the scaffold waits
and the workmen sit on the kerb
eating their lunch and looking up
when now when now will they fly

the site foreman has a deadline
he has a fine for every day
he has a phone that keeps ringing
where is the school where is it finished
and he holds the binoculars and looks

and one morning it happens
a young swift climbs to the edge
tries its wings is afraid for a second
and then throws itself into the air
and does not fall and flies

and the workmen on the kerb applaud
and the foreman writes in his logbook
delay six weeks
reason the swifts
the most beautiful delay in the history of the town

the concrete can wait
the wings cannot
and the only deadline you must never miss
is the one when someone very small
above your head must learn to fly

---

## 353. THE SILENCE IN THE LIBRARY

On the library door hangs a sign
SILENCE PLEASE
and the woman at the desk puts a finger to her lips
and everyone walks on tiptoe
as in a room where someone is sleeping

but it is a mistake
it is the loudest place in town
louder than the square on market day
louder than the station
only it is heard with the eyes

here a thousand voices speak at once
poets dead a hundred years speak
kings and murderers speak
lovers long since parted speak
and complain and laugh and cry out

you open a book and the talking begins
here is the storm here is the war
here someone whispers I love you
here a ship goes down and no one hears
except you with your finger on the line

the woman at the desk knows it best
that is why she makes this silence
not so that people should be quiet
but so that those may be heard
who can no longer speak any other way

hush she says hush
not that she wants silence
she wants everyone to be able to speak
the dead and the living all at once
and that is only possible in silence

and so the library is full of uproar
full of singing and weeping and laughter
under the sign that says SILENCE PLEASE
and silence is not the ban on speaking
silence is the condition
so that at last everyone can

---

## 354. THE PIANO AT AUCTION

In the auction hall a piano
an old piano
black as sorrow
the auctioneer calls three thousand
who says three thousand?
no one
two thousand
no one
one thousand
people look down at their catalogs
five hundred
no hand goes up
the auctioneer wipes his forehead
one hundred
no one
then for the hauling away
he says tired
who will take it off our hands?

at the back a hand goes up
the big hand of a mover
I'll take it
this man carried pianos all his life
up the stairs
down the stairs
to the fifth floor with no elevator
and never once touched a single key

he brings it home
he sets it by the window
and in the evening
his little daughter lifts the lid
and plays her first song

now this piano is beyond price
the price stayed in the hall
the worth came home
and the two of them meet
only when the little girl plays

---

## 355. THE PHONE BOOK

An old man keeps hidden
the last phone book of the town
a thick book of names
every evening he leafs through it
under the lamp
his finger down the lines
Miller Newman Baker
here lived Emil
here sang Marie
here Mister Weiss kept his shop

half the names no longer live
the numbers no longer exist
you call and a voice answers
this number has been disconnected
or no one answers at all
just long empty tones

his neighbor tells him
throw it out
it's only old paper
who calls a phone book these days?

but the old man won't throw it out
it's the only book
where they are all together
the living and the dead
on the same page
arranged in alphabetical order

an address book and a graveyard in one
he says to himself
and turns another page
and keeps on calling
not by telephone
by memory
hello Emil can you hear me?
and Emil picks up every time

---

## 356. THE BET ON THE RAIN

Two farmers all the dry summer
stand at the fence and argue
when the rain will come
Thursday says the first
no Sunday says the second
and they bet a beer
one single beer
that's the whole stake

the field cracks with thirst
the corn has dropped its leaves
the well only rasps now
and every day they meet again
so is it coming that rain?
we'll see
and they look at the sky
the way you look at a debtor

and one morning
with no warning
no Thursday no Sunday
the rain arrives
and it pours
it pours on them both the same
on both fields
because the rain doesn't read bets
and knows no fence between them

they stand under the same gutter
soaked and happy
so who won?
both of them
who lost?
both of them
and they drink that beer together
because rain never belongs to just one

---

## 357. THE HOLE IN THE PHOTOGRAPH

The granddaughter leafs through the album
old photographs
weddings name days Sunday lunches
and suddenly
in the middle of one picture
a hole
someone was cut out with scissors

grandma who was missing here?
grandma says nothing
she only waves her hand
someone we don't want anymore

but the hole stayed
the hole has the shape of a man
the shoulders
the head
an arm laid around someone
the scissors cut out the face
the place they cannot cut out

and it's strange
the whole family in the photo is smiling
the uncle the aunt the cousins
and no one remembers them
it's the hole they remember

because scissors do not know how to forget
you cut out a face
and exactly that man remains
only empty
and everyone looks
at the spot where he stood

grandma and do you miss him?
grandma is silent a long time
and then she says
every day
I reach him my hand through that hole

---

## 358. THE ANCHOR

The tavern THE ANCHOR
stands far inland
a hundred kilometers from the sea
the nearest water is the village pond
on the wall hangs a ship's wheel
and an old net
and a lighthouse painted on a napkin

people come here
who have never seen the sea
and speak of it like sailors
today the wind's out of the southwest
there'll be a storm
says the baker
who never once left the county

and they drink rum
and they sing of harbors
and the mast here is the broom behind the door
and the sea
the sea is under the tables
in the talk
in the singing
in the way they look out the window

hey captain where do we sail tonight?
where it's deeper
answers the landlord
and pulls another beer

the sea is there
where it is spoken of
the anchor holds the tavern
so it won't drift away
and the tavern holds the dreams
so they won't drown

---

## 359. THE BROKEN RADIO

Grandfather's old radio
picks up only one station now
the others went dark one by one
you turn the knob
and nothing
only that one is left

it's all the same everywhere anyway
says grandfather
and waves his hand
songs commercials weather
tomorrow like today
and today like yesterday

he sits by it every morning
listening to that one station
like an old friend
who never moved away

and then one day they shut it down
they sell the frequency
and out of the radio comes
a hiss
only a hiss
a shoreless sea of hiss

the neighbor says
come on throw it out
it doesn't play anymore

but grandfather goes on sitting
and listening
now it's playing the sea
he says
and he smiles
the hiss is the last station
that runs no commercials
and plays all the time
and for everyone
and for free

---

## 360. THE KEYHOLE

The child kneels at the door
of the locked room
and looks through the keyhole
inside the grown-ups are preparing something
paper rustles
glass clinks
they whisper
hush so he won't hear

what are they doing in there?
the child can't see the whole room
he sees only a slice
a piece of table
a hand with a ribbon
and a shadow on the wall

the grown-ups lock the doors
and carry the keys in their pockets
heavy keys on a ring
the key is for hands
the hole is for eyes
and eyes cannot be locked

you can lock a room
but not curiosity
you can lock a gift
but not the waiting
you can lock a secret
but not that small eye at the hole

and do you know what the child sees?
he sees his birthday
wrapped up in tomorrow
and he pretends he knows nothing
because the finest gift
is the one
you were allowed to see in secret
and can still marvel at all the same

---

## 361. THE SEED IN THE POCKET

In spring the woman puts on
her winter coat one last time
before hanging it in the wardrobe
and in the pocket
among crumbs and a tram ticket
she finds a seed
a small hard seed
and can't remember where it's from

where was I then?
in the park? by the river?
in someone's garden?
the coat says nothing
the pocket says nothing
the seed says nothing either

so she plants it in a pot
on the balcony
she waters it
and she waits
and one morning
a green leaf grows
then a second
then a flower

and it's a flower
that no one knows
not her
not the neighbor
not the gardener on the corner
what kind of flower is that?
nobody knows

we carry whole gardens in our pockets
and never know it
every old coat
is a secret flowerpot
and what we have forgotten
blooms on its own in spring
and asks
do you know me?

---

## 362. THE SWALLOWS ON THE WIRES

Before the great departure
the swallows settle on the wires
five electric wires
like the five lines of a stave
and each swallow
is a note

below them stands the composer
notebook in hand
and copies them down
this one's a quarter note
that one a half
the one up top is a high C
and the one that flew off
was a rest

people pass by
what are you writing there?
they ask
and he points to the sky
notes
sitting on the wires
you only have to copy them

and then comes the north wind
and the swallows lift
the whole score at once
and fly away to Africa
and the wires are left empty
five empty lines

but the melody has stayed
in his notebook
because music
cannot be forbidden to leave
and he knows
that in spring
they will settle differently
and a new movement will begin

---

## 363. THE MAN WHO SAID HELLO

In the town where people don't greet each other
there's a man
who greets everyone
good day madam
good day sir
good morning
lovely day isn't it?

a madman people say
and they hurry on
eyes to the ground
collar turned up
who greets anyone these days?

some out of politeness answer
softly
almost against their will
good day
and at once they're ashamed
of that smile
that slipped out of them

he goes on greeting
for years
every morning at his corner
by the bakery
good day good day good day
the way you sow
even if nothing grows

and then one day
he's no longer there
and people pass by
and don't know why
but they greet each other
good day
at his corner
by the bakery
out of a habit
that one madman began

politeness is contagious
it needs only one
patient carrier

---

## 364. THE LAST POEM

The poet has to write a poem
and nothing comes to him
he looks out the window
a cloud drifts to the right
then another to the left
he tidies the desk
lines up the pencils by size
feeds the cat
the cat writes no poems
the cat sleeps

he makes coffee
counts the pigeons on the sill
one two three
and the fifth flies off
before he finishes counting
he waters the plant
turns the calendar page
sharpens a pencil
he won't use anyway

and still nothing
an empty page
white as winter
poet where is your poem?
tomorrow he says
tomorrow for sure

and in the evening
when he blows out the lamp
he finds
that the poem was about all of this
about the cloud to the right
about the coffee
about the cat that sleeps
about the fifth pigeon
that left too soon

poems are not written
poems happen
when you know how to wait
and that is how this one came to be

---

## 365. THE LAST PENCIL

In the case full of pens
lies one last pencil
wooden
chewed
quiet

The pens gleam
the pens have colours
the pens are sure of themselves
the pen never hesitates
the pen writes straight in fair copy

The tablet lights up
the tablet crosses nothing out
the tablet remembers everything
every mistake
forever

And the pencil
the pencil is short
the pencil grows shorter still
the pencil is ashamed

But when the child slips
when a word comes out wrong
he reaches for the pencil
always the pencil

Because the pen announces
here is where you were wrong
and leaves it standing for all time

Whereas the pencil says nothing
the pencil turns around
and with its other end
gently
takes the error away

The last pencil in the classroom
is the only one
allowed to be wrong

---

## 366. THE KEY WITHOUT A LOCK

In the drawer among the buttons
and the old stamps
lies a key
heavy
brass
alone

The lock is long gone
the house is long gone
the door is long gone
and the street
and the town too perhaps

People say
throw it out
it is good for nothing
it unlocks nothing
it locks nothing
it shuts nothing

But a hand takes it up
a hand weighs it
a hand puts it back
in the drawer
among the buttons

Because this key
does not open a door

this key
opens the doors that used to be

the kitchen where they laughed
the threshold the bride was carried across
the creak in the night of coming home late

The key is not for unlocking
the key is for remembering

That is why you do not throw it away
the key to a house that has fallen
is the only key
that truly opens anything

---

## 367. THE LEAKY POT

The housewife has a pot
old
of tin
and in its side a little hole

Every morning to the well
every morning back again
and on the way home
drip
drip
drip

The neighbour says to her
why don't you throw it out?
you lose half the water
before you reach your door

The housewife says nothing
the housewife carries
the pot is ashamed
I am faulty
I am leaky
I bring back not even the half

And the housewife walks
and the water drips
and the pot weeps the whole way home

Until one summer
she showed it the path

Down its side of the road
flowers had bloomed
blue ones
white ones
yellow ones

on the other side
nothing but dust

You watered them
she told it
all that time
you spent ashamed of your hole

The pot does not know it is generous
it thinks it only leaks

---

## 368. THE UNFINISHED LETTER

In the drawer of the desk
there is a letter
half written
the same for years

My dear
it says
I wanted to tell you that

and then nothing
only blankness
only white
only half a sentence

The man takes it out now and then
the man reads it over
I wanted to tell you that
and he lifts the pen
and he sets the pen down

Tomorrow
he says to himself
tomorrow I'll finish it

And the drawer closes
and the year closes
and another year

His friends advise him
finish it
send it
or throw it away

But he knows what he knows

Finished letters
lie

they claim a man has said it all
that he already knows
that he has understood the whole of it
and set the final stop

His stayed true
half-done
like him
I wanted to tell you that
and he still does not know what

---

## 369. THE OLD CHAIR BY THE ROAD

Someone carried out a chair
and set it by the road
beside the bus stop
FOR COLLECTION

One leg shorter
the seat worn hollow
the back missing a rung

It was a kitchen chair
the father sat on it
the mother sat on it
the morning sat on it over coffee
a whole life long

Now it belongs to no one

So the woman with the shopping bags sat down
waiting for the bus
the boy with the satchel sat down
the grandfather sat down
who can no longer stand for long

In the morning the bricklayer sits there
in the evening the lovers sit there
at night the rain sits there

The bin men came
they looked at it
and left it standing

It is still needed
they said

The chair never had so many guests
as now
now that it belongs to no one

They threw it out
and by that gave it
all the people in the world

---

## 370. THE STOPPED WATCH

A man wears on his wrist
a wristwatch
that is stopped
stopped for years
always at ten past ten

He does not wind it
he does not change the battery
he does not have it mended

The hands do not move
the small one is stopped
the big one is stopped
the second hand as well

And yet
when in the street they ask him
excuse me what time is it?

the man lifts his eyes
looks at the church tower
and answers
it is quarter to five
exactly right

Then why wear that watch
if it doesn't go?
they ask him

And the man covers his wrist with his hand

The time
he says
the towers give me that
there are plenty of them everywhere

This watch was given to me
by someone
who has no time left at all

I don't wear it for the hour
I wear it for the hand
that used to fasten it on

---

## 371. THE LAST COIN

It was a currency
that had ceased to be legal tender
a decree had said so
from midnight IT IS WORTHLESS

People carried the coins to the bank
people exchanged them
people forgot them

Only one remained
in the pocket of an old coat
the last coin
worn
rubbed smooth
round

Once it bought bread
once it bought milk
once it bought the cinema ticket
where they first sat side by side

Now it buys nothing
now it is only metal
now it is only a little disc

The man takes it in his palm
the man weighs it

And it weighs
more than before
much more

When it had value
it was light
it ran from hand to hand
barely warmed

Now that it is worthless
it has stayed
and grown heavy with remembering

They abolished its value by decree
its weight they did not
that no government abolishes

---

## 372. THE MIRROR IN THE JUNK SHOP

In the junk shop between the lamps
and the old clocks
hangs a mirror
in a cracked frame
tarnished at the edges

The dealer praises it
this mirror
madam
remembers

it remembers countesses
it remembers brides
it remembers faces
a hundred years of faces

Come and see
who ever stood before it

An old woman came
she wanted to see herself young
she looked
and saw an old woman

A soldier came
he wanted to see his father
he looked
and saw a soldier

A child came
it looked
and a child laughed back

The mirror showed
not a single face that had been
not one

it always showed
only the face
that stood before it now

And the dealer was mistaken
the mirror remembers nothing
nothing at all

that is why it is the only
honest historian in the world
it never lies about what was
because it remembers none of it

---

## 373. THE UMBRELLA TURNED INSIDE OUT

On the street corner
the wind caught an umbrella
and turned it inside out

The ribs stuck out
the cloth flung up
the owner swore

You useless umbrella
he said
you're good for nothing now
you don't shelter me any more
you don't fend off the rain

And he made to throw it in the bin
soaked
broken
inside out

But the rain kept falling
and the umbrella turned this way
suddenly did not fend off the water

it gathered it

Into that bowl of ribs and cloth
the rain came down
and the sky shone in it
birdsong rained down
a piece of cloud rained down
a child's mirror for paper boats rained down

Passers-by stopped
looked inside
as into a spring

All its life
it had driven the rain away
and no one had ever stopped beside it

It took only the gale to turn it inside out
and for the first time
it caught something

---

## 374. THE CEREMONIAL SCISSORS

They brought them on a velvet cushion
large
gilded
gleaming
the ceremonial scissors

The sign read
GRAND OPENING

A gentleman in a suit came
smiled at the cameras
took the scissors
and cut the ribbon

snip

Applause
bouquets
speeches

And then they wrapped the scissors
in silk
and shut them in a display case
behind glass
where it says DO NOT TOUCH

Ordinary scissors at home
cut all their lives
cloth
paper
hair
nails
the string on a parcel

They make dresses
cut out a picture
take up a pair of trousers

And these ceremonial ones
the most glorious scissors in the world
never sewed a thing

they cut one ribbon
and went into the cupboard

To open and to vanish
that is their whole biography

---

## 375. THE STATION DOG

Every day at noon
he comes to the station
sits down on the platform
between the suitcases and the tickets
and waits

The train whistles
the doors slide open
people embrace
people pass each other by
and the master does not come

The master did not come yesterday
the master did not come last year
the master has not come for so many winters
that even the benches
have forgotten his name

The stationmaster brings him bread
the old woman with her knitting strokes him
the conductor knows him by now
So little dog
nothing again?

But the dog does not wag his tail
for the master
the dog wags his tail
when the train blows its horn

He is not waiting for the master
that was only what we believed
he is waiting for the noon train
faithfulness is a habit
more beautiful than the reason it lost

---

## 376. THE CAT WHO CHOSE

One morning
she simply came
she sat down on the doorstep
as if she had lived there forever
and she stayed

No one had invited her
no one had bought her
no one carried her in a basket
she walked up the street
she nosed the gate open
and she decided

Here there will be the window
here there will be the cushion
here there will be that hand
that will stroke me
whenever I please

The man says that's my cat
the woman says that's our cat
the children say she belongs to us
and the cat half closes her eyes
and stays silent the way masters stay silent

For she belongs to no one
she has only permitted
the house to belong to her
and whoever thinks he owns a cat
has by the cat been filed
among the warm furniture

---

## 377. THE SPARROW AND THE STATUE

On the square stands a general
of bronze
his hand outstretched
somewhere ahead
toward a battle that is no more

He points to the assault
he points to the glory
he points the direction
that no one has taken in ages
and no one looks that way

A sparrow came
tiny and grey
he inspected that hand
weighed it the way you weigh a branch
and said to himself this will do

He carried a straw
he carried a tuft
he carried a feather of spring
and in the general's open palm
he built his nest

The passersby laugh
look soldier
your grand gesture
now holds four eggs
and for the first time it is good for something

All his life he pointed to the battle
now he holds a life
and glory is never so becoming
as when upon its head
a sparrow comes to sit

---

## 378. THE FISH WHO SAW THE SKY

He swam in the shoal
the way a shoal swims
to the left and the right
up and down
and always in the water

Water above
water below
water on the left
water on the right
water with no ceiling

One day he leapt
not knowing why
he jumped clear of the surface
and for a single heartbeat
he saw

Blue upon blue
light without water
a cloud white and silent
a sun that does not drip
and air you do not drink

Then he fell back
wet and dumb
and swam to the shoal
I saw the sky
I saw the sky he cried

The fish turned round
what sky
what is the sky
and they swam on left and right
Each of us has his moment up there
that he can never prove to those below

---

## 379. THE CAROUSEL HORSE

A wooden horse
a red saddle
a golden mane
mouth open for the gallop
and legs forever in the air

He gallops
he gallops from morning
he gallops till evening
he gallops all his life
and arrives nowhere

Behind him goes a horse
before him goes a horse
beside him the barrel organ clatters
always the same song
always the same round

Where are you running little horse
asks the little girl
clutching the mane
where are you running so fast
are we there yet?

The horse does not answer
the horse only gallops
and the little girl laughs
because they will arrive nowhere
and that is exactly it

Children love him
for exactly the reason
that he arrives nowhere
sometimes it is beautiful to run without a goal
while the barrel organ plays

---

## 380. THE BUTTERFLY OF A SINGLE DAY

In the morning he was born
from a crumpled little sack
he spread his wings
for the first and the last time
and did not know it

He flew out into the garden
where everything was new
the grass was an ocean
the fence was the end of the world
and the sun was eternity

At noon he fell in love
with a white she-butterfly
they danced above the cabbages
for endless hours
three perhaps four

In the afternoon he drank from a rose
in the afternoon he slept in the shade
in the afternoon he wondered
where have I been all this long time
why does life pass me by so fast

He did not know it was evening
he did not know it was the end
he thought tomorrow again
and that tomorrow was now
and that now was enough for him

He lived a whole life
in a single afternoon
and it was enough
length is not the measure
he who knows not when he ends lives whole

---

## 381. THE ROOSTER WHO CROWED TOO LATE

The rooster on the fence
his red comb
proud as a flag
opened his beak
and it was too late

The sun was already over the shed
the sun already warmed the yard
the sun already woke the flies
when the rooster at last
crowed

The farmers grumble
what use is a rooster like that
who sleeps through the morning
who comes crowing
when you can see anyway

The neighbour woman laughs
sell him for the soup
a rooster should crow before the sun
and not trail after it
like a guest arriving late

But the rooster knows
the rooster always knew
the sun came up even without me
the sun needs no alarm
the sun rises on its own

The rooster does not wake the sun
the rooster only greets it
the world gets up by itself
it only wants
someone to welcome it

---

## 382. THE SPIDER IN THE LIBRARY

In the corner of the library
between the dictionary and the wall
lives a spider
tiny and silent
and no one drives her out

The cleaning woman sees her
every morning
she wipes the dust
she sweeps the crumbs
but leaves the web alone

Let her be madam director
begs the cleaning woman
she will harm no one
she only sits there
and waits

The director shrugs
a spider in the library
where there are only rare books
what does she catch there
flies are not rare

But the cleaning woman knows
every night
when the doors are shut
from the open books
words fly out

Words no one has read
words that escaped their sentences
and the spider catches them
in her silent web
every library needs a hunter of what got away

---

## 383. THE PIGEON WITH NO MESSAGE

The last carrier pigeon
grey and grave
takes off from the loft
like every morning
and flies

He flies over the river
he flies over the chimneys
he flies over the wires
where the telegraph runs
that made him obsolete

Once he carried loves
once he carried orders
once he carried the war is over
and people wept
and people laughed

Now he has at his leg
no little note
no scroll
no word
he flies empty

Where are you going little pigeon
asks the wind in the wires
you carry nothing
your route is useless
the telegraph is faster

But an empty pigeon
is a message too
he flies to say
I am still here someone is still waiting
hold on

---

## 384. THE BEE IN WINTER

She woke too early
far too early
in the middle of winter
someone it seemed to her
had said it is spring already

She flew out of the hive
into the white world
where there was no flower
where there was no honey
where there was only frost

She looked for a dandelion
she looked for grass
she looked for clover
she looked for at least a daisy
and found nothing

The street was empty
the garden was dead
the tree was only a skeleton
and the wind said
go home little fool

But at the bus stop
on a wall
hung a poster
and on the poster a meadow
and on the meadow a flower

The bee alighted
on the painted flower
and she was happy
sometimes what saves us for a moment
is even the picture of what is not yet

---

## 385. THE TREE THAT GREW CROOKED

Against the wall grew a tree
and the wall was high
and the sun was behind it

so the tree bent
a little
then more
then all of it

all its life it reached
sideways
toward the light
the wall stole from it

the neighbors said
that tree is crooked
that tree is sick
that tree grows wrong

and the tree said nothing
and kept on growing
crooked
toward the sun

then the workmen came
and tore the wall down
in one afternoon
it was gone

and the sun spilled out
everywhere
and nothing anymore
held anything back

but the tree
the tree stayed crooked
bent toward the place
where the wall had stood

we carry our bends
from walls
that fell long ago
and we ask why we don't stand up straight

---

## 386. THE LAST LEAF

On the tree stayed one leaf
the last
in November
all the others already down

the wind blew
and the leaf held on
the rain fell
and the leaf held on

people walked by
and pointed
look
that one's afraid to fall

that one clings out of fear
they said
that one won't come down
that one fears the ground

but the leaf was not afraid
the leaf stood watch
the last sentry
on the bare branch

for it knew
the leaf knew
when I fall
winter will come
and cover everyone

so it stayed
the longest
alone up there
so winter would come at no one's back

and one morning
it was down as well
and the snow arrived
and there was silence

and no one knew
that all that time
someone had held them
just before the winter

---

## 387. THE SEED IN THE ASPHALT

In the middle of the car park
where the cars stand
and oil drips from them
a seed broke through the asphalt

a small blade of grass
a green comma
in the black plain
between the wheels

people built this road
mixed the concrete
rolled it flat
smoothed every seam

NOTHING WILL GROW HERE ANYMORE
they said
and went home
satisfied

and autumn came
and winter came
and the asphalt held
hard and black

but underneath
in the dark
the seed waited
and was in no hurry

and in spring
one morning
in the crack there was
a green comma

people walked by
and cursed
a weed
even here grows a weed

and they did not know
that concrete has the last word
only until spring
and then the grass speaks

---

## 388. THE RIVER THAT CHANGED ITS COURSE

After the flood
one night
the river found itself
a new bed

across the meadow
where it had never flowed
now it flows
as if it had always been there

in the morning people came
with maps
and the maps said
the river is over there

but the river was here
calm
wide
and not even apologizing

this is wrong
the mapmakers cried
the river flows where it shouldn't
the river made a mistake

and they measured
and drew
and crossed out the old lines
and they were miserable

but the river
the river asked no one anything
water doesn't ask the maps
water makes its own map

the one who flows
corrects the world
with its own body
and lets the paper catch up to what is real

and the maps were redrawn
because the river was right
the river is always right
the river is the line

---

## 389. THE CLOUD THAT DID NOT RAIN

Over the parched land
floated a cloud
heavy with water
full of rain

and below
the fields were cracking
and the trees were thirsty
and the people looked up

now
they said
now it will rain
the cloud is here
the cloud will save us

and they raised their palms
and waited
and prayed
each in their own way

but the cloud floated on
silent
over their heads
and let down not a drop

and it crossed the mountains
and beyond the mountains
where no one called it
where no one cursed it

there it rained
generously
on the forest
that needed no rain

we give exactly where
we are not wanted
and we pass over
the thirsty beneath us

and we never know
why the cloud
saves its water
for a foreign land

---

## 390. THE STONE ON THE CAIRN

At the start of the path
the pilgrim picked up a stone
small
grey
ordinary

and carried it up
the whole way
in his palm
and the stone weighed

why does he carry it
the others asked
it's only a stone
throw it away
spare your shoulders

but he carried
step after step
uphill
in silence

and at the top
there was a cairn
of stones
that others had carried up

and he set his down
on top
and the mountain was one stone higher
and he was one stone lighter

and he went down
with an empty palm
and he was smiling
and did not know why

each pilgrim adds one
the mountain grows out of thank-yous
of small reliefs from the weight
we carried all the way here

great things
are built
from what
we were finally allowed to set down

---

## 391. THE APPLE TREE BY THE DEMOLISHED HOUSE

There stood an apple tree
and beside it a house
and in the house a family
and by the window a kitchen

and the apple tree bloomed in spring
and bore fruit in autumn
and the children climbed the branches
and the mother baked a pie

then the family moved away
and the house stood empty
the windows blind
the chimney cold

and one day the diggers came
and the house was suddenly a heap
and then not even a heap
just grass and foundations

but the apple tree
the apple tree stayed
and in spring it bloomed
as if nothing had happened

and in autumn
it bore apples
heavy
red
sweet

for no one
they fell in the grass
where the kitchen had been
and rotted in silence

every autumn again
faithful
to the house
that is no more

the one who planted
stayed
and bears fruit
even for the empty place where love was

---

## 392. SNOW IN SPRING

It was April
and the trees were in bloom
white pink
full of bees

and in the night
quite softly
snow fell
on the blossoming branches

in the morning
the world was twice white
the blossoms below
the snow above
and between them a scent

and whoever passed early
stood still
and did not breathe
so it would not vanish

blossom and snow
at once
spring and winter
on a single branch

it's impossible
he whispered
this cannot be
and it was

and an hour later
the sun rose
and the snow melted
and dripped from the petals like weeping

and whoever did not see it
will not believe
the most beautiful things
have this bad luck

that they are at once
impossible
and last
just long enough that you cannot run to reach them

---

## 393. THE ROOT UNDER THE PAVEMENT

Under the pavement
grew a root
thick as an arm
old as the street

and year after year
it pushed upward
slowly
tirelessly

until one day
it lifted a paving slab
and there was a step
where there should be none

and people stumbled
and cursed
who fixed this like that
who is to blame

the lady with a tree in her bag
fell on her knee
and cursed the tree
and cursed the town

and the tree
the tree knew nothing of it
the tree was only breathing
the tree was only living

it wanted to trip no one
it wanted to harm no one
it only needed
a little room to breathe

under every flat pavement
grows something
that refuses to submit
and one day it lifts the stone

and we call it a fault
and it is only life
that would not fit
into our straight lines

---

## 394. THE TWO TREES GROWN TOGETHER

By the path stood two trees
so close
that their trunks had grown together
into one

and people stopped
and had their photos taken
and said
look how they love each other

a couple in love
they whispered
a tree that holds a tree
together forever

they carved hearts in the bark
and names
and swore each other faithfulness
beneath the fused trunks

and the trees said nothing
and grew
grown together
unable to do otherwise

for once
many years ago
a gardener passed this way
tired as evening came

and he had two saplings
and only one hole
and it was late
and he said to himself never mind

and he pushed the two together
too close
and filled it in
and went home to sleep

out of a tired man's mistake
a legend of love was born
and people believe the bark
more than the gardener

and perhaps they are right
for what began as a mistake
now holds
tighter than many a vow

---

## 395. THE GRAVEDIGGER AND THE NAMES

Every morning the gravedigger takes his spade
and walks the cemetery
the way you walk a street
where you know everyone

Good morning Mary
good morning Joseph
here lies Anna who baked the cakes
here Thomas who limped on the left

Nobody answers him
and still he keeps talking
about the weather
about the rain that will fall by evening

Down in the town below the hill
people forget
the name of the neighbor on the third floor
the name of the postman
the name of the girl who died the other day

He alone does not forget them
he keeps them in his mouth like small change
they jingle when he walks

And when someone says
how lonely they are these dead
the gravedigger leans on his spade
and quietly says
lonely is only the one
no one can name anymore

---

## 396. THE BRIDGE KEEPER

All night the keeper sits
beside the drawbridge
and waits

The river flows
the lamp burns
the wheel does not turn

Cars come by
and ask
what are you waiting for old man?
a boat

What boat?
that one

The cars laugh
they honk
they go around by the other bridge
and he sits on

Once a week
sometimes on Tuesday
sometimes on Thursday
a small barge of sand comes up
and he stands
pulls the lever
the bridge lifts
like a hat being tipped

People say
a useless job
one bridge one boat

But the captain of that barge knows
that rounding the bend in the night
someone will be there
and that is the whole job
you can count on it

---

## 397. THE WASHERWOMAN

At home she has a machine
white silent modern
and still she takes her basket
and goes down to the river

She kneels on the stone
rinses a sheet
the water is cold
her hands turn red

People ask
grandmother why do you come here
you have a machine at home
she smiles
and says nothing

Once there were washerwomen here
a whole row of them
Teresa Betty Marion
they chattered they laughed
they beat the linen on the stone
like beating a drum

Now the row is empty
only her and the current

She rinses still
and they rinse with her
each on her own stone
that is no longer there

I don't come here to wash
she says softly to the water
I come here
to meet someone

---

## 398. THE STONECUTTER

All his life he carved others' names
into the cold stone
letter after letter
the hammer and the chisel

Miller Smith Baker
here rests
here sleeps
here in peace

The hand does not waver
the eye is sure
the marble obeys

He grew old among the names
fingers twisted like roots
and one evening in the workshop
he took a fresh stone
to cut his own

The hammer rose
and did not fall

He sat there into the dark
the name he wrote for everyone
suddenly he did not know it

In the morning he called the apprentice
boy come here
write it for me
and he dictated letter by letter
his own name

The hand that writes for all
needs at the end
a stranger's hand

---

## 399. THE SWITCHBOARD OPERATOR

Once she sat at the great board
full of holes and cords
and she connected

Hello operator?
put me through to the doctor
one moment please
click
and already they were talking

For years
strangers' voices passed through her hands
lovers made up again
mothers called their sons
people wished each other good night
along the wires she had plugged in

Then came the automatics
the board vanished
they coiled the cords in a box
and told her
we don't need you anymore
they connect themselves

Now she sits at the doctor's
an old woman with her bag on her lap
and beside her two strangers keep quiet

So what's the trouble?
she asks one of them
and before the nurse arrives
the two are already chatting
about grandchildren about backs about rain

She smiles into her lap

A switchboard operator
you can't unplug

---

## 400. THE BAKER AND THE SHAPE OF BREAD

For each regular
the baker makes the bread differently

For the schoolmaster crusty
for old Mrs. Miller soft
for old Victor without caraway
for the girl on the corner a double crust

He knows them by their hands
he knows them by their voices
before they even say good morning
he knows which loaf is theirs

In the morning he lines the loaves on the shelf
like little soldiers
each has its place
each has its name without letters

And when one morning
a place stays empty
the baker stops
wipes his hands on his apron
and is silent a while

No one has told him anything
the paper wrote nothing
but he knows

He has baked one loaf fewer
an obituary of flour
that no one will print

---

## 401. THE DEAF MUSIC TEACHER

The old music teacher
went deaf as a stump
and still he teaches on

Play it for me again Annie
he says
and lays his fingers on the lid of the piano

He does not hear the note
but he feels the trembling
the string sounds in the wood
and from there to the palm
and from there to the heart

The wrong note he knows
by the crease on the child's brow
by the way the mouth tightens
by the finger that hesitates

Very good
he says when the face lights up
once more from the top

People shake their heads
deaf and teaching music
it's like a blind man painting the sky

But the children know better

Silence taught him
to watch the playing
he hears with his palm
he hears with his eyes
and he hears them better than before

---

## 402. THE JUDGE ON THE BENCH

Retired the judge sits
on a bench in the park
he judges no more
he only listens

Mr. Finch comes and sits beside him
your honor you won't believe it
the neighbor threw his leaves over my fence again
and the judge is silent
and the judge nods

Mrs. Groat comes along
mine never takes out the bin
thirty years I've asked him
and the judge is silent
and the judge nods

He gives no one the verdict
no judgment
no gavel
only the sun and the pigeons

And still each one rises lighter
straightens up
smiles
thank you your honor
I feel better already

For years he judged
thinking it was the sentence that heals

Now he knows
people are not relieved
when they win
but when someone hears them out

---

## 403. THE MIDWIFE

By the window the old midwife sits
counting on her fingers
they haven't been enough for years

How many she brought into the world
in this town beneath the tower
how many first cries
how many small wet heads

Half the town has passed through her hands
maybe more

That gentleman who plays the mayor
it was in her hands he first cried out
that lady who sells at the grocer's
the butcher the barber the postman
all of them first lay
in her palms

And yet when she walks down the street
no one knows her
good day grandmother
and on they go

She is not angry

The first face of all
that a person ever sees
is the very one
they forget

She remembers every one
and that is quite enough

---

## 404. THE RETIRED POSTMAN

Every morning the postman sets out
down the same street as for forty years
only the bag is missing from his shoulder

Empty hands
empty shoulders
and still he walks

He passes Mrs. Marek's house
and she waves from the window
good morning Mr. Smith
he passes the bakery the shop the clockmaker
and everywhere a hand goes up
hello hello

He carries nothing
not a letter
not a postcard from the sea
not a bill

And still they watch for him
as before
behind the curtain
behind the counter
on the doorstep

So many years he brought them news
the happy and the sad
the births and the black border

until he became the news himself

When he passes down the street in the morning
each one knows the only thing
they still need to hear

the day goes on

---

## 405. THE BENCH WITH A PLAQUE

On the bench there is a little plaque
made of brass
already a bit green
IN MEMORY OF MISTER MILLER
WHO LOVED TO SIT HERE

Mister Miller used to sit here
he watched the chestnut trees
he fed the pigeons
and he said nothing

Now others sit on the bench
two people in love
her head on his shoulder
he doesn't know what to say
so he says nothing

The children come
they unwrap their snack
crumbs fall for the pigeons
Miller's very own pigeons

An old woman comes
she takes off her shoe
she rests
and she thanks mister Miller
whom she never knew

And mister Miller
from deep in his little brass plaque
invites the whole town
sit down
sit down please

the finest monument
is the one
you can still
after all this time
rest on

---

## 406. THE CROSSING FOR THE BLIND

At three in the morning
the street is empty
not a dog
not a drunk

and the crossing beeps
for the blind
beep beep beep
green now

Nobody is crossing
there is nobody here
only the rain
and a puddle
mirroring the light for nothing

Beep beep beep
says the crossing
into the dark
into nowhere

The repairman would say
we're wasting electricity
the mayor would say
we'll fit a sensor

But the crossing keeps on beeping
because what if
what if right now
someone came by
just one
who cannot see
and needs to hear

Kindness does not check
whether anyone is looking
kindness beeps
just in case
into the empty street
all night long

for that one person
who maybe
will come

---

## 407. THE DISCONTINUED POSTBOX

On the corner hangs a postbox
yellow
rusted
and on the door a slip of paper
COLLECTION DISCONTINUED
THANK YOU FOR UNDERSTANDING

But people are deaf to little signs
so they keep dropping letters in

A girl posts a letter
to the boy who moved away
she wrote forgive me
though she forgave him long ago

An old man posts a note
to his wife dead a year now
he wrote I sleep badly
buy some milk

A boy posts a letter to Santa
even though it's July

The postbox swallows all of it
and sends nothing
nowhere
to no one

Inside it all piles up
forgiveness
milk
Santa Claus
everything we never say to the living

And so it turns out
a postbox with no collection
is the most honest
confessional in town

people write the truth in it
precisely because
it will never
arrive

---

## 408. THE DRY FOUNTAIN

They shut the fountain off
in autumn
because of the frost
because of the savings
because of everything

Only a hollow remains
dry
made of stone
with a few leaves

But people still come
and toss their coins
clink
onto the dry bottom

A girl wishes for love
a boy wishes for a bike
an old man wishes
that someone still needs him

The coins make no splash
they only clink
against the stone
against the dry wish

The keeper grumbles
you don't throw coins in an empty fountain
there's no water
there's no magic

But the magic was never in the water

A wish needs no water
all it needs
is a hollow
and the faith
that down there
someone is listening

drought can stop the water
but never
never
the longing

---

## 409. THE CLOCK THAT RUNS SLOW

The clock on the tower
in the square
runs five minutes slow
and has for twenty years

The clockmaker died
the next one feared the climb
the third one said
it doesn't bother anyone anyway

And it really doesn't bother anyone

The baker bakes five minutes later
the school bell rings five minutes later
the lovers meet
five minutes later
and wait five minutes less
so it all evens out

The whole town runs late
and gets everything done
because everyone is late by the same amount

A tourist arrives
he looks at the clock
he looks at his phone
Is your time wrong here?
he asks

No
says the old woman on her bench
here we keep
only our own

time is not a truth
time is an agreement
and our agreement
simply
by five minutes
fell behind

---

## 410. THE LAMP IN LOVE

It's an ordinary lamp
on the corner by the park
nothing special
iron and glass

But underneath it
since forever
people kiss

The first kisses
the most trembling ones
the ones that pound the heart up into the ears

They've come here since the dawn of time
grandmothers came here as young girls
and now their granddaughters do too

The lamp shines
and says nothing
and warms all that trembling

And then one day it goes out
it burns through
before every other lamp on the street

The town sends the electrician
strange case
he writes in his report
this bulb lasts the shortest time
and we don't know why

But the old woman on the corner knows

Whoever warms other people's loves
she says
whoever lights up all that trembling
is the one
who burns out first

and the lamp lights up again
and once again it waits
for the next ones

---

## 411. THE PHONE BOOTH LIBRARY

From the old telephone booth
they made a library
TAKE ONE LEAVE ONE
written on the glass
where it used to say TELEPHONE

Inside instead of the receiver
shelves
and on them books
that nobody wanted

Cookbooks with no covers
crime novels with no endings
a Russian textbook
and an atlas of the world
that isn't true anymore

People come here
who would never buy a book
because a book you pay for
is a commitment
while a book for free
is only an invitation

A laborer takes a book of poems
for the first time in his life
because well
it's free

A little girl takes the atlas
where a country still exists
that exists no more

And so it turns out
the books borrowed the most
are exactly the ones
nobody ever bought

abandoned stories
find their readers
only when
no one
wants
to profit
from them anymore

---

## 412. THE WORN BANISTER

At the foot of the old stairs
there is an iron banister
black
but in one spot
it shines like gold

There
right there
thousands of palms hold on

The worker heading to his job at dawn
whose back aches
The old woman counting the steps out loud
The drunk holding on so he won't fall
The mother carrying her child and afraid
The boy taking the steps three at a time
who grabs it anyway

Nobody agreed on it
and yet
everyone touched
the very same spot

A painter would say
it's the patina
an engineer would say
it's the wear on the metal

But the banister knows better

It shines exactly there
where people
were afraid of falling

the city is most
handled
worn
polished to gold

precisely there
where hardest of all
we held on

---

## 413. THE TWO STATION CLOCKS

In the station there are two clocks
one above the ticket window
the other on the platform
and each shows a different time

The one by the window runs ahead
the one on the platform drags its feet

The traveler sees them both
and has to choose
which one to believe

The one in a hurry
afraid of missing his train
believes the slower one
I still have time
I've got five minutes left

The one fleeing from someone
believes the faster one
I have to go
sorry
I really have to go now

The stationmaster knows this
and fixes neither of them
let everyone
choose their own

A boy comes in
running after a girl
is it one minute left or six?

He looks at the slower clock
because he is hoping

and so it becomes clear
that the truth
is not something we choose
by the clock

the truth
is something we choose
by our hope

---

## 414. THE CHAINED BICYCLE

Against the railing stands a bicycle
chained up
the chain rusted
the tire flat
the saddle cracked by the sun

It has stood here for years
winter summer winter
and no one comes for it

The passersby know it
the way you know a tree
it's part of the corner
it's part of the street

The council wanted it removed
it's in the way
it spoils the look
it's just a wreck

But the neighbor came out of his house
and he said
leave it be

That bicycle was left by Thomas
before he went away
he promised he'd come back
and unlock it himself

Thomas never came back
maybe he forgot
maybe he couldn't
maybe he never can again

But the lock holds fast

and so it turns out
it isn't a monument to a bicycle
it's a monument to a promise

the lock isn't holding the bicycle
the lock is holding
that one single word
I'll return

---

## 415. THE BIRD IN THE OPEN CAGE

The little door is open
the little door was open
yesterday
the day before
and God knows how long before that

The bird sits on its perch
the bird preens its feathers
the bird stares at the rectangle of light
where the wire ends
and the air begins

Come out
calls the sparrow from the gutter
come out
calls the wind
come out
calls the whole sky at once

The bird tilts its head
the bird says later
the bird says when it warms up
the bird says tomorrow

And it has spoken like this
through so many tomorrows
that it has grown itself another cage
this one without any wire

The door stayed open
even the night it fell asleep for the last time
one wing outside
one wing in

Sometimes we open the door
to the one
who has forgotten how to fly
and he thanks us
for letting him stay sitting

---

## 416. THE GUIDE DOG WITHOUT A MASTER

Left at the bench
right at the fountain
stop at the curb
wait
now cross

The dog knows all of it
the dog does all of it
only the hand on the harness is missing
only that hand

In the morning it comes out the door
that nobody opened for it
down the stairs
it counts from habit
and the park takes it in like every day

Slow down begs the lady with the little dog
slow down begs the man with the newspaper
he is blind
no
he is guiding
someone who isn't there

At the third bench it sits
that is where they always rested
it lays its head on the empty place
and waits
for an elbow to lean that never comes

People bring it water
people bring it bones
and it accepts them politely
like a dog on duty
because it is still on duty

The care it was taught
has nowhere to go
so it walks in circles
love outlives the one
it served
and looks for him in every face

---

## 417. THE BOOKSHOP CAT

In the window among the volumes
a cat sleeps
curled like a comma
at the end of an unfinished sentence

Above it the signs read
HISTORY OF THE WORLD
POETRY OF ALL AGES
MANUALS ON HOW TO LIVE

and it sleeps
because it already knows how

The bell over the door
rings
rings
rings

I came for some Rimbaud
says the young man
and he strokes the cat
I came for a dictionary
says the lady
and she strokes the cat
I came just to look
says the old man
and he stays longest by the cat

The bookseller counts the till
the bookseller counts the small till
the bookseller counts how many left
with empty hands
and a warm palm

Sometimes the bait is wisdom
and the catch is warmth
the cat knows nothing of it
the cat only knows
that every hand
reaching for a book
reached in the end for it

---

## 418. THE SWAN ON THE FROZEN POND

All the swans have flown
south
toward the warmth
toward water that does not freeze

One stayed behind
no one knows why
maybe it forgot
maybe it fell in love with the place
maybe it simply would not

And the pond froze around it
like a ring around a finger
the ice gripped it
the ice held it
the ice said now you are mine

The first man came
with a pickaxe
and broke the ice
GENTLY DON'T HURT IT
shouted the second
and already two were chopping

In the morning the baker comes
before he opens
to cut the swan a window in the water
in the afternoon the children
throw crumbs and chop the ice
in the evening the old man with the lantern
watches that the hole won't freeze

The bird that did not fly
gave the town a task
and for the first time all winter
the town rises earlier
to break the ice
for one white stubbornness

The one who does not fly away
teaches the others
how to care

---

## 419. THE ANTS AND THE CRUMB

Ten ants
under a single crust
the crust is a mountain
the crust is a ship
the crust is ten times all of them together

Left heave
right heave
over the blade of grass
around the stone
down the slope that is their Alps

They carry it
they carry it an hour
they carry it without a single word
because ants do not complain
ants only carry

A child came
big as a cloud
it bent down
it lifted the crumb with two fingers
I'M ONLY LOOKING
it told the sky

And the ants stood
at the empty place
where the mountain had been
where the ship had been
where the meaning of the whole morning had been

The child held the crumb in its palm
the child watched below
the small ones circling nothing
and something in that big child
grew ashamed

It bent down a second time
it set the crumb back
exactly there
where it had taken it from

Even the giant feels shame
before the stubbornness of the small
and sometimes it stoops
only to give back
what was never its own

---

## 420. THE OWL ON THE TOWN HALL

In the tower of the town hall
between the clock and the bell
lives an owl
no one elected it
and yet there it sits

Below they administer
a stamp here
a stamp there
REQUEST DENIED
REQUEST GRANTED
according to how each one breathes

And the owl sleeps
the owl sleeps all day
because the owl works at night
when the town hall is dark
and wisdom may sit wherever it likes

When the tower strikes twelve
the clerks are long since home
and the owl opens its eyes
great round eyes
and looks upon the town
that no one governs
and that keeps turning anyway

In the morning the men with briefcases return
the owl closes its eyes
they pass on the staircase of time
it goes to sleep
they go to decide

Wisdom and office
in one tower
never meet
one is open
when the other closes

And so the town possesses
a clock that shows the hour
a bell that announces it
and an owl that in silence
alone of all
truly keeps watch over it

---

## 421. THE DOG AND THE POSTMAN

Every morning at eight
the gate creaks
and the dog knows
now
now
now comes the enemy

It barks
it barks at the blue cap
it barks at the leather bag
it barks at the hand
that drops letters in the box
as into its mouth

Ten years
fifteen years
a whole dog's life
is one long war
for the fence
for honor
for a piece of the morning

The postman laughs
good dog
faithful dog
and he lays down the newspaper
the envelope
the postcard from the sea
that no one reads

And then one day
the postman comes without a bag
the postman comes without a cap
and he says over the fence
I won't be coming anymore
I'm retiring old friend

The dog stops
the dog falls silent
the dog pushes its nose between the slats
and licks that hand
which for fifteen years
handed out the letters and the anger

The enmity was only a job
and when the service ends
what remains
is what lay beneath it all along
affection

---

## 422. THE STRANDED WHALE

The sea has gone away
and left her lying
big as a ship
heavy as a whole church
on the wet sand
where the sky dries out her skin

The fisherman came with a bucket
one bucket of sea
splash
The little girl came with her watering can
a small yellow sea
splash
The old man came with a pot
and greetings from his rheumatism
splash

Hold on begs the woman from the bakery
hold on shouts the boy on the bicycle
we're bringing you the sea
we're bringing you the ocean
by the handful
by the mouthful
by the tear

The whole town stands in a line
from the waves up to the whale
passing water from hand to hand
like a candle at Christmas
except this candle
is meant to put out drought

The whale breathes
slowly
heavily
like the bellows of an organ
and her eye
big as a plate
follows that procession of ants
carrying her the sea

When someone is big enough
even our small helps
our ridiculous buckets
our cooking pots
add up
into a tide

---

## 423. THE HORSE AND THE TRACTOR

In the yard stands a tractor
red
new
it smells of diesel and the future

Beside it a horse
an old draft horse
it smells of hay and yesterday
and it looks at its successor
with one big wet eye

The farmer walks around the tractor
the farmer strokes the hood
so much power
so many horses under the metal
say the neighbors laughing
and the horse hears it
the horse hears everything

In the morning the farmer starts it up
the tractor growls
the tractor puffs its smoke
the tractor rolls out to the field
alone
without a bridle
without a word

And the horse stayed by the fence
the horse is no longer needed
the horse is only for looking at now
thinks the whole yard

But in the evening
when the field is plowed
the farmer leaves the tractor standing there
and walks to the fence
unties the horse
and they go home together
on foot
down the field path

Strength can be replaced
how many horses under the metal do you want
but the companion
the one who walks beside you
in silence
all the way home
that one cannot be replaced

---

## 424. THE STORK ON THE CHIMNEY

On the old factory chimney
that is to come down
there is a nest
and in the nest a stork
that knows nothing of it

It crossed all of Africa
the sea
the mountains
the borders it does not know
just to land
exactly here
on its chimney

Below the diggers wait
below the demolition waits
CHIMNEY TO BE DEMOLISHED
says the sign
and the date
and the stamp
and the signature of the engineer

But up top the beak clatters
clatter clatter clatter
and the stork lays twig upon twig
mending the roof of the world
as it does every spring

The engineer looks up
for the first time in his life
and he sets down his pencil
well then
he says
after the season, then

And the town begins to walk with its head raised
it's still there
it's feeding already
there are three of them now

One single nest
put off the demolition by a spring
one single nest
saved the chimney
and that long forgotten habit
of looking up

---

## 425. THE SHADOW

The shadow complains
all day long it complains
always behind you
always behind you

when you go to work
I go behind you
when you come home
I go behind you
never ahead
never beside
always behind

I carry your bags that I don't carry
I open the doors that I don't open
and nobody thanks me
not even you

I lie down on the pavement
I lie down on the stairs
people walk all over me
and you don't stop

who even counts me
who even sees me
I am your darkness
that you drag behind you
like a sack

but at noon
at high noon
when the sun stands at its highest
I grow small
small as a puddle
and I lie down at your feet

and for the first time
the first time in your life
you stand upon your shadow

only in the highest light
are we equal with our darkness

---

## 426. THE ECHO LEFT ALONE

In the abandoned quarry
among the stones
lives an echo
and it has nothing to do

once there were people here
they called out hey
and it called out hey
they called the stone is falling
and it called stone stone stone

now there is silence
and silence does not repeat itself

the echo sits on a rock
and it waits
say something please
say anything at all
I will give it back twice
I will give it back ten times
just give me a word

a fox comes by
the echo rejoices
the fox says nothing
the echo says nothing too

the wind comes by
but the wind has its own voice
and won't lend it out

the echo grows weaker
each day it is quieter
for whoever has nothing to repeat
slowly forgets how to speak

one day a boy comes
he shouts his name into the quarry
and nothing comes back

the echo is no longer there

it was the most faithful of listeners
it died because no one would talk to it

---

## 427. THE SILENCE IN THE HOSPITAL

At three in the morning
in room number nine
the silence came in
and sat down at the edge of the bed

all day there had been noise
the trolleys the machines the footsteps
the doctor said we'll see
the nurse said hold on
the telephone rang behind the wall

now there is silence
and it does nothing

it gives no injection
it takes no blood pressure
it doesn't ask how are we feeling today
it doesn't shine a light in your eyes

it only sits there
and breathes with you
breathe in breathe out
breathe in breathe out

the nurse opens the door
she looks
she sees the silence on the bed
and closes it again very softly

she lets it be
she knows
she knows it best of all

in the morning the round comes
the patient slept for the first time
for the first time without a pill
for the first time without pain

what did we give him asks the young doctor
he leafs through the papers
nothing is written there

we gave him nothing
we only let the silence stay

some things heal only when nothing happens
silence mends what has no medicine

---

## 428. THE FIRST SNOW

The first snow is shy
it comes at night
on purpose at night
so no one will see it fall

it doesn't like an audience
it doesn't like people watching
and pointing and shouting
look look it's snowing

so it waits
it waits for the windows to go dark
for the postman to fall asleep
for the dog to fall asleep
for even the old woman who never sleeps to fall asleep

and then very quietly
very quietly
it begins to change the world's clothes

to the roofs it gives white caps
to the cars white eiderdowns
to the park bench a white coat
and to the general's statue a little white cap
as if to tease him

it works all night
it sweeps away its own tracks behind it
and by morning it is done

people open the shutters
and the world is white
as if it had always been white
as if this snow had always been here

who did this asks the child
nobody knows
nobody saw it

the loveliest transformations happen
exactly when we are not looking
the world changes its clothes in secret
and in the morning plays the innocent

---

## 429. OBLIVION

Oblivion walks through the town
with a great sack
and gathers up names

it gathers names from gravestones
where the rain has already washed the letters away
it gathers names from letters
that no one reads
it gathers names from the lips
of old people

good day Mrs Brown
no one remembers you anymore
come into the sack

good day schoolmaster
your pupils have grown old
come into the sack

the sack fills up
it grows heavy
the names jostle and whisper inside
we were we were we were

oblivion is diligent
oblivion never goes on strike
every day a full sack
every evening an empty town

but in the evening
when it counts its catch
one name is always missing

it searches under the lining
it shakes out the sack
nothing

it is the name
that someone spoke aloud today
at the table
with a tear
because they loved him

love is the only sieve
that oblivion cannot fall through

---

## 430. THE WIND AND THE WASHING

In the yard between the houses
the washing hangs on the line
and the wind comes along
the curious wind

and right away it puts on other people's lives

it slips into the blue shirt
and now it's a postman
carrying letters that will never arrive

it slips into the white apron
and now it's a cook
making soup out of clouds

it slips into a baby's swaddling cloth
and it giggles
it slips into a wedding dress
and dances all alone

it puffs out the sleeves
it kicks in the trouser legs
it fills the stockings with air
and everything in the yard begins to dance
dance dance

the woman on the ground floor comes out
oh the washing is flying away
and she pulls in the line
bad wind she says
wicked wind

but the wind is already gone
already trying on another life
round the corner in another street

it is the only one in the world
who puts on every life
and keeps none of them

that is why it is always hurrying on
to try just one more
and one more
because to live only one
would be too little for it

---

## 431. COUNTING SHEEP

The insomniac lies in bed
and counts the sheep
one sheep
two sheep
three sheep

the sheep jump over the fence
obediently one after another
they jump and jump
all night long

but tonight
at the three hundred and seventeenth
the sheep stops

why are we jumping it asks
I don't know says another
we jump because we jump

and the insomniac counts
four hundred five hundred
the sheep are tired
the sheep want to sleep

one sheep turns around
looks at him over the fence
and asks

and who counts you
so that you can fall asleep?

the insomniac says nothing
here is the question and the answer nowhere
he lies there and is silent
and he stops counting

he stops worrying
what time it is
whether he'll sleep
whether he won't sleep
whether tomorrow is Monday

and as he stops worrying
he falls asleep

even the shepherd of sleep
needs a shepherd of his own
and the sheep knew it first

---

## 432. THE ZERO

The number zero sits in a corner
and cries
I'm good for nothing
I'm good for nothing

everyone laughs at it
you got a zero they shout at school
you've got zero in your account they shout at the bank
he's a zero they say of a man
when they scorn him

the zero is round
round as a hole
round as an empty plate
round as a mouth that's surprised

the one struts about
I'm the first
I'm the best
and the zero cowers behind it

a big bill comes in
big numbers come along
and every one of them needs the zero
but not one of them admits it

zero come here says the one very quietly
I need you
stand behind me

the zero wipes away its tears
it stands behind the one
and the one suddenly becomes ten

another zero stands there
and that makes a hundred
and one more
and that makes a thousand

whoever feels like nothing
is perhaps only standing on the wrong side

worth is not in what you are
worth is a question of place

---

## 433. THE QUESTION MARK

The question mark envies the full stop
it is so calm
it sits down at the end of the sentence
and it's done
and there's silence
and there's certainty

whereas the question mark
keeps bending over
keeps asking
who what when where why
and never has any peace

full stop how do you do it
asks the question mark
the full stop says nothing
the full stop answers with silence
for the full stop has said its last word

the question mark stoops
it has a body like a hook
like a man who is always asking about something
and is never sure

I'd rather be straight
says the question mark
I'd rather be a full stop
to finish and have peace

and evening comes
and people read

the sentence with a full stop they read
and they move on
and they forget

but at the sentence with a question mark
they stop
and they think
and they come back
and they keep on asking

do you still love me?
that sentence no one passes by

whoever asks stays
certainty is the end of the conversation

---

## 434. THE REST IN MUSIC

In the score between the black dots
sits a rest
a small quiet rest
and it is ashamed

all the notes are singing
do re mi
each one has its tone
each one sounds
each one shows off

only the rest is silent
I make no sound
I can't do anything
I'm only a hole in the song

the violinist skips over me
the singer takes a breath and moves on
I am that empty place
where nothing happens

and the rest would love to run away
far from the score
who needs a silence
in the middle of all this music

the concert comes
the great hall
a thousand people
and the orchestra plays

it plays the storm it plays love
it plays until the windows tremble
and then

and then comes the rest

the conductor lifts his hand
he stops the whole orchestra
and holds the silence in his palm
one second two

and the whole hall
a thousand people
hold their breath
and listen

they listen to it
they listen to that silence

the strongest note of the evening
is the one that never sounds

---

## 435. THE HIDDEN CLOAKROOM TICKET

In his wallet between the coins
and an old tram ticket
he keeps a cloakroom ticket
number eighteen

The coat that went with it
he stopped wearing long ago
the elbows worn through
the collar frayed
someone threw it out

But the ticket stayed
eighteen
a little disc of cardboard
with a nail-hole in it

That evening there was music
and a hall
and someone said come in
please come in
and he came in

He handed over his coat
he took his ticket
and all evening long
he was somewhere invited

Now he is older
the coat is gone
the hall perhaps as well
knocked down or turned into a bank

But eighteen remains
eighteen
and when he opens his wallet
he always remembers
that once
somebody said
come in

---

## 436. GRANDMOTHER AND THE SOAP OPERA

Every evening at eight
grandmother turns on the television
and her family arrives

There is Clare who can never decide
there is the doctor who always lies
there is the kind one who will die too soon
and the scoundrel who hurts them all
and yet she rather loves him

She simmers them a soup in her mind
she scolds them
Clare don't marry him
do you hear me
don't marry him

She knows their birthdays
