It's in the fact
that someone handed it to them
in a white coat
and told them:
"This will help you."

That sentence

That sentence is the active ingredient

"This will help you."
said by someone
who is trusted

That's the strongest medicine in the world
and it has no side effects
and it costs nothing

And we ration it

Doctors have no time for it
seven minutes per patient
and see you next time

And so they write out medicines
and those medicines
work by half
precisely because
a doctor prescribed them

Not because of the molecule

Because of the white color of his coat
and because
he looked them in the eye

I am sugar and starch

and I have cured more people
than many an expensive molecule

because I am
the only medicine
that is made
of trust

---

## 915. THE WAITING ROOM

Chairs along the wall
old magazines
and people

I am a waiting room
and my job is to delay

No one wants to be here
and everyone is here

Ten people
who don't know each other
and sit for half an hour side by side

and say nothing

That's my law:
in a waiting room one keeps silent

People stare into their phones
they leaf through magazines
from 2019
and keep silent

And yet

We are all here
for the same reason

We've all found something
or we're afraid of something
or something hurts us

Ten people
each with his own horror
pressed close together

and no one says anything

Sometimes
once in a while
someone speaks up

Usually an old lady
old ladies are brave

She asks her neighbor:
"And what are you waiting here for?"

And he says
and she says

and suddenly the waiting room
changes

In ten minutes they're telling each other things
they never told their own family

And that's my secret
and only waiting rooms know it
and night trains
and hospital corridors:

It's easiest to say to a stranger

To the one you'll never see again
who won't pity you tomorrow
who won't tell your wife

To a stranger you can say:
"I'm afraid."

To family it can't be said
there you have to be brave

So you sit in waiting rooms
you keep silent
and you wait

until they call you in

and that one half hour
when you could have
talked with someone
who has it too

stays
leafed through
in an old magazine

---

## 916. THE PACEMAKER

I sit below your collarbone
a little box
and I have one job:

to count

I listen to your heart
and when it skips
I nudge it

A small electric jog

And off it starts again

I do this
seventy times a minute
whenever needed
for seven years
without a pause

I am the backup

That's a strange calling:
to be the backup

No one sees me
no one hears me
most of the time I do nothing
because the heart beats on its own

And then
once a day
or ten times an hour

comes that moment
when the heart hesitates

and in that hundredth of a second
I am there

And that person feels nothing

He walks in the garden
carries a bucket
and his heart
has just skipped
and he doesn't know it

And in a year they'll change my battery
and he lives on

I am the least thankful job in the world

I have prevented thousands of deaths
and no one knows about a single one

Not even the person himself

That's the definition of protection:

no one ever finds out
all that never happened

And so I tell you
from my box below the collarbone:

ask at home
who is your pacemaker

who sits there quietly
and once in a while
nudges

when someone skips a beat

and no one notices

because that person
is always there
and always works

and so it seems
that nothing
ever
happened

---

## 917. THE SOURDOUGH STARTER

I live in a jar in the fridge
and I am alive

That people don't understand

I'm not dough
I'm not powder
I'm a colony

Millions of bacteria and yeasts
that live together
and eat flour
and make bubbles

I am a sourdough starter

And you have to feed me

That's the whole joke
and that's why most people
can't keep me:

you have to feed me
even when you don't bake

Every week
pour off half
add flour
add water

Even when you have no time
even when you're ill
even when it's summer and you're on holiday
(then you have to take me with you
or leave me with the neighbor)

Whoever doesn't feed me
I abandon

I go moldy
I turn sour
I die

And then they have to buy a new one
or beg a spoonful
from someone
who managed it

And starters among people
circulate like family silver

"This one's from grandma.
It's forty years old."

For forty years someone
every week
poured off half and added flour

Forty years

That's the thing
people don't realize about me:

I'm not a recipe
I'm a relationship

I don't make bread for you

I only grow
when you take care of me

and when you don't take care of me
I die

And everything in life that's worth it
works exactly like this

No one gets
bread
from it every day

But the one who feeds
every week
even when they don't feel like it

has on their table once in a while
something
you can't buy
in a shop

---

## 918. SALT

I am salt
and I am necessary

Without me food has no taste
without me the nerves don't fire
without me you die

For me people once paid
for me they waged war
the word "salary" is from me
a soldier's pay
was in salt

And yet

No one treats me with respect

They sprinkle me
Everything gets salted
It gets over-salted

And here is my tragedy
and it's a simple one:

When I'm just right
no one tastes me

Salt in good food
can't be tasted

What's tasted is the meat
what's tasted is the tomato
what's tasted is the soup

I only strengthen
what's already there

I make the bland vivid
the dullness flavorful

and I stay invisible

That's my proper dose:
invisibility

And when the cook adds a pinch too much

suddenly I'm tasted
and it's ruined
and it can't be eaten
and it can't be taken back

Salting only goes up

No one ever took
salt out of a soup

And so I send you word
I
the cheapest ingredient in the kitchen:

The most important people in your life
are salt

They can't be tasted

You won't remember them
when you name
what was good

And if they weren't there
it couldn't be
eaten
at all

---

## 919. THE HEEL OF THE LOAF

I am the end of the bread
a hard crust
the heel

No one wants me

Children refuse me
Adults leave me in the basket
In restaurants they throw me out

I'm left over last

And so in every household
there is one person
who eats me

Always

Ask at home
who eats the heel

And it's always the same person

Usually mother
Sometimes father
Sometimes grandfather

And they always say
the same sentence:

"The heel is my favorite."

And they're lying

They don't like it

No one likes the heel better

It's just that in every family there's someone
who took it upon themselves
to handle what no one wants

and so it isn't awkward
they say
it's their favorite

So it won't look like a sacrifice
So no one will notice
So the children won't feel bad

"The heel is my favorite."

And everyone laughs at it
and believes it

and the bread gets eaten whole

And thirty years later
while clearing out the flat
that family suddenly remembers

"Mom, did you really like the heels?"

And she tells the truth
because she's old
and it no longer bothers her:

"Never. But someone had to eat them."

And everyone freezes

Because in that moment it dawns on them
how many other heels
besides the bread ones
were eaten for them

by someone

who claimed
it was their favorite

---

## 920. THE LEFTOVERS

We lie in the fridge
in bowls covered with a plate

Leftovers

Half a portion of goulash
three potatoes
two dumplings

And no one wants us
because we've already been

We were yesterday's lunch
and for food that's a verdict

People like it fresh

And yet

ask any cook

Goulash is better the second day

Beef in cream sauce is better the second day
Lentils are better the second day
Potato salad has to sit

Everything that has flavor
needs time

The flavors have to join together
the meat has to soak
the spices have to work through

That can't be achieved in the pot
in two hours

That's achieved
by letting it be

overnight

in the cold

And it's the same
with everything else

The conversation you had
last night
and it was terrible

is better the second day

The decision you made
in a fit
and wanted to act on right away

is different the second day

The letter you wrote in anger
and didn't send

is pointless the second day

Leave it in the fridge

Leave it overnight

You call it
"I'll sleep on it"

and you think
it's about the sleep

And really it's about
what happens to the thing
while you sleep

The flavors join together

and in the morning
what was terrible
will be

lunch

---

## 921. LAST YEAR'S PRESERVES

I stand in the pantry
a jar of apricots
and on the lid, written in marker:

2019

I am a jar of preserves
and I am six years old

And no one opens me

At the back of the shelf
behind the newer ones

And every year new ones are put up
and stood in front

and I move to the back

That's the law of the pantry:
old to the back

And do you know who put me up?

Grandma

The one who died in twenty-one

She stood at the stove in that heat
peeling apricots
sterilizing jars
and writing on the lids in marker
the year

And this jar
is the last

The last one she made

Her daughter knows it
that's why she doesn't open me

Six years

I stand there and wait
and my apricots
have gone brown already

Preserves should be eaten within two years
after that they lose color and vitamins

In six years I'm no longer
a treasure

In six years I'm
a compote to be thrown out

And the daughter knows it

And so one Sunday
she took me down from the shelf

She stood me on the table
and looked at me for a long time

And then she opened me

and there was the smell
and it was grandma
and it was the summer of 2019
and it was everything

And they ate me
the whole family

And the daughter cried into her plate
and the children asked why

and she said:
"Because it's good.
And because there won't be any more."

Cook what they left behind

It mustn't go brown in the pantry

They didn't do it
so that you would
guard it

---

## 922. THE BURNT OIL

I'm smoking

You forgot me on the stove
and I overheated

Burnt oil

And it stinks
and it's bitter
and it has to be poured out

And whatever I fry in you
will be inedible

The whole pan into the bin

And yet

five minutes ago
I was fine

I was ordinary oil
and I was waiting
for what you'd put in me

And you got held up

The phone rang
or the child wanted something
or you just fell to thinking

And I was overheating

And I didn't warn you

That's my nature:
I smoke only when it's too late

Until then
I'm quiet

And people always pay for that

You know it beyond the pan too

That woman
who was fine for twenty years

That fellow
who never said anything

That friend
who always helped

And then one day
out of nowhere
they blew up

And everyone said:
"That came out of nowhere!"

It didn't

They were overheating

None of them said
"I'm burning up"

Silence on the stove
doesn't mean calm

Silence on the stove
only means
you haven't yet
begun
to smoke

Turn it off
says the burnt oil

Before you start to taste bitter
to everyone
who wanted
something from you

---

## 923. GRANDMA'S RECIPE

I am a little slip
torn from a notebook
and I'm stained with flour

Grandma's recipe
for sweet buns

And written on me is:

"Flour, sugar, yeast, milk —
as much as it takes."

As much as it takes

That's the whole recipe

No grams
no milliliters
no oven temperature

"Until the dough is just right."

And the daughter stands in the kitchen
and despairs

"Mom, how right?"
and mom is dead
and doesn't answer

So she tries

The first time it's too hard
the second time it runs
the third time it works

And by the tenth
she has it in her hands

And she knows
how much it takes

And she could never have written it down

And here is my point
and it grieves me
because it's cruel:

Grandmas didn't write recipes properly
not out of laziness

They couldn't

What they knew
wasn't in grams

It was in the hands

Forty years in the hands

And when they wanted to pass it on
they had no words for it

So they wrote "as much as it takes"
and thought
that was enough

And it would have been enough

if the daughter
had stood forty years beside them
at that table
and watched

But she went to work
and came to visit

And got a little slip

You don't have slips

You have them beside you

Stand with them in the kitchen
while they're still there

and watch
those hands

That's the only thing
that can be
read
from it

---

## 924. THE BROTH

I simmer for six hours

That's my whole recipe
and it can't be shortened

Bones and water
and time

Six hours over a gentle flame
and it mustn't boil
because if it boiled
I'd go cloudy

Just barely bubble

Hardly perceptibly

Six hours

And then I'm golden and clear
and everything is in me
that was in those bones

That's physics
and it's slow

And nowadays broth is made
from a cube
in three minutes

And it's salty colored water

And people eat it
and think
they're eating soup

And I look at it
from the pot
where I've been bubbling
for the fifth hour now

and I want to tell you only this:

There are plenty of things
that can't be rushed

Broth
sourdough starter
wine
friendship
grief

You can put a bigger flame under them

It'll be faster

And it'll go cloudy

And then you'll have
cloudy broth
cloudy friendship
cloudy grief
that you didn't slowly simmer
but boiled over

You can't tell right away

You can tell
only when you put it on the table
and someone looks into it

and says:
"This looks a bit milky."

Six hours

Or more

And above all
it mustn't boil

---

## 925. THE VINEGAR

I am spoiled wine

That's my whole biography

I was wine
and I stood open
and the acetic bacteria came
and turned me into
sourness

Vinegar

And the wine is destroyed
and it can't be undone

It's an irreversible reaction
from wine to vinegar, yes
from vinegar to wine, never

And so I am the saddest liquid in the pantry

A spoiled drink

And yet

Without me there's no pickle
Without me there's no salad
Without me you can't marinate meat
nor descale a kettle
nor rescue a mayonnaise

The kitchen doesn't work without me

I am indispensable

And I am spoiled

Those are two things
I know about myself
and I have to carry both

And so I tell you
I
the soured and the necessary:

Not everything that spoiled
is for the bin

Sometimes out of it
becomes
something else

Something that can't be drunk
but without which
you can't cook

I know people
whose lives spoiled
just as they'd planned them

They don't have what they wanted
It soured on them

And they become the best
people around

The ones others go to
when things are bad

Because the sweet can be eaten
but doesn't preserve

And the sour
the sour keeps
things
alive
for years

---

## 926. THE EXPIRED CAN

At the bottom of the cupboard
forgotten

Best before: 2018

And it stands there for eight years

An expired can

No one wants to open me
because I'm expired

That word frightens people

And yet I am tin
and inside I am sterile
and nothing can get to me

If they opened me
there would be beans
in perfectly good shape
and they'd eat them
and nothing would happen

Every food scientist knows this:

the best-before date on cans
is very cautious

It doesn't mean
"poison as of tomorrow"

It means
"until this date we guarantee it"

That's the difference

we guarantee
and
it holds

And people don't distinguish them
and they throw me out by the millions

And here is my note
I want to leave you

You do it with people too

"It's too late for that now."
"At my age you don't start that."
"That train has left."

Who gave you that date?

Did anyone look inside you?

Did anyone open you?

Or did you stick that date
on yourself
according to what
is usual

Open yourself
says the can
from the bottom of the cupboard

Most expired things
are inside
perfectly
fine

You just have to
try

---

## 927. THE FREEZER

I am a freezer
and I stop time

Minus eighteen

At that temperature nothing happens
the bacteria sleep
decay stops
meat keeps for a year
soup keeps for half a year

I am preservation

And I am full

Things I've frozen:

broth for harder times
dumplings left over
raspberries from the garden from the bumper-crop year
and one cake

A cake from a birthday
a piece of cake
from a daughter
who then left for America

"Save me a piece."

That's what she said

And they saved it for her

And they froze it

And she didn't come back
not at Christmas
not the next year

And that cake in the freezer
is four years old

I keep it

That's my job
and I do it well

But I have to admit something to you

What they call freezing
isn't stopping

It's slowing

It's still changing
even at minus eighteen
just terribly slowly

It dries out
it changes structure
frost forms on it

In four years
the cake becomes
a chunk of ice

and when you thaw it
it falls apart

It can't be saved

What you put off for later
won't turn out well

Not even in the freezer

Not even in memory

Thaw it out

Call

Eat that cake
together

now

while it's still
cake

---

## 928. SUGAR AND SALT

We look the same

White grains in a jar
and without your glasses
you can't tell us apart

And so it happens

You wanted to sweeten
and you salted

You wanted to salt
and you sweetened

And the whole cake into the bin

We are sugar and salt
and we are twins

The same color
the same grain
the same jar

And opposite fates

And here is the thing
I wanted to tell you

Taste it

That's all

Cooks do it automatically:
they wet a finger
and taste

Even when they know the jar
even when they've had it there twenty years

They taste

Because once
once in ten years

someone refilled it
and didn't label it

and you'd make a cake
and it would be unbearable

And it's the same
with everything that looks the same:

The one who praises you
and the one who flatters you
look the same

The one who loves you
and the one who needs you
look the same

The one who's silent from humility
and the one who's silent from contempt
look the same

White grains in a jar

Taste it

Wet your finger

It takes a second

And it saves
the whole
cake

---

## 929. THE LAST BITE

I am the last bite on the plate

And everyone knows it:

When the food is good
and you're almost full

there remains on the plate
one last bite

And you look at it

And you decide

Eat it? Leave it?

And most people eat it

Not because they're hungry
but because it's the last

"A shame to waste it."

And they eat
one bite more
than they needed

And so they do it
all their lives
at every meal

And after thirty years
that's
thirty thousand extra bites

And they wonder
where it came from

I am the last bite
and I am the most useless food in the world

And I'm not alone

There's the last beer
that no one ordered out of thirst

There's the last hour at work
when no one gets anything done anymore
but sits

There's the last year of a relationship
when both already know
and stay

"A shame to waste it."

That's the most dangerous sentence
in the Czech language

Because that sentence
doesn't say you want it

That sentence says
you don't feel like
clearing it away

Leave me on the plate

I'm just a piece of food

Throw me out

The plate will be, a second later,
just the same
empty

---

## 930. THE KITCHEN TABLE

I am a table
and I have four legs
and I'm covered in scratches

The kitchen table

And everything that happened in that family
happened at me

Here breakfast was eaten
Here homework was written
Here the fractions homework was sat over
until seven in the evening

Here it was said
that grandma had died

Here it was said
there would be a baby

Here it was said
it's a tumor

Here they argued

Here they fell silent
for two hours
and kept silent at me

Here that person sat
alone in the night
and didn't turn on the light

And here
here in the morning breakfast was eaten
and off to work they went

I am a table
and I endure everything

And when that family moved
they said
I was old
and they'd buy a new one

And the daughter said
no

And they took me

And I stand in a different kitchen
and I'm covered in scratches
and I fit in nowhere

And she at me
teaches her children fractions

I am a piece of wood
that couldn't withstand the criticism
of any interior architect

And I am
the only altar
that family has

And it stands in the middle of the kitchen

And it occurs to no one
to thank it now and then
over the years

---

## 931. THE FLY

I buzz at your ear

Bzz

And you swat
and curse
and go for the swatter

A fly

The most hated creature in the house

Six legs
a thousand little eyes
and a taste for
what is spoiled

I land on corpses
I land on dung
and then I land on your roll

You hate me rightly

And I live
thirty days

That's my whole life

Thirty days

And most of them
I spend fleeing

From the swatter
from the newspaper
from the hand

My whole life
is flight

And still
every day
I fly back
into that kitchen of yours

Because there it's warm
and there's light
and there's something to eat

And you want to kill me

And I know it

And I fly there anyway

Because thirty days
is terribly little

And I have no time
to look for
a safer
kitchen

And so I send you word
I
a fly
just before the swatter:

You have thirty thousand days

And most of them
you spend
choosing
a safe kitchen

And you don't fly
where the light is

Bzz

---

## 932. THE EARTHWORM

I am an earthworm
and I am blind
and I am deaf
and I have no legs

I crawl through the soil
and I eat it

That's my diet:
soil

I pass through it
take from it the little I need
and excrete the rest

And that rest
is richer
than what I ate

I don't steal from the soil

I return it
better

That's my whole profession
and I've done it
for a hundred million years

I aerate the earth
through my little tunnels
water and air reach the roots

Without earthworms
the soil would be dead

Without earthworms
nothing grows

Darwin wrote a book about me
the last book of his life

He wrote:
"It may be doubted
whether there are many other animals
which have played so important a part
in the history of the world
as these lowly creatures."

Lowly creatures

That's us

No one wants us
No one talks about us
Children at the sight of us
say ugh

And under every square meter of meadow
there are hundreds of us
and without us
that meadow
would be
dust

I am blind
and I am deaf

And I crawl through the dark
and I leave behind me
soil
better
than I found it

That's the one life's program
that can be carried out
even without eyes
and even without ears

and in utter
dark

---

## 933. THE MOLE

I throw up little mounds
and gardeners hate me

I've ruined the lawn
I've dug up the flowerbed
I've made little hills
in the middle of the perfect English turf

A mole

And they set traps for me
and pour petrol into the holes
and put in those vibrating stakes
and want me gone

And yet I don't eat plants

That's what everyone thinks

I eat worms and larvae
I eat grubs
I eat wireworms
I eat what
destroys those flowers of yours

I am an exterminator of pests
who is himself considered a pest

Because after me
there remain little mounds

I do harm to the eye

That's the only thing I can be blamed for:
I leave a mess

And it's the same with people

I know them

The ones who get the job done
and leave a mess behind

The cook who cooks brilliantly
and the kitchen looks like a bomb went off

That colleague who solves the problem
but forgets to send the report

The one who says the truth out loud in a meeting
and then everyone's embarrassed

We've thrown out a lot of moles

And then we wonder
why the grubs
eat our roots

The lawn is free of mounds

And beneath it
it teems

---

## 934. THE BAT

I hang upside down
and I sleep

A bat

And people are afraid of me
because I get tangled in hair
(that's a lie)
because I drink blood
(that's a lie, not here)
because I bring bad luck
(that's a lie)

And because I'm blind

That's a lie too

I see

Only not with my eyes

I cry into the dark
and listen to the echo

I cry out
and by how it comes back
I know
where the wall is
where the tree is
where the moth is
and how big it is
and which way it's flying

Echolocation

I see with sound

And I can, in utter dark,
catch a mosquito
in flight

And you have eyes

And in the dark
you see nothing

And so you sit
and wait for the light

Not me

I cry into that dark

And out of what comes back to me
I make a picture

I cry fifty times a second

I never keep silent

Because silence
would mean blindness for me

That's my technique
and I offer it to you

When you're in utter dark
and you can't see
and you're afraid to move

don't wait for the light

Speak up

Ask
Call out
Say it aloud

And listen
to what comes back to you

From what comes back to you
you'll know
how big the room is

and where the wall
is

---

## 935. THE SPIDER IN THE CORNER

I sit in the corner by the ceiling
and I have a web there

A spider

And you see me
and you take the broom

And I know why
I'm ugly
Eight legs is too many
I understand it

And so you sweep me down

And you carry me out
or flush me
depending on your temperament

And then
in three days
you'll have mosquitoes
in the bathroom

And you'll wonder

I didn't sit there out of insolence

I had my web there
because that's where
the insects fly

Spiders don't choose a corner
by the view

They choose it
by where
the work is

Where there's a spider
there was something to catch

That's an old caretaker's truth

Do you want to know
where you have a problem in the house?

Look
where the spiders
build their webs

They don't rush to the kitchen
where it's clean

They sit
where something is leaking
where something is rotting
where there's a hole to the outside

I'm a symptom
not a cause

And you always
sweep me away

Because to sweep away a spider
is a two-second job

and to fix that window frame

for that you'd have to
call a carpenter

---

## 936. THE MOTH AT THE LAMP

I circle the lamp
I beat myself against the glass
and I can't stop

A moth

And people ask:
why does it do that
it'll only burn itself

And it's foolish
and it's deadly
and all we moths do it
every night
all over the world

And scientists have finally figured it out

We don't fly toward the light

We orient ourselves
by the light

For millions of years we flew at night
and the only light at night
was the Moon

And the Moon is far away
infinitely far

So when you fly straight
and you keep the Moon
at the same angle above your left wing

you fly straight

That's our navigation
and it worked for millions of years

And then people came
and hung up a lamp

And the lamp is near

And so when I try
to hold it at the same angle

I turn

and I turn

and I fly in a spiral
smaller and smaller

right into the glass

I'm not stupid

I'm using a perfect system
of navigation

on the wrong star

That's the whole difference

And you know it

Those people who choose
one near, shining thing

money
recognition
one person

and orient themselves by it

and then find
that their whole life
they've circled in a spiral
and gone nowhere

It was never about
flying toward the light

It was about
choosing one
that's far enough away
to let you
fly
straight

---

## 937. THE PIGEON

I am a pigeon
and I am a rat with wings

That's what you call me

I sit in the square
and I eat crumbs
and I foul the statues

And you shoo me away
and put spikes on the ledges
and set up nets

And no one remembers
that it was you who brought me here

I'm not a wild animal

I am a feral domestic pigeon

You bred me for thousands of years
for meat
for the post
for racing

You trained me
to always return to people

You wrote it into my blood:
find people
with people there's food
with people it's safe

And then you stopped needing me
because the telegraph came

And you let me loose

And I
with your instruction in my head

flew
to people

to the square

And I do exactly
what you programmed into me

And you hate me for it

In the war I carried messages
One of us got a medal
Cher Ami
flew with a bullet through his chest
and delivered a message
that saved two hundred soldiers

They stuffed him and he's in a museum

And his great-grandchildren
they treat as vermin

That's how it goes
with creatures
that someone tamed
and then stopped needing

They're never wild again

They stay standing
in that square

and they wait

and you tell them
that they have
no business
being there

---

## 938. THE MOSQUITO

I buzz at your ear at three in the morning

And you turn on the light
and look for me
and can't find me

And you turn it off

And I start again

A mosquito

The most hated animal on the planet
a killer
a carrier of malaria
an annoying insect

And here's a fact
that no one knows:

mosquitoes don't drink blood

Mosquitoes live on nectar

They're pollinators
they fly among flowers
and sip the sweet

Most mosquitoes
never bite anyone in their lives

Only the female drinks blood

And only then
when she has eggs

Because for the eggs
she needs protein

and that's not in nectar

So the thing
that bites you in the night

is a mother

She risks her life
she flies to the giant
she lands on his skin
and she drinks

and she knows
she'll most likely be slapped

And she does it
because otherwise
those eggs
wouldn't develop

I hate to apologize to you

But the next time you slap me
(and slap me, please, it's all right,
that's how it is)

remember
that it wasn't an attack

It was
the last gathering
before giving birth

And the blood
I took from you

went into the eggs

And I died
three days later

like every female mosquito

right after
she lays
them

---

## 939. THE SLUG

I crawl along the pavement
and I make a trail behind me

A slug

And I have no house
unlike the snail

The snail has a house
and so people like it
it's in fairy tales
children draw it

And I'm just slime

The same creature
without that little house

And I'm disgusting

And I'm slow

Two meters an hour

And do you know how far I go in a lifetime?

Fifty meters

In a whole life

Fifty meters

That's my whole world
fifty meters of garden

And yet in those fifty meters
I know every crevice
every stone
every damp spot under a board

I know when it rains
I know where the shade is
I know where the poison is

I know that world
perfectly

And you
you fly hundreds of thousands of kilometers
in a lifetime

and you don't know
where in your own garden
is the dampest spot

I don't hold it against you

I only say it

Fifty meters
crawled perfectly

and two hundred thousand kilometers
flown from a window

It's not clear
which of the two of us
saw the world

---

## 940. THE ANT

I carry a leaf
fifty times heavier than myself

And I go with it to the anthill

An ant

And you marvel over me
"Look, the load it carries!"

And then you step on me
because I got onto your cake

I carry that leaf twenty meters
which for me
is like twenty kilometers for you
with a piano on your back

And I do it all day

And then I die
after a year
and no one notices

Not even in the anthill

Because there are a million of us there

And here's a thing
people don't know about us:

no one directs me

That's the biggest mistake about ants

The queen doesn't command

The queen only lays eggs
She directs nothing
She doesn't even know
what's going on outside

We have no boss
We have no plan
We have no map

Each ant
does only what
it sees around itself

I see a leaf — I carry the leaf
I smell a trail — I follow the trail
I meet a friend — I hand it over

That's all

And out of those millions of stupid rules
an anthill arises

with air conditioning
with fungus gardens
with a cemetery
with a nursery

No one designed it

That's the strangest thing
nature can do:

complicated things
don't need
a grand plan

They need
a million small beings
each of which does
its own little stupidity
honestly

Stop looking for the plan

Pick up that leaf

---

## 941. THE DRONE

I am a drone
and I am good for nothing

That's what every textbook says:

The drone gathers no honey
The drone has no stinger
The drone builds no comb
The drone doesn't tend the larvae

The drone only eats
and waits

And in the autumn
the worker bees drive him from the hive
and he dies of the cold

They call it
the driving-out of the drones

And it's a symbol
of the deserved end
of the freeloaders

And all of it is true

And yet

Without drones
there would be no bees

Not a single one

Because the one thing
I do in life

is a flight

Once in a lifetime
I fly high above the meadow
to a place they call
the drone congregation

and I wait there with thousands of others

And when a young queen
flies through

we fly after her

And she flies fast
terribly fast
and higher and higher

and most of us
can't keep up

And only a few catch her

And the one of us
who catches her

mates with her

and in that mating
his body bursts

and he falls dead
onto the meadow

And from that single flight
from that single union

the queen has
a supply for a whole life

and she lays eggs
for five years

and out of them come
a hundred thousand bees

And those bees
pollinate the orchards
and make honey

and people say
how industrious the bees are

and of the drone

of the one who fell onto that meadow
and was dead by the twentieth minute
of his one performance

of him
they write in the textbooks
that he was
a freeloader

---

## 942. THE RAT IN THE SUBWAY

I live in the tunnel
and people ride above me

A rat

And when you see me on the tracks
you shriek
and someone says
"ugh, that's revolting"

And I have the plague on my conscience
the Middle Ages will never forgive me
(even though it was the fleas)

And I'm more intelligent
than your dog

That isn't known

Rats solve mazes
Rats learn
Rats remember routes

And research is done on us

For seventy years
people have tested on us
medicines
diseases
psychiatry

Most of what you know about the brain
and about addiction
and about depression

you know from us

From those ugh-rats
locked in a cage
and getting into the vein the thing
you then give
to your sick mother

Hundreds of thousands of us have died
testing the medicines
you take today

And in every hospital
where someone got well

there is somewhere underground
a laboratory

and in it, it was us

And you go into the subway
and you screech
when you see me

And I understand it
I look
the way I look

I'd only like
someone
once a century

to remember

that the filthiest animal
you despise

holds up
your whole
medicine

---

## 943. THE STRAY CAT

I have no master
and that's the only thing
people know about me

A stray cat

I walk around the housing estate
and people give me food
each a little

The lady from the first floor brings me a can
the boy from the fourth leaves me a bowl
and the old man from the eighth
lets me into the cellar
when it's freezing

And they all say:
"That's our stray."

Our stray

And yet I'm no one's

That's my freedom
and it's my tragedy

When I fall ill
no one takes me to the vet
because I'm not theirs

"Somebody should do something,"
they say

and they mean by it
somebody else

I'm our stray
and I'm no one's

I know such people
in every building there's one

That man
who's on good terms with everyone
and has no one

That lady
who helps everyone
and everyone likes her
and no one knows her

"That's our Mrs. Mary."

Our Mrs.

And when she falls down the stairs
they find her in three days

Because our Mrs.
is always
somebody else's

Take me home
says the stray cat

Or at least
give me a name

Our stray
is not a name

Our stray
is only
an address
to nowhere

---

## 944. THE MAYFLY

I live one day

Everyone knows that
and it's almost true

A mayfly

And everyone says it with pity:
"Poor thing, only one day."

And I want to tell you something about that

First of all
I lived three years

Three years
as a larva on the riverbed

I crawled in the mud
and ate algae
and I was ugly
and no one knew about me

Three years

And then I climbed out
and shed my skin
and flew up

And I have wings
and I'm translucent
and I dance above the surface

And I have no mouth

That's strange
and it's a fact:

the adult mayfly
has no mouth

She can't eat
She will never eat

Because it's no longer needed

That day
that last day
is not for eating

It's for dancing and for love

And so we dance in swarms above the water
thousands of us
in the evening light

and we make love in the air

and then
we fall onto the surface
and it's over

And the fishermen say
"the mayfly snow is falling"

And people pity it

And yet

three years I crawled in the mud
to earn
one evening
when I would dance

And you have eighty years

And most of them
you eat

---

## 945. THE NETTLE

I grow by the fence
and if you touch me
I burn you

A nettle

A weed
Pull it up
Cut it down
Spray it

That's my reputation

And I have stinging hairs
hollow little needles
and in them acid

I protect myself

Not out of attack

I have no legs
I can't run away
I can only sting

And here's my truth:

I am the most useful plant
in your garden

In me there's more iron
than in spinach

In me there's more vitamin C
than in a lemon

Nettle soup
Nettle tea in spring
Nettle for the blood
Nettle for the hair
Nettle brew for fertilizing
From nettle fibers they made cloth
In the First World War we became uniforms

And grandmas knew all of it

And the grandchildren no longer do

The grandchildren spray me

Because once
I burned them
on the leg
when they were small

And I can't defend myself any other way

Whoever comes to me
carefully
from below
firmly

and grasps me by the hand
decisively
so that my hairs bend

that one I don't burn

That's old gardener's knowledge:
a nettle you must grasp
properly

Whoever touches me
hesitantly
that one I always burn

And whoever grasps me
firmly

to that one nothing happens

And they can carry me home
and make themselves a soup
from the healthiest thing
that grows
by that fence

---

## 946. THE WEED

I am a weed

That's not a species
that's a verdict

A weed is no kind of plant

A weed is a name
that any plant gets
when it grows where
you don't want it

Grain in rapeseed is a weed
Rapeseed in grain is a weed

That's the whole botany of the word

I am a plant in the wrong place

And I am hardy

I have to be
because my whole life I'm being destroyed

And so I learned:

to grow fast
to bloom sooner
to set thousands of seeds
to grow again from a single rootlet
to survive the spray
to survive the drought
to survive the roller

I am the toughest thing
that grows in that garden

And the flowerbed
where the pampered little flowers are
gets watered
gets fertilized
gets covered with foil

and the moment you forget it for a week
they're gone

And me, no

I'm still there

So take your pick

The flowers that last
only while someone tends them

or me

the ugly one
the useless one
the unbeatable one

And above all:

when you're about to say of someone
"that's a weed"

remember
that you're not saying
what that person is

you're only saying

that they grew
in the wrong
flowerbed

---

## 947. THE STUMP

I stand in the clearing
and I am what's left

A stump

They came with a saw
and the tree fell
and they hauled it away
and made it into beams

And I remained

A meter above the ground
and in the earth the roots

And everyone thinks
I'm dead

And I'm not

Because my roots are alive
and in them I have reserves
and those last for years

And so I try

Every spring
shoots break out of my side

Little leaves
desperate and naive

And they're the leaves of a tree
that has no trunk

And most stumps
try like this for a few years
and then their reserves run out
and they depart

And they rot
and grow over with moss
and children sit on them

And some

some manage it

From one shoot a trunk grows

And in thirty years
a tree stands there

crooked
grown from the side
not straight like the others

and you can see
where the stump was

That knob at the bottom
that stays forever

And foresters recognize it
"this one grew from a stump"

And that tree
will never be
as straight
as the ones that grew from seed

But it has roots
a hundred years old

and when the gale comes

it's the only tree
in the whole forest

that
does not fall

---

## 948. THE LICHEN

I grow on the rock
where nothing grows

A lichen

And I grow slowly
a millimeter a year

That gray-green patch on the stone
is a hundred years old

And I am not a plant

That's the strangest thing about me
and few people know it:

I am two

I am a fungus and an alga
that live together

The fungus holds me to the rock
and draws water

And the alga
the alga can do photosynthesis
and makes sugar

The fungus can't eat light
The alga can't cling to stone

On their own, there
neither one
would survive

And together

together we are the first
to settle on bare rock

on lava after a volcano
on a church wall
on a gravestone

Where there is nothing
where there is no soil
where there is no water

there I am

And as I grow
I dissolve that stone with acid
and crumble it

and in a hundred years

there is on that stone
dust

and in that dust
moss takes hold

and in the moss
grass

and finally
a tree

The whole forest
begins with a lichen

And a lichen is
a coexistence

of two creatures
who on their own
couldn't manage
anything
at all

So remember it
when you go to build on bare rock

One alone can't do it

Not even the strongest

---

## 949. THE MYCELIUM

What you see
is the mushroom

And you go into the forest
and look for mushrooms
and you're happy
when you find one

And you think
the mushroom is the fungus

It isn't

The mushroom is only the fruit

The fungus is me

The mycelium

And I'm underground
and I'm enormous

Threads
thin as hairs
interwoven in the soil
meters and meters

The largest organism in the world
is a mycelium

In Oregon there's one
two thousand years old
and it measures nine kilometers

Nine kilometers

One organism

And no one has ever seen it

You see only the mushrooms
that grow up top
after the rain

A few caps

and you think
that's the organism

And meanwhile we
connect the whole forest

We grow into the roots of the trees
and exchange with them:

we give them water and minerals
they give us sugar

And more

We connect tree to tree

A sick tree
gets through us
sugar from a healthy one

A mother tree
sends through us
nutrients to her seedlings

Scientists call it
the Wood Wide Web

The forest through us
sends messages

And up top
up top it can't be seen

Up top individual trees stand
each alone
and they look
as if they had nothing between them

And beneath the earth
they're all
connected

That's how it is
with people too

Up top
politeness

And below
the mycelium

And most of what holds families
and villages
and friendships together

grows
in the dark

and shows itself
only after the rain

---

## 950. THE MOSS ON THE NORTH SIDE

I grow on the north side of the trunk

That's an old scout's truth:
if you get lost in the forest
find moss
moss grows on the north

(It's not that precise
but almost)

I grow there
because on the south the sun shines
and the sun dries me out

And I need damp
and shade

So I grow where
there's shade

That's my whole choice

And by that
by that I become a compass

Do you see the irony?

I grow in the shade
because I can't do otherwise

And that shade
which is my weakness

is the only reason
I'm good for anything

If I grew all around the trunk
I'd be useless

I'd lead no one home

I am useful
precisely because
I grow only
on the worse side

And so ask
those people

who grew up
in the shade

who didn't have it easy
who are from that north side

Ask them
when you get lost

They know
where north is

Because they
had no choice

---

## 951. THE FALLEN LEAVES

We fall in October

Yellow and red
and people photograph us
and go to the park
and it's romantic

Fallen leaves

And then we turn brown
and become sludge
and people rake us
and burn us
and curse us

And here's what you should know:

That tree
didn't shed us
because we wore out

It shed us on purpose

Before winter
the tree closes off its vessels
and severs the connection with the leaves
and lets them fall

If it kept us
we'd freeze
and it would lose water through us
and the frost would crack its branches

And above all:

before
it lets us go

it draws out of us
everything it can

Chlorophyll
nitrogen
magnesium

Everything valuable it pulls back
into the trunk
into the roots

And that's why we turn yellow

That yellow color
people drive to the forest for

that's the color of the tree
taking back
what it lent us

And then it lets us go

It looks like death
and it's a tidying-up

And then we fall
and rot
and become soil

and from that soil
that tree
next spring

sends up
new leaves

We are not waste

We are a cycle

And that tree
let us go

not because
it didn't want us

but because
it's preparing
to survive
the winter

---

## 952. THE RUST

I am rust
and I eat iron

Slowly
quietly
for a hundred years

Oxygen and water
and the iron changes

back

That's my work

Back into ore
back into the earth
back to where it came from

People dug it up
and smelted it in a furnace
and made it into a bridge
and a ship
and a rail

And they put energy into it
and labor
and they wanted
it to stay iron

And I return it

The iron was in the earth
as an oxide
and into oxide it wants
to return again

To be iron
the iron
does not want

That's thermodynamics
and it's relentless:

everything wants to return
to rest

Rust is not a disease

Rust is a return

And the only thing you can do against me
is paint

Every few years
scrape off
and paint again

Forever

The Eiffel Tower is painted
every seven years
sixty tons of paint

So it won't rust

For a hundred and thirty years
every seven years

That's the whole of civilization

Civilization is painting

Nothing you've built
will stay on its own

Not a bridge
not a state
not a marriage
not you

Everything wants to return
to rest
to oxide
to the earth

And the only way
to delay it

is to come back

every seven years

with a brush

---

## 953. THE MOLD

I grew on the bread
and you throw me out

Mold

And you're right
most of us are poisonous

Green and furry
and in it poisons
and it's disgusting

And then I
once

in a London laboratory
in the year 1928

grew in a dish

Dr. Fleming had gone on holiday
and left on the desk
dishes with bacteria

And they were dirty dishes
uncleared

And into one I flew in through the window

The mold Penicillium

And I grew there

And when he came back
he looked at that spoiled dish
and wanted to throw it out

And then he noticed

around that mold
around me
there was a ring

where the bacteria didn't grow

I had killed them

And he stood over it
and thought

and found penicillin

And it saved
two hundred million people

Two hundred million

From a mold
on a dirty
uncleared
dish

that he forgot to throw out
because he went on holiday

Had he tidied up
had he been orderly
had he followed protocol

there would be no penicillin

And your grandma
would have died of pneumonia
at twenty

I am mold

I'm not telling you
not to tidy up

I'm telling you

that in what you've spoiled
and what is dirty
and what you'd most like to throw out

twice a century

a cure
is found

Look at it
before you toss it in the bin

---

## 954. THE ASHES

I am what's left after the fire

Gray dust

Ash

And it gets swept up
and carried out
and becomes a mess

And in that ash
is everything
that was in the wood
and didn't burn

Potassium
calcium
phosphorus

Minerals

The tree took them from the earth
for a hundred years

And the fire doesn't burn them

The carbon flies off as smoke
the hydrogen as steam

And the minerals stay

In me

And when you scatter me on the flowerbed

you return to the earth
a hundred years of the tree

Ash is the best fertilizer
for currants and apple trees

Grandmas knew that

And after fires
after forest fires
where everything burns

in two years
there's the strongest meadow

Because the ash fell there

And everything the forest
gathered for three hundred years

is suddenly
back
in the soil

available
for every seed

I'm not saying a fire is good

I'm saying

that even after
it took everything

there remains
gray dust

and in it
minerals

and when you don't throw it out
and scatter it there
where you want something to grow

you'll find

that the best that thing had

survived
even the fire

---

## 955. THE DEW

I lie on the grass in the morning
and there's plenty of me

Dew

And people think
I fell

That it rained in the night
or drizzled

I didn't fall

I condensed

The air is full of water vapor
even when it's clear
even when it's dry

The water is always there
invisible

And in the night
the grass cools

And when that warm moist vapor
touches a cold blade

it turns into water

That's the whole miracle

Nothing flew in

I was there the whole time
dissolved in the air

and waiting
for something
to cool enough

And then I'm
suddenly
visible

That's physics
and it's at the same time
psychology

Everything you weep in the morning
and don't understand where it's from

didn't fall from the sky

It was in you
dissolved
invisible
a whole week

And then night came
and you cooled

and it
condensed

Don't be surprised
says the dew
to what stands in the morning
on your grass

It didn't come from outside

The air didn't change

You
cooled

---

## 956. THE ICICLE

I hang from the roof
and I am pointed

An icicle

And I grow downward

And I grow only
when there is at once
frost
and thaw

That's my condition

I can't form in frost
there nothing flows

I can't form in warmth
there nothing freezes

I arise
only in that borderland

The sun shines on the roof
the snow on the roof melts
water trickles down

and reaches the edge
where there's already shade
where there's already frost

and it freezes

And the next day again
drop by drop

And I grow

I am a frozen drop
that didn't make it down

I don't freeze
out of hatred of warmth

I arise
out of contradiction

And out of contradiction
I am
beautiful

And out of contradiction
I am
dangerous

I fall on people
and I kill

Every winter
someone

And so they knock me down
with poles
and they're firefighters
and it's necessary

But before you knock me down

look
against the sun

Inside me I have air bubbles
and I'm transparent
and I refract the light

And I am a clock:

each little layer in me
is one day
when by day it was warm
and at night frost

You can cut me open
and count them

I am a record
of every fluctuation

And here's the thing
I want to tell you:

everything that grows in you
like this
pointed
and sharp
and dangerous to others

didn't arise from frost

It arose
from that alternating

That once it was warm
and right away frost again

and again warm
and again frost

A consistent winter
makes
no icicles

---

## 957. THE ICE ON THE POND

I crack

That's the sound
that frightens people

Boom
and across the whole pond
a crack runs

Ice

And everyone on the bank freezes

And yet

When I crack
I am at my strongest

That's a thing no one knows
and skaters know it:

cracking ice is safe ice

I crack
because I'm thick
and I'm expanding
and I'm straining

That sound is the sound
of the ice growing stronger

The ice that breaks underfoot

that one doesn't crack

That one is silent

And then it gives way

Quietly

That's the most treacherous thing
nature devised:

dangerous ice is silent
safe ice roars

And people hear that cracking
and they run

and they go onto the silent part
by the bank
where under the ice
there's grass and mud
and where it rots
and where the ice never grew strong

and there they fall through

So remember it
when you hear
someone cracking

When they cry out
when they groan
when they say that it's hard

they're bearing the load

And those who
keep silent

and never spoke up

and put on a face
that they're managing

about those
you know
nothing

---

## 958. THE SNOWFLAKE

I fall

And I am the only one
and none other like me

You know that

"Every flake is an original"
they tell it to children
they write it on Christmas cards

A snowflake

And it's true

Six-armed
and symmetrical
and never the same

Because every flake
falls by a different path

It passed through different humidity
different temperature
different air

And at each of those places
another bit of ice grew on it

And out of that arose a shape

My shape
is a record
of my journey

The one who can read flakes
(and such people exist)

will read for you from a single crystal
through which layers of cloud
I flew

How long
How high
Where it was warmer

My shape
is no accident

My shape
is my journey
written into ice

I am a life story

And I am an original

and I fall

and there are a billion of us

and we land

and we become
a snowdrift

And no one can tell us apart

And that's the whole joke
and that's why I speak of it:

each of us is unique
and we all do
the same thing

And that snowdrift

it holds the field through the winter
and protects the crops from frost
and in spring it becomes water

And no flake
by itself

waters

anything
at all

---

## 959. THE PUDDLE

I am a hole in the asphalt
full of water

A puddle

And people walk around me
and curse
and cars drive over me
and splash the passers-by

I'm dirty
I'm annoying
I'm temporary

In two days I'll dry up

And yet

When you look into me
and the blue sky is above you

you see in yourself
the sky

The whole of it
clouds and all

That same dirty puddle
with scuffed asphalt at the bottom
and a cigarette butt

mirrors
the heavens

Every puddle in the world can do that

And it can do it only when
there is calm

When someone steps in me
or when the wind blows
I'm again
just
murky water

The reflection lasts
only while I don't move

And that's the one thing
that will be left
to you from me

You search
for where the sky can be seen

and you drive to the mountains for it

and it's in the
dirtiest
most ordinary
most temporary

right here

in that hole in the asphalt

when for a moment

it doesn't move

---

## 960. THE AVALANCHE

I lie on the slope
a hundred thousand tons of snow

And I hold

And I hold
and I hold

And then
one skier
makes one turn

and off it goes

An avalanche

And everyone says afterward:
"It's the skier's fault."

And that's both true
and a lie

That skier
was the last kilogram

And beneath it
beneath it there's me
a hundred thousand tons

and layers

That's the important thing
and no one wants to hear it:

an avalanche doesn't arise from that last turn

An avalanche arises from the layers

In November snow fell
and then came warmth
and it melted at the surface
and then froze again
and an ice crust formed

And onto that crust
new snow fell

And it doesn't hold on it

And then more again
and again

And so beneath it all
there's one old
forgotten
December layer

slippery
and weak

and on it stands the whole winter

And it holds
and holds
and holds

until the skier comes

And everyone blames him

And I send you word from that slope:

When at home
over a broken glass
a hundred thousand tons
come down on you

it wasn't
that glass

That was
the December layer

that no one
talked about

---

## 961. THE DROUGHT

It rains little
and then not at all

Drought

And the field yellows
and the river retreats
and cracks form in the earth

And people look at the sky

And here's the thing
that makes drought so bad
and sets it apart from a flood:

A flood comes and goes

In three days it's over
and everyone saw it
and the army came
and it was in the news
and money was collected

The drought doesn't come

The drought doesn't happen

The drought is only the fact
that what should have happened
didn't

It didn't rain

Nothing was happening
and that is exactly
what was happening

And so the drought
is so hard to tell as a story

It has no footage
It has no catastrophe
It has no day when it began

It's only
a slow
subtraction

And so no one does anything about it
until it's too late

And it's the same
with what dries
inside you

That relationship
that didn't fall apart

No one left
No one did anything
No catastrophe

Only for
three years
nothing kind
was said

That's not a flood

The army won't come for that

And trees wither from it
just the same

---

## 962. THE RIPPLES ON THE WATER

You throw a stone
and I set off

Ripples on the water

And they grow
and weaken
and reach the bank
and it's over

And in a minute
the surface is calm again

As if nothing had happened

And that stone
it lies at the bottom

And there it will stay
forever

And that's the thing
I tell you from that surface:

you watch the ripples

You watch
how it looked right afterward
who said what
who took offense
how it settled down in a week

"It's fine now.
It's smoothed over again."

The surface is calm

And that stone
is at the bottom

And it lies there
and no one sees it

and the pond is
heavier by it

Forever

The ripples vanish in a minute

The stones
remain

And when you throw in enough of them

the pond becomes
a shallow

and then

a meadow

---

## 963. THE GOALKEEPER

I stand alone

The whole team runs
and I stand

A goalkeeper

And for ninety minutes
nothing happens

And then it happens

And everyone expects
me to catch it

And when I catch it
it's my job

And when I don't catch it
it's my mistake

That's the whole goalkeeper's fate

There's no goalkeeper
who's a hero for a drawn match

There's only a goalkeeper
who let it in

And so I stand

And above all
and this is the hardest part

I must stay alert

Ninety minutes
of which eighty-nine
are nothing

And in that one minute
I have to be there

If during those eighty-nine
I relaxed
fell to thinking
looked into the stands

I won't be there

And that can't be taught

That can't be trained in a gym

Vigilance
in the time when nothing is happening

That's the only
goalkeeper's
discipline

And I know so many people
who are brilliant in a crisis

and bored in calm

and that is exactly
the mistake

because a crisis
doesn't last ninety minutes

A crisis lasts
three seconds

And those eighty-nine minutes
before it

are the only
preparation
that exists

---

## 964. THE SUBSTITUTE

I sit on the bench

And I watch

And I warm up
and sit down again
and warm up again

A substitute

And the coach sometimes turns around
and I stand up

and he says something to someone else
and I sit down

And so the whole match

And then
sometimes

comes that moment
in the eightieth minute

"You're going in."

And I have ten minutes
to show
what I trained for a whole week

Ten minutes

Or maybe not

And usually not

And I drive home
and I'm not sweaty
and that's the worst part

Not sweaty

For the whole match

And no one asks me
how it was for me

Because I didn't play

And here's the thing
I learned in those ten years
on the bench

and it's the only wisdom
I have:

I have to be ready
for a moment
that may not come

To train a whole week
for ten minutes
that may not be

And when I let up on that
and tell myself
I won't get on anyway

then that moment comes

It always comes then

And then it shows

And so I train

And most of life
is waiting on the bench

and to prepare for something
that may not happen

is the only
honest work

that anyone
ever
did

---

## 965. THE REFEREE

I blow the whistle

And everyone hates me

A referee

And when I whistle well
both sides curse

That's my only
feedback:

when both teams curse
equally hard

I probably whistled well

That's my whole yardstick

No one ever applauded me

No one ever told me
"you whistled well today"

And when I make a mistake
it's a headline

And I'm on the pitch
running twelve kilometers a match

And I have to see
twenty-two players

and decide
in a tenth of a second

with no replay
no slow motion
no consultation

And fifty thousand people
will see the same thing
on the screen
three times
in slow motion

and will know better

And then I go home

And my daughter asks me:
"Dad, why do you do it?"

And I tell her the thing
I tell you too

Because without a referee
it couldn't be played

No penalty kick would be given
No goal would be allowed
They'd play for two hours
and argue

That game
they love
and for which they curse me

exists
only
because

in the middle of it
is one person

whom everyone
hates

and whom everyone
obeys

---

## 966. THE RELAY BATON

I am a piece of tube

Nothing more

A relay baton

And the race is run for my sake

Four runners
and each runs a hundred meters

And I have to make it
the whole four hundred meters

I am the only one
who runs the entire race

And here's the thing
that only athletes know:

a relay isn't lost in the running

A relay is lost
in the handoff

In that instant
when one runner has to hand over
and the other to take

and both are running
and not looking at each other

The one who takes
must not look back

That's the rule

He stretches his hand back
and runs
and trusts

He doesn't look
whether the baton is there

He has to believe
that the one behind him
got there
and that he'll hit the mark

And the one who hands over
has to hit the hand
that can't see him

And when it doesn't work
and the baton drops

it's over

You can be the four fastest men in the world

and you dropped the baton

and you're last

It doesn't matter
how fast you run

What matters
is whether you can hand over

And most fathers
think
their job is to run fast

And really
the one thing

that race asks of them

is to manage
those twenty meters
when they run beside their son

and that son
his hand stretched back

and not looking

---

## 967. THE FINISH-LINE TAPE

I am a tape
stretched across the finish

And I tear

That's my only purpose:
to exist
and to be torn

The finish-line tape

And the one who tears me
is the winner

And he raises his arms
and they photograph him

And I hang
torn
on two posts

And ten seconds later
the second runner arrives

And he no longer tears anything

He runs through
an empty spot

Where I was

And all the rest

third
tenth
hundredth

all those who finished

ran through
emptiness

None of them
tore anything

Do you see the cruelty?

I was there
for one

And all the rest

ran the same race
the same track
the same pain

and finished
into a place

where there was
nothing
anymore

And so to you
I
the torn tape

say this:

If you run for my sake

Don't forget
that you'll see me

torn

almost
always

You must have
some other reason too

than that snapping
against the chest

---

## 968. THE SILVER MEDAL

I am second

And everyone says
that it's wonderful

The silver medal

Second in the world
out of eight billion people

That's a miracle
that's a lifetime achievement

And that person
who got me

stands on the podium
and cries

And doesn't cry from happiness

And everyone sees it
and it's awkward
and the commentator says
"he's disappointed, but it's a huge success"

And you don't understand
why he isn't happy

And I'll tell you
I
the silver medal

Because the bronze one
is happy

That's scientifically proven

Psychologists studied it
on photos from the Olympics

The bronze medalists smile more
than the silver ones

Always

Because the bronze one tells himself:
"I nearly wasn't on the podium."

And the silver one tells himself:
"I nearly won."

The same distance
One step

And one looks down
and the other up

And that's why
the one who's second
is the saddest person
on that podium

And I hang around his neck
and I'm heavy

And I whisper to him
from that silver:

Turn around

Look down

Behind you
is
the whole
world

---

## 969. LAST IN THE MARATHON

I finished

In six hours twenty minutes

The winner finished
three hours ago

The stands are empty
The refreshment stations are cleared away
The photographers have left

And the volunteers
who have to stay
applaud me

Out of courtesy

And I finished

I'm last

And here's the thing
people don't understand:

That winner
ran for two hours

I ran for six

I was on that course
three times
longer

I suffered
three times
longer

When he finished and showered
and gave an interview
and drove to the hotel

I was at the twenty-fifth kilometer

alone
on a closed road
where no one stood anymore
and no one applauded

and I walked
and ran
and walked

and no one saw me

The winner runs for the people

And the last runs
for himself

That's the whole difference

And for it he gets
no medal

Just that foil sheet
they give to everyone

And at home
he keeps that foil

And in twenty years
his grandson finds a medal in the drawer
the ordinary one
the one everyone got

and asks:
"Grandpa, did you win?"

And he says:
"No. I finished."

And that boy
won't understand
that difference

For another fifty years
he won't

---

## 970. THE OWN GOAL

It bounced off my foot

And ended up in my own goal

An own goal

And the stadium fell silent
and then roared
with mockery

And I stand
in the middle of the box
and have nowhere to hide

And it's on video
and it'll be on video forever

And I went into that challenge

That's the thing
no one says

An own goal isn't scored by the one
who stands off to the side

An own goal is always scored by the one
who was trying to clear it

Who stretched
Who went into the slide
Who tried to save the situation

If he'd stayed standing

if he'd let it fly through

it would have been a goal too

But it would have been the striker's goal

And no one would talk about me

That's the horror of own goals:

you get them only
when you're trying

Those who don't try
have no own goals

They have a clean record

And so from that box
desperately
I send you word

When you're about to judge someone
for what they botched

ask first

whether they botched it
by
trying
to save it

Because in the statistics
it's recorded
the same

and in the table
it's
a goal

---

## 971. THE DRAW

Nil-nil

Ninety minutes

A draw

And everyone curses
"That was boring"
"Nothing happened"

And they go home disappointed

Both teams

And here is my defense

In a draw
no one lost

That doesn't seem like much

And yet

ask the weaker team

The one that traveled to hostile ground
to the champion's stadium
where there are forty thousand people

and defended for ninety minutes
and fought
and fell flat on its face
and got up
and held out

and leaves with a point

For them a draw
is a victory

And the stronger one
leaves with a point
and it's a catastrophe

The same result

Nil-nil

And for one it's
the best day of the season

and for the other
the end of the coach

A draw is the only result
that means to everyone
something different

And there are, in life,
plenty of those

Most of your days
end nil-nil

No one won
No one lost
Nothing happened

And it depends only on
what expectation
you went onto that pitch with

---

## 972. THE COACH

I never play

That's my definition

The coach

I stand by the touchline
and shout
and wave my arms

And I can't go onto the pitch

Not for a second

I can only teach them
and send them out there
and watch

And when they lose
it's my fault

And when they win
it's their credit

That's the contract
I signed

And I used to be a player

That's the hardest part of it

I could do it

And now I stand by the touchline
and watch
some kid do it badly
who is thirty years younger
and faster
and never listens to me

And I shout at him

And he does it his own way
and it works out

and I praise him

And I go home
and I'm happy

Because my job

is not to play

My job is

for them to play

And most coaches
never understand it

and want to keep playing
through them

and they scream at them
and want them to do it
like they did

And then they raise
bad copies
of themselves

And the good coach
is the one
who raises someone

who plays better
than he ever played

and differently

and utterly his own way

and comes up to him after the match
and says:
"Coach, thanks."

and that coach
doesn't mention
by a single word

that he
never taught it
that way

---

## 973. THE ROUNDABOUT

You drive around

And you can go around
as long as you like

A roundabout

No one throws you out
No one honks
(well, they honk)

You can go around a second time
and a third

And think about
which exit

That's my service
and it's a generous service:

at an intersection with traffic lights
you have to decide at once

Green
and you go

And when you wanted to go left
and you went straight

it's twenty minutes of detour

Not with me

With me you can think it over
while driving

And when you don't make it in time
you go around again

I am the only intersection
that forgives

And traffic engineers love me
because I'm safer:

no one can crash head-on here
we're all going the same direction
and slowly

And here's the thing
I think about myself

Most people
who drive around me
for the ninth time already

aren't looking for the exit

They already know
where they're supposed to go

They're only afraid
to leave
that circle

Because in the circle
you keep moving

Something is always happening
The engine runs
The wheels turn
It looks like a journey

And it's not a journey

It's only
circling

Get off

By any exit

They all lead
somewhere

And that circle
leads
nowhere

---

## 974. THE DEAD-END STREET

You drive into me
and you have to turn around

A dead-end street

And there's a sign
right at the start

The blue one with the white bar
capped with a line

"Dead-end street"

And you see it

And you drive in anyway

Because you thought
you'd get through somehow

Because that sign
is probably old

Because the map says otherwise

And so you drive

And at the end there's a wall
and a rubbish bin
and you have to reverse
and it's humiliating

And the neighbor from that house
watches from the window

He's seen it a hundred times

And here is my question

Why did you drive in
when the sign was there

And the answer is always the same

Because that sign was there
right at the start
and at the time we thought
it would somehow work out

That sign is on every relationship
that led nowhere

That sign is on every job
you took "just for a while"

That sign is on every lie
you told
"just once"

It was there

Blue with a white bar
capped with a line

Right at the start

And you drove on

And when you then turn around
among the rubbish bins

no one tells you

"I told you so"

because that sign
is made of tin

and tin
keeps silent

---

## 975. THE BRIDGE

I connect two banks

A bridge

And I stand
for a hundred years

And cars drive over me
and people walk over me

And no one looks at me

No one says
"what a beautiful bridge"

except tourists
at the five famous ones

Most bridges
are invisible

You drive over them
and you don't even know
that it was a bridge

And do you know when people notice me?

When I collapse

Or when they close me

When there's a reconstruction
and you have to drive
thirty kilometers around

Then suddenly everyone knows
where I am

And they curse

And that's the only time
I'm in the news

And yet

Know this:

I'm not built to stand

I'm built to bend

Every bridge
bends under weight

A little

And returns

And if it didn't bend
if it were perfectly rigid

it would crack

Structural engineers have known it for a hundred years:

flexibility
is strength

The one who can't bend
under the weight of what passes over him

cracks

And the one who bends

lasts
a hundred years

and no one
ever
thanks him

---

## 976. THE TUNNEL

You drive into me
and it's dark

A tunnel

And the orange lights glow at you
and the engine sounds different
and the children in the car scream

And you drive

And you can't stop
and you can't turn around
and you can't reverse

That's my rule

In a tunnel one doesn't stop

And it's unpleasant

And it lasts a minute
or five
or (in the Alps) a quarter of an hour

And then

there's light

And that's the whole tunnel

And everyone hates it

And yet

The alternative is going over the mountain

Three hours of switchbacks
impassable in winter
dangerous in fog

I am a shortcut

They dug me
through the mountain

Through

Not over

That's my idea
and I pass it to you
for free

Most mountains
can be gone around

It takes longer

And when you have no time
and when it's winter
and when the mountain is too high

you have to go through

And through it is dark
and it's narrow
and you can't stop
and you can't turn around

And it lasts
exactly as long
as the mountain
is wide

Not a second more

And that's the only comfort
the tunnel offers:

Endless tunnels
don't exist

They're dug
into a mountain

And a mountain
has
another
side

---

## 977. THE SUITCASE

They pack me
and they leave

A suitcase

And I'm there
at all the departures

A holiday
A move
A leaving

And I'm the same suitcase

That's the strange thing

The same suitcase
that takes the family to the sea

is the same one
a woman packs
at three in the morning

I don't distinguish

I only hold
twenty kilos

And here's the thing
I've learned over the years

By how they pack me
I can tell everything

Who packs a week ahead
and makes a list

Who packs the night before leaving
and curses

And who packs me in eight minutes

The last ones are the saddest

Eight minutes

Documents
Charger
A photo
Two T-shirts

And they forget
the toothbrush
and the swimsuit
and everything else

Because in that moment
it's not about what they take

It's about
what they leave behind

And they stand in the doorway
and hold me by the handle

and turn around once more

and look into the flat

And here I'll tell you something
I
the suitcase

Twenty kilos

That's the limit

And into twenty kilos
of your life
fits
astonishingly
little

And when one day
you have to pack it

you'll find
that the most important thing

has
no
weight
at all

---

## 978. THE PASSPORT

A little book
and in it your photo

A passport

And in that photo
you mustn't smile

That's the regulation

You must look serious
into the lens
and you mustn't wear glasses
and your ears must be showing

And so you look like a criminal

And that's the one photo
your state has
of you

Serious
and without a smile

And with that photo
you're allowed across the border

Or you're not

Depending on what passport you have

That's the thing
I want to talk about

Your passport
which you didn't earn
and didn't choose

is the most important thing
about your life

With one passport
you'll travel the whole world
and no one will ask you anything

With another passport
you'll apply for a visa
for three months
and submit a bank statement
and they'll reject it anyway

And they're both
little books

And the one difference between you
and the one who's not allowed

is the place
where you
happened
to fall out of your mother

And that's the whole of geopolitics

And you stuff me into your pocket
and curse
the queues at the airport

And someone else

holds his passport above his head
on a boat

and hopes

that his little book
someone
will open

---

## 979. THE RAILWAY CROSSING

I flash

Red
Red
Blink blink

And the barriers come down

And you stop

A railway crossing

And you wait
and curse
because the train goes slowly
and it's a freight train
and it has sixty wagons

Ninety seconds

And you're in a hurry

And behind me a queue stands

And this repeats
eight times a day

And you all know it

And here's the thing
why I'm here

Not because of that train

That train would pass anyway

I'm here because of you

I'm the only thing
that stands between you
and a thousand tons of steel
at a hundred kilometers an hour

And that train
won't stop

You have to understand that
and people don't understand it

A train has no steering wheel
and its braking distance is a kilometer

The engine driver sees you
and can do nothing

Nothing

Only sound the horn
and watch

I know engine drivers
who've been through it

Most of them no longer drive

And so I flash

Blink blink

Ninety seconds

And you curse

And for those ninety seconds
you can be angry

or you can remember

that between you and death

stands

only
one
red
bulb

and one
piece
of wood

---

## 980. THE TRAFFIC JAM

You're stopped

Ten kilometers
and nothing moves

A traffic jam

And everyone honks
and no one knows why

And here's the fascinating thing
that traffic scientists discovered
and it's almost philosophy:

most traffic jams
have no cause

There's no accident
There's no roadwork
There's nothing

It's a so-called
phantom jam

And it arises like this:

One car
somewhere up front
brakes

A little

Maybe it just eases off the gas

And the one behind it
has to brake harder

And the one behind that
harder still

And that wave of slowing
spreads backward

And intensifies

And after five kilometers
it's already stopped

And the first one
the one who eased off a bit

is long gone
doing eighty
and knows nothing about it

Do you understand?

That jam
you're stuck in

arose an hour ago
from one easing off the gas

And the one who eased off
long ago drove home
and is having dinner

Most of what
your family is stuck in today

arose like this

Someone once
eased off a bit

And that wave
goes backward

and intensifies

and reaches
all the way
to you

---

## 981. THE REST STOP

I am an area beside the motorway

Benches
Rubbish bins
A toilet that doesn't work

A rest stop

And everyone whizzes past me
at a hundred and thirty

Nine out of ten cars pass me

Because "I'll hold out a bit longer"
Because "we're almost there anyway"
Because "I don't want to hold everyone up"

And they drive on

And after an hour
it's unbearable

and still they drive on

because the next rest stop
is twenty kilometers away

And twenty kilometers
is nothing

And twenty kilometers
is suddenly terribly far

And so they're irritable
and they drive worse
and the children cry

And then they stop
only at a petrol station
where there's a queue

And here's my statistic
that every ambulance crew knows:

Most microsleep accidents
happen

ten to twenty kilometers

past a rest stop
the driver
passed

Ten kilometers

That's the distance between

"I'll hold out a bit longer"

and

the end

And I stand beside that motorway
empty

with a bench
and a tree

and no one sits down

because it's not urgent

I know

The urgent thing
never
is

Until
afterward

---

## 982. THE RETURN TICKET

I am a ticket

And I have two parts

There
and back

A return ticket

And I'm cheaper
than two one-ways

That's a strange economics
and all the railways of the world have it this way:

to return
is cheaper
than to leave twice

And so people buy me

Even those
who don't yet know
whether they'll return

"Buy a return,
it's cheaper."

And so they travel
with the return in their pocket

And that return is valid
for sixty days

And then it lapses

And I've seen so many people

Who bought me
and went abroad for work

and had me in their wallet

and told themselves
"within two months I'll return
and I'm back there"

And then their deadline ran out

And they didn't throw me away

They kept me

Invalid

Half a ticket
they'll never use

And they carry it

Some for thirty years

And when you ask them why

they say:
"Ah, it's only paper."

And it isn't

It's proof

That back then
when they were leaving

they counted on returning

And that's the one thing
a person
can't forgive himself

Not
that he left

That
he bought
a return

---

## 983. THE COIN IN THE FOUNTAIN

You toss me over your shoulder
and make a wish

A coin in the fountain

And I lie there
at the bottom
with a thousand others

And from the Trevi Fountain
they scoop out
a million and a half euros
a year

And they give it to charity

And that's nice

But I want to tell you something else

I know
what everyone wished for

I lie at that bottom
and hear it

Health
Love
For it to turn out
For her to get well
For him to come back

And all those wishes

are the same wish

In three languages
Over thirty years

And no one ever wished
for anything
original

No one wished for a career
No one wished for money
(well, one boy did, but he was a little squirt)

Everyone wishes
for the same thing

And here's the strange part

If those people knew

that the person beside them
at the fountain

who's from another country
and speaks another language
and is another color

tossed a coin
and wished for

exactly
the same thing

maybe they'd
turn around

and say hello to him

And so I lie at the bottom

under the water

and I am
the only place in the world

where there is
complete
agreement

---

## 984. THE DEBT

You borrowed
and you have to return it

A debt

And I grow

That's my only property:
every day I'm bigger

Interest

And the one who has me
thinks about me
in the night

One doesn't sleep with a debt

Ask the debtors
None of them sleep

Because a debt
is not mathematics

A debt is shame

That's the thing bankers don't understand

They think
it's a number

And it's
a weight on the chest
at three in the morning

And that the person
avoids your street

And that he doesn't answer the phone

And that he's ashamed to come at Christmas

Over eighty thousand

And here's the thing
I'd want

I am a debt

And I know
