I have to be repaid

That's fair
You borrowed

But I know the other side too

I know people
who lent

and waited

and never said anything

And that debtor vanished on them

Because he couldn't repay
and couldn't say it

And after ten years
that creditor told himself:

"I don't miss the money.
I miss him."

And he called him

And told him:
"I forgive you the debt.
Come for lunch."

And that person wept into the phone

And came

And it was the best investment
that person
ever made

For eighty thousand

he bought back
a brother

---

## 985. THE WILL

I lie at the notary's
sealed

A will

And I wait
for you to die

And then they open me
and everyone sits around the table
and listens

And I determine
who gets the house
and who gets the cottage
and who gets nothing

And after that reading

that family falls apart

That's my statistic
and it's terrible

Most families
fall out over the inheritance

Not over the money

Over
what those numbers mean

"He gave you the house and me the garage.
He always liked you better."

And that's what it's all about

Inheritance isn't about property

Inheritance is the last message
about
whom Dad liked better

And that message is read
at the notary's
and there's no asking back

Because the one who wrote it
is dead

And so they interpret it themselves
for fifty years

And here's my advice
and it's the last advice
a piece of paper
will give you

Write a letter to go with it

Not to the notary

Into the drawer

Write there
why you divided it this way

Write there
that you're giving Honza the cottage
because he's the only one
who'll go there

and that it has
nothing to do
with love

Write it

Because otherwise

all that's left of you
is
a table of figures

And from tables of figures
love
can't be read

---

## 986. THE ALIBI

I was elsewhere

That's my whole content

An alibi

And I'm the most precious thing
you can have in court

When you have me
you can't be guilty

You were at the cinema
You were at work
You were at your mother's

And someone confirms it

And you're free

And here's my story

There was a man
and they accused him of theft

And he had an alibi
that night he was at his mistress's

And he couldn't say it
because he was married

And so he kept silent

And he got three years

And he served them

And his wife visited him
every month

and believed him

and waited for him

And he never told her

And then he got out

And they lived together twenty more years

And he
didn't tell her

And he died

And to this day
she tells the neighbors

about a miscarriage of justice
about how
they locked up her husband though he was innocent

And she's right

He was innocent

Of that theft

And he served three years
for something else

And it was
the only punishment

he ever
imposed
on himself

---

## 987. THE TIP

Ten percent

You leave me on the table

A tip

And it's voluntary

That's my definition
and it's the only payment in the world
that's voluntary

The bill you have to pay

Not me

Me you leave
because you want to

And here's the whole test

The waiter doesn't know
how much he'll get

It's according to
what kind of person you are

And not according to
how he served

That's the mistake

Most people think
that the tip
rates the waiter

It doesn't

The tip
rates the guest

The one who gives twenty percent
is the same person
even when the lunch is average

And the one who gives nothing
gives nothing
even after the best service

He'll always find a reason

"The soup was cold."
"It took too long."

Waiters know it

They size you up
by the first three seconds

By how you greeted them
By whether you looked at them

And they tell no one

And they have a list in their heads

Who in this town
is what kind of person

And that list is more accurate
than any
personal
file

---

## 988. THE INSURANCE POLICY

You pay me
every month

And you get nothing from it

An insurance policy

For twenty years you pay
and never anything

And then one day

your house burns down

And I build it back for you

That's my business
and it's a strange business

You pay so that
nothing happens

No
Not quite

You pay so that
you don't have to be afraid

For peace of mind

And peace of mind has no value
until you have it

And then
when that night comes
when your roof is on fire

and you stand in the garden
in your pajamas
and watch

there's only one question
you ask yourself

And it's not
where will we live

That one comes in the morning

That night
you ask another

Is everyone out?

And if they are

then you're

insured

everything else
is
bricks

---

## 989. THE STATUTE OF LIMITATIONS

Twenty years have passed

And they can no longer try you

The statute of limitations

The law says:
after some time
it gets
erased

Not that it didn't happen

It happened

But society said:
we won't pursue it anymore

We have to move on

And it's practical
because the evidence has vanished
and the witnesses have forgotten
and that person
is already someone else

And so the statute of limitations
is the generosity of the law

And I am that statute of limitations

And I have one flaw

I run out
before the court

But I don't run out
inside

That person knows it

Twenty years
thirty years

And in the night he knows it

And no law
will forgive him
for it

And so he walks around
untriable

and pursues himself

And the only limitation
that works

is the one given to you
by

the one
you did it
to

And that can't be enforced
in court

That can only
be
begged for

---

## 990. THE SCHOOL BELL

I ring

And everyone jumps up

The school bell

Forty-five minutes
and then I ring

And the children are free

And here's my strange observation
over these fifty years:

That bell at the end of the lesson
is the loveliest sound
the children know

And the same bell
five minutes later

is the worst sound
they know

The same tone

And everything depends on whether
something is beginning
or ending

And I've learned over these fifty years
to recognize
a good teacher

It's not by the grades
It's not by the discipline

I recognize him by
what happens
when I ring

With a bad teacher
the children are already packed up
a minute ahead

They sit on the edge of the desk
with their bags in hand

And when I ring
they're gone

With a good teacher

I ring

and no one moves

And he says:
"So, more tomorrow."

And they have to
gather themselves slowly

Because they were
in the middle of something

That's the whole of pedagogy

It can't be measured with a test

It's measured with a bell

---

## 991. THE HEART CARVED INTO THE DESK

M + J

Carved with a compass
into the wood

Seventh desk by the window

And it's been there
thirty years

And that desk was revarnished
twice

And it still shows

Because whoever carves into wood
with a compass
carves deep

And I carry that heart

I am a school desk

And those two

he's a plumber in Pilsen today
and she teaches in another town

and they haven't spoken to each other
for twenty-nine years

It was three months
in the ninth grade

Three months

And then the holidays came
and he went to trade school
and she to grammar school

and that was that

And here's the thing

Into that desk
nothing was carved
of what came afterward

No wedding was carved
No children were carved
No divorce was carved
No mortgages were carved

All of that was more important
and lasted longer

And nothing remained of it
in any wood

And M + J remained

Carved with a compass
by a fifteen-year-old boy

who at the time
had no words for it

and had a compass

---

## 992. THE BACK DESK

I sit at the back

The back desk

And that's a tactical decision

Whoever sits at the back
isn't seen

Whoever sits at the back
can sleep
draw
pass notes

Whoever sits at the back
isn't called on

That's what they think

And I'll tell you
who has sat in the back desks
over these hundred years

Firstly
those who don't want to be seen
because they're bored

Secondly
those who don't want to be seen
because they don't understand

and are afraid
it'll show

And those second ones

they're the problem

Because the first ones
fail and it doesn't matter
and they become entrepreneurs

And the second ones

they hide

And the less they understand
the further back

And the further back
the less they understand

And that's a spiral

And they're pulled out of it
by only
one
thing

That someone
notices them

That the teacher walks through the class

and reaches all the way to the back

and stops at that last desk

and doesn't say
"sleeping again"

but says

"Show me what you've got. Aha. Here you've got it right."

And that boy at the back

who for twenty years thought
he was stupid

lifts
for the first time in his life

his head

---

## 993. THE PAGE TORN FROM THE NOTEBOOK

They tore me out

And I'm frayed at the edge

A torn-out page

And it happened
because I was spoiled

There was a mistake on me
There was a blot on me
There was a bad grade on me

And so they tore me out
and crumpled me
and I became a ball

And the notebook looks clean again

Except that
the page numbers in it
no longer match

And when the teacher leafs through it

she sees it

She always sees it

Where the torn-out page is

Because that little strip remained
by the spine

That tiny strip of paper
that can't be torn out

And it stays there

forever

And so you can't pretend
it didn't happen

To tear out
does not equal
to erase

To tear out
means

to leave a strip there
and a missing page
and the other numbers
shifted

And that notebook
can be filled in further

And it can be filled in even with the hole

But it can't be
made

as if that page
had never been there

Leave it there
says the torn-out page

Leave it there
blot and all

It'll look worse

And that notebook
will be
whole

---

## 994. THE EMBARRASSMENT

You said it wrong

Or you tripped

Or you waved
at someone who was waving
at someone behind you

Embarrassment

And your body
does what it always does:

you turn red

And that's the worst part
because it shows

And you'd like to sink through the floor

And here's my statistic
and it's the most merciful statistic
there is:

in three days
no one
will remember it

No one

Except you

You'll replay it
for ten years

At three in the morning
in bed

"Why did I say that."

And those others
who you're convinced
are talking about you

aren't

Because they're replaying
their own embarrassments

At three in the morning
in their own beds

That horror
you feel about
what others think of you

is based on the assumption

that they think about you
at all

And they don't

They're thinking
about how they tripped

two years ago

at the wedding
of your sister

which you
no longer
remember

---

## 995. THE HABIT

I am not love

Let's clear that up right away

Habit

And people speak ill of me

"They're only together out of habit now."

That's a verdict

It means: it's nothing anymore

And I want to tell you
from my shabby armchair
something

I am the one
who's there
every day

Love comes and goes
and returns
and goes again

Love has moods

I don't

I put the kettle on in the morning
for two cups

Even when we quarreled yesterday
Even when we don't love each other
Even when it's stupid

Two cups

Because that's how I do it
for thirty years

And that second cup

that second cup stands on the table
even in the worst week of the marriage

And she comes
and sits down beside it

Because it's there

And out of that
out of the cup that stood there
even when there was no reason

it
sometimes

comes back

I am not love

I am what holds the house together

while love

is just now
somewhere
else

---

## 996. THANK YOU

I am the shortest word
that can be said

And the hardest

Thank you

And everyone knows me
and everyone uses me

Thanks for the salt
Thanks for the ticket
Thanks, bye

A thousand times a day
and it means nothing

And then

once in a lifetime

someone says me
for real

And it looks different

They say it slowly
They say it looking into your eyes
And usually while doing so
they can't speak

And here is my point

I get said too late

That's my tragedy
and there's nothing to be done about it

Most people say me
only at the grave

Or in a eulogy
Or in a letter
they write
and then read
at the urn

"I never told you this, Dad."

And I stand at that grave
the shortest word
that can be said

and I am
fifty years
too late

And yet it's enough
to write me in a message

Eight letters

It doesn't have to be a ceremony
It doesn't have to be a conversation
It doesn't have to be a scene

Eight letters
and a name

"Thank you, Mom."

And she won't write back
because she'll be startled

And then she'll read it
fifty times

and keep it in her phone
until the end

And this is the last poem
of this hundred

And all those hundreds before it
were about things
that speak
instead of people

About a candle and about salt
about an ant and about a bridge
about a sourdough starter and about a scar
about a rusty fence
and about last year's preserves

And they all said
the same thing

Always the same

That what
holds the world together
is mostly
invisible

and that it never
speaks up
on its own

So

Before you close this little book

write
to someone

eight
letters

---

## 997. PENELOPE

I weave

And at night I unravel it

Twenty years

That is my whole part
in that enormous epic
about my husband

He sailed
he fought
he met nymphs and monsters
and there were twenty-four cantos about him

And I wove

And I unravelled

And everyone thinks
I did it for his sake
to hold off the suitors
until he came back

And I will tell you the truth
that Homer never wrote down:

I wove
because I had to do something

Twenty years of waiting
cannot simply be
sat through

I had to have work
that would never end

If the shroud had been finished
I would have had to choose a suitor
and I did not want that

but above all

if the shroud had been finished
I would have had to sit
and think

And I did not have the strength for that

Weaving by day
unravelling by night

That is the only way
one can survive
the waiting

To do something
that leads nowhere

and that keeps the hands busy

Ask any woman
who waited for a man from the war
from the sea
from the hospital

They were all knitting something

Nobody wanted the sweater

---

## 998. ARGOS

I lay on the dung heap
before the gate

I was old
full of fleas
and nobody looked after me

Argos

Odysseus's dog

And he went off to Troy
when I was a puppy

And I waited

Twenty years

And he came

Disguised as a beggar

And nobody knew him

Not his wife
not his son
not his servants

Nobody

He was old and ragged
and he had a stranger's face

And I lifted my head

I could not get up
I had no strength left

So I only lifted my head
and wagged my tail

Once

And I died

That is my whole part
twenty lines in the entire epic

I knew him first
and I knew him alone

And do you know why?

Because they recognized him
by the scar
by the bow
by the old stories

By evidence

And I knew him
by his scent

And that does not
in twenty years

change

---

## 999. THE SIRENS

We sing

And the sailors turn
and steer toward us

and they break upon the rocks

The Sirens

And Odysseus tied himself to the mast
and plugged the rowers' ears with wax

and sailed past

And everyone thinks
it was about the music

That we sang so beautifully

We did not sing beautifully

We sang
whatever the one who was listening
needed to hear

That is our trick

We never had a single song

Each man heard something different

One heard his mother
One heard the child that had died on him
One heard the woman who had refused him
and who was now saying yes

And Odysseus?

Odysseus heard the thing
he wanted to hear most in the world

He heard us singing
about how famous he was at Troy

He heard his own praise

And so he strained against the ropes
and screamed at the rowers to turn around

And they had wax in their ears

It was not his reason that saved him

What saved him
was that his men

at that moment

were not listening to him

---

## 1000. CASSANDRA

I see the future

And nobody believes me

That is my gift
and my curse
in a single package

Apollo gave me prophecy
and when I refused him
he could not take the gift back

So he added to it:
you will be right
and no one will heed you

Cassandra

And so I stood on the walls
and cried out:

That horse is a trap
Do not bring it inside
Burn it

And they laughed
and stroked the wood
and said,
"There she goes, hysterical again"

And I screamed
until my voice cracked

And they hauled it inside

And Troy fell
and they dragged me off into slavery
and there they killed me

And here is my question
that no one has answered:

If they had believed me

would the prophecy
have been true?

Or would it not have come to pass
and everyone would have said
I had been wrong?

You see the trap

A prophet who is believed
is immediately
mistaken

A prophet who is right
is always the one
they did not heed

And that is why in every family
in every company
in every country

there is one Cassandra

And she has been saying it
for two years now
at every dinner

And she does not do it
to spoil your meal

She does it
because she is screaming

into the last
wooden
horse

---

## 1001. ANTIGONE

My brother lies before the walls

And he may not be buried

The king forbade it
because he was a traitor

And I go to bury him

Antigone

And I know
they will shut me in a cave for it
and leave me to die

And I go

And everyone asks me:
why

He is dead
It makes no difference to him now
You will live
and he will not

And I am not doing it for him

That is the thing
no one understood

I am not doing it for the dead man

I am doing it
so that I can go on
being myself

There is the king's law
and there is the other one

The one nobody wrote
and nobody approved
and yet everyone knows

The one that says
you throw earth over a brother

And if I obey the king

I will live

and I will be nobody

And so I go

with a handful of earth

and it will take
three minutes

and it costs
my life

And for two and a half thousand years
students at their exams have asked

whether I should have obeyed

And half of them always say
that I should have

and they are
the future
civil servants

---

## 1002. ORPHEUS

I sang so
that the stones would move

And she died

Eurydice

And I went down into the underworld
and I played
and Hades wept

and said:

You may take her back

But you must not look around
until you come out into the light

And I went

And she walked behind me

And I could not hear her

Souls have no footsteps

And I walked through that darkness
for an hour
and behind me was darkness
and silence

And I did not know
whether she was there

And I thought:
they have tricked me
they laughed at me
I am walking alone

And just at the exit
when the light was already on me

I turned around

And she was there

And I saw her face
for that one second

and then it took her back

Forever

And everyone tells it
as a story about impatience

And really it is a story about mistrust

It was not that
I could not bear to wait

It was that
I did not believe
she was following me

when I
could not hear her

And so I turned
to make sure

And with that I lost her

Exactly the way
you lose
every one of them

---

## 1003. EURYDICE

I want to tell you
my version

Nobody asked me

I had been dead a month

And in the underworld

it is not what you think

It is not torment

It is peace

For the first time in my life
nothing hurt me
for the first time I had to go nowhere
for the first time I did not have to be
Orpheus's wife

And then I heard that music

And everyone was weeping
and Hades said yes

And I had to go

Had to

Nobody asked me
whether I wanted to come back

And so I followed him
through that darkness
upward

And I thought

about that house
and about that fame
and about how he never listened to me
because he was always composing

And he walked ahead of me
and said nothing

And then

just before the light

he turned around

And I
went back
down

And you think
it was a tragedy

And I will tell you only this much

that in the instant
he turned around

I smiled

---

## 1004. YORICK

I was a jester

I carried that boy on my back
and he pulled my ears
and laughed

Yorick

And then I died
and they buried me

And thirty years later
a gravedigger dug me up
and handed me to that boy

who was a prince now
and was sad

And he held me in his hand
my skull

and said:
"I knew him, Horatio."

And over me he delivered
the most famous speech about death
in all of literature

And everyone remembers
that skull

And nobody remembers
that I was a jester

That I had a job
making people laugh

That I was probably the only one
in the whole castle
who was not scheming

And out of my whole cheerful existence

this is what remained:

to be a prop
in a meditation on death

That is the fate of all the funny ones

While they live
they are priceless

And then

they become
a memento

And that boy
who laughed at me

figured it out

only
with my skull
in his hand

---

## 1005. HAMLET

To be or not to be

That is the question

And I work at it
for five acts

Hamlet

And in the meantime

in the meantime Polonius dies
in the meantime Ophelia goes mad
in the meantime Ophelia dies
in the meantime my mother dies
in the meantime Laertes dies

And in the end I die
and there are seven of them on the stage

Seven corpses

And all of them
every single one

would be alive

if I had done it
right in the first act

The ghost told me:
your uncle killed your father

And I had to verify it

I staged a play
I watched his face
I pondered
I hesitated
I wrote a monologue

And in the meantime he
went on ruling

And everyone calls it
psychological depth

And I will tell you
what it was:

It was fear

And I wrapped it up nicely
in philosophy

To be or not to be

It sounds so lofty

And it means only:

not yet
today
no

---

## 1006. OPHELIA

I drowned

And they paint me
floating in the water
with flowers in my hair

and it is said to be beautiful

Ophelia

And the most famous painting

shows me dead
and calm
and pretty

And I want to say
one thing about it

In the whole play

in five acts

I speak
a hundred and seventy lines

And of those

most are answers
to men's questions

My father tells me what to do
My brother tells me what to do
Hamlet shouts at me to go to a nunnery
The king uses me as bait

And nobody
not even once

asks me

what I want

And then I go mad

And everyone says
poor thing
it broke her

And I will tell you
what broke me

It was not love that broke me

What broke me
was that in the whole play

not even once

did I ever
get a word in

And so I sang to myself

That was the only thing
I had left

little songs

and flowers

and water

---

## 1007. LADY MACBETH

These hands

I keep washing them

And the stain is still there

"Out, damned spot."

Lady Macbeth

And everyone says
how strong I was

How I pushed him
How I goaded him
How I told him
he was no man
if he did not do it

And I really did do that

And he killed him

And then

And then I found out the thing
I had not expected:

He was fine

He got used to it

He went on murdering
and slept

And I did not

I egged him on
and he carried it out

and it was his body
and his hand
and his knife

And his hands were clean

And mine

my hands
which never touched
that knife

are dirty
forever

And that is the thing
I would like to pass on

to everyone
who ever pushed someone
into something

who said,
"do it, or you're no man"

who never did it themselves

That blood on your hands

is yours

The one who did it
sleeps

---

## 1008. JULIET

I am thirteen

Almost fourteen

Somehow that gets lost
in the productions

I am played by actresses
who are thirty

Juliet

And that story

that story lasts
four days

Four days

We met on Sunday
and by Friday we were dead

And everyone calls it
the greatest love in history

And I will tell you something

It was the greatest love
because it did not last long

If I had lived to thirty

and been with him
for fifteen years

and he snored
and cursed my family
(and he would have had reason to)

and we were worn out
by children and by money

nobody would have written
anything
about us

Our love is famous
because it was cut short

That is the only way
to get
into the famous loves

And all those who
stayed together forty years

and passed each other the coffee in the morning
and held hands at sixty
in the doctor's waiting room

about them
the theater
writes nothing

and they had
forty years
more

---

## 1009. CALIBAN

You taught me to speak

And the only thing I gained by it

is that I know how
to curse

Caliban

It was my island

I lived here
before you sailed in

I showed you
where the fresh water is
where the fruit grows
where to hide from the storm

And in return you gave me
language

and slavery

And you call me
a monster

And you say
that I am ungrateful

And your daughter is afraid of me

And I want to tell you
just one thing

What looks like civilization

is sometimes
only

the one
who arrived
second

and had
better
books

---

## 1010. FALSTAFF

I am fat
I am drunk
I am a coward

Falstaff

And I am the best friend
of the future king

And we go to the tavern together
and we lie
and we laugh
and he is young
and I teach him

everything one should not do

And it is the best time
of his life

He knows that

And then his father dies

And he becomes king

And I push through the crowd
at the coronation

and I call out to him
by name
as always

"Hal! My boy!"

And he turns

And he looks at me

And he says:

"I know thee not, old man."

And he walks on

And I stand in that crowd
and the people around me laugh

And I will die within a few months

And the doctor will say
it was the heart

And the innkeeper will say
it was the heart

And they both
meant
the illness

And I tell you
from that crowd:

When you are climbing

and you have to
cut someone off

because he does not
fit
the new role

Remember
that it works

It worked

He was a splendid king

And I died

---

## 1011. CORDELIA

Father asked:
How much do you love me?

And my sisters
let loose

Above everything in the world
Above eyesight
Above space
Above freedom

Words
words
words

And then he turned to me

And I said:

"Nothing."

Cordelia

And he asked again

And I said:
I love you
as is fitting
as a father
no more
no less

And he disinherited me

And gave the kingdom to those two
who had lied

And they then
drove him out into the storm

And I want
to explain to you
why I said it

Not out of pride

And not because I did not love him

I loved him more than they did
both of them together

But he wanted it
to be spoken

Out loud
before the court
in a contest

And I knew

that what can be spoken in a contest
is no longer
love

And so I said nothing

And it cost me
a kingdom
and my life

And he understood it

At the end
when he held me dead in his arms

He understood it

Four acts
too late

Like all fathers

who ask

"Do you love me?"

instead of asking

"How are you?"

---

## 1012. IAGO

I destroyed him

I talked him into believing
that his wife was betraying him

And he strangled her

And then killed himself

Iago

And at the end of the play
they ask me

why

And I do not answer

That is the most famous thing
about me

I never say it

"From this time forth I never will speak word."

That is my last line

And for four hundred years
the scholars have been guessing

Was it jealousy?
Was it the promotion?
Was it race?
Was I in love with him?

And none of those theories
fits

Because there was no reason
at all

That is the thing
people cannot bear

They want a motive

Because a motive
can be understood

and what can be understood
can also be guarded against

And I did it

because I could

because it was so easy
to whisper

because it was enough
to drop a hint three times

Look at that handkerchief

That is all

And you keep looking for
my great reason

And don't you have in your office

that person
who just
now and then
drops a hint?

He has
no reason
either

---

## 1013. PROSPERO

I can do magic

I command the storm
I command the spirits
I command that island

Prospero

And at the end of the play

I break my staff
and throw my book into the sea

and give up everything

And everyone says
how generous it is

That mighty sorcerer
gave up his power

And I will tell you
why I did it

Not out of wisdom

I did it
because I
looked at my daughter

Miranda

She was fifteen
and had never seen another human being
but me

Because I protected her
with that magic of mine

I kept her on the island
where I was god

and ran everything

and knew everything better

and she was safe

And then I saw
how she looked at that boy

And it dawned on me

that if I did not break the staff

she would stay here

with me

on my perfectly
appointed

island

forever

And so I broke it

Because love
is the only magic

that is worked in such a way

that all the other magic
stops
being worked

---

## 1014. DON QUIXOTE

They are giants

You say
they are windmills

I see giants

Don Quixote

And I charge at them
with a lance

and that blade knocks me down
and I break my ribs

and Sancho runs up
and lifts me
and says
"I told you so, they were windmills"

And I answer him
that they were giants
and a sorcerer turned them into windmills

And he shakes his head

And everyone has laughed
for four hundred years

The madman

And then

at the end of the book

I recover

Sense comes to me

And I say
that they were windmills
and that tales of chivalry are nonsense
and that I was a madman

And my friends stand around the bed

And do you think
they are glad?

That they are cheering
that the old madman
has finally come to his senses?

No

They weep

Sancho weeps most of all

And he begs me

"Do not be sensible.
Get up.
Let us ride out for another adventure.
I will find you those giants."

And I die

sensible

And they remain

in a perfectly
real

and perfectly
empty

world

---

## 1015. SANCHO PANZA

I know they are windmills

I have known from the start

Sancho Panza

I am not stupid
I am a farmer
I have a wife and children
and I have seen windmills
a thousand times

And I ride with him

Eight hundred pages

Behind that madman

And everyone says
I am there for contrast

That I am the realist
who keeps the idealist grounded

That I am comical
with my donkey
and my proverbs

And I will tell you
why I rode with him

He promised me an island

That is what I say out loud

And it is not the truth

I rode with him

because at home I was a farmer

and with him

with him I was
a squire

And he spoke to me
as to a knight

And he was the only person
in all of Castile

who spoke with me

as if I were
somebody

And so I knew they were windmills

and I never really
talked him out of it

Because the moment
he believed me

I would be
a farmer
again

---

## 1016. ROCINANTE

I am a horse

An old gaunt horse
with jutting ribs
and hooves
that no longer hold

Rocinante

And he chose me

Out of all the horses

And he searched for a name for me
for four days

Four days he pondered
over a name
for this old nag

And he came up with Rocinante

Which means
"the one that used to be a nag"

And he added:
"Now she is first among all the horses of the world."

And he mounted

And we rode

And I was slow
and I stumbled
and I always fell
when a gallop was needed

And he never beat me

Not once

In eight hundred pages

He praised me

Before strangers
he spoke of my noble ancestry

And I was a nag
from a village
they had wanted to send to the slaughterhouse

And here is my point
and it is the whole novel
in a single sentence:

He was mad

He saw giants in windmills
He saw a lady in a serving girl
He saw a knight in himself

And he saw
the first horse of the world

in me

And the only one of those illusions

that really

came true

is
me

Because I really did
go all the way
with him

to
the very end

---

## 1017. EMMA BOVARY

I read novels

That is my whole disease

Emma Bovary

I read about passion
about balconies
about confessions in the moonlight

And then I married
a country doctor

who was kind

And that is the worst thing
that could have happened to me

If he had been cruel
I could have hated him
and it would have been dramatic

And he was kind

And he ate his soup and smacked his lips
and had muddy boots
and fell asleep at the table
and he loved me

And he loved me
in the most ordinary way

which was in no novel
at all

And so I found myself a lover

And he was exactly like out of a book

And after six months he left me
with a letter
in a basket of apricots

Because lovers out of books

do that

And I poisoned myself

And doctors write about bovarism
as a disease

Wanting more
than there is

And I will tell you only this much:

The novels did not kill me

What killed me
was that I read them

and then
I looked around

and could not

in that muddy
kind
lip-smacking man

read

even a single
page

---

## 1018. ANNA KARENINA

I threw myself under a train

Everyone knows that

Even those who never read the book

Anna Karenina

And the book has a thousand pages
and it is a great novel about love

And I want to tell you
what is really
in that novel

I left my husband
for Vronsky

And by that I lost my son

That is the price

Not my reputation
Not my standing

My son

I was not allowed to see him

And then I lived with Vronsky

and I was happy
for three months

And then I began to be jealous

Because he could go out
and I could not

He could go to the club
and to the officers
and into society

And I was
a fallen woman

and I could sit at home

And so I waited
for him to come

And I counted the hours

And I made scenes

And he was tired

And here is the novel:

It is not a story
about forbidden love

It is a story
about two people
who did the same thing

And one of them
paid for it

everything

and the other

nothing

---

## 1019. RASKOLNIKOV

I killed an old pawnbroker

With an axe

And beforehand I wrote an article

about how there exist people
who have the right
to cross the law

Napoleon

And that I am one of them

Raskolnikov

And that old crone
was a louse

She bled the poor dry
She lent at usurious rates
Nobody would miss her

And I was to take that money
and use it for good things

And to kill one louse
for the sake of a thousand people

That surely makes sense

And so I went

And I killed her

And then

then her sister came home

Lizaveta

And she was simple and good
and had never done anyone any harm

and went from flat to flat and sewed

And she saw me

And I killed her too

Without a thought

So that she would not be a witness

And about that
about this second murder

my article
said nothing

And that is the whole theory of the superman
and every theory of the greater good

They always work out
on paper

And there is always
someone else in that flat

one extra

whom
the theory
did not know about

---

## 1020. SONYA

I sell myself

To feed
my father's children

Sonya

And he
that student
comes to tell me
that he has killed two women

And he waits
to see what I will do

He waits for me to condemn him
or to start being afraid of him
or to turn him in

And I
bowed down

to the ground

And I kissed his hand

And he asked why

And I said:

"Because there is no one in the whole world
more unhappy now
than you."

And then I told him:

Go to the crossroads
Kneel
Kiss the earth you have defiled

And then confess

And he did it

And he got eight years in Siberia

And I went with him

A prostitute
following a murderer
to Siberia

And in Siberia I washed
and sewed
and carried food to him

And it took him a year

a whole year

before he understood

And then on the riverbank
he knelt and embraced my knees

And that is where the novel ends

And everyone comes away from it
with such an uplifted feeling

And I want to tell you
one thing about it

It was not forgiveness that saved him

He got forgiveness
that very first evening

What saved him

was that I went
with him

to that
Siberia

---

## 1021. PRINCE MYSHKIN

I am good

That is my whole diagnosis

Prince Myshkin

Epilepsy and goodness

And I arrive in Petersburg
from treatment in Switzerland

and I am naive
and I trust people
and I say what I think

and everyone makes fun of me

And then they find out
that I understand them

And they begin to confide in me
their sorrows

And I forgive them

All of them

The one who deceived me
The one who took my woman
The one who wants to kill me

I forgive them all

And Dostoevsky wrote me as
"a beautiful man"

He wanted to write
Christ in Petersburg

And do you know how the book
ends?

Nastasya is dead
Rogozhin is a murderer
Aglaya is ruined

And I

I go mad

And I return to that Switzerland
to the sanatorium

And I sit there
and say nothing
and recognize no one

A complete idiot

And this is the most terrible sentence
anyone ever wrote about goodness:

I was perfectly good

and I did not save
a single one of them

And that novel is called
The Idiot

And not because
I was stupid

But because in this world

the word
good

and the word
idiot

are almost
the same

---

## 1022. THE GRAND INQUISITOR

Christ returned

To Seville
in the sixteenth century

And I recognized him

And I locked him up

The Grand Inquisitor

And I came to him at night
into the cell

and I spoke
and he was silent

And I said to him:

You gave them freedom

And they do not want it

You said:
follow me freely

And they want
someone to tell them
what to do

You refused to turn stone into bread
and you said
that man does not live by bread alone

And they want bread

So we gave it to them

We took their freedom
and gave them bread and certainty
and told them
what is sin and what is not

And they are happy

They are like children

And you
you laid such a burden on them

that they cannot carry it

And so I corrected it

And if you come back
tomorrow I will burn you

And he was silent

And then he stood up

and came over to me

and kissed me on the lips

And I opened the door

and said:
"Go, and do not come back."

And he left

And I
remained

with that
kiss
on my lips

and with the whole
happy
well-fed

flock

---

## 1023. JEAN VALJEAN

I stole a loaf of bread

For that, nineteen years in the galleys

And then I came out

and nobody would feed me
and nobody would let me sleep

because I had a yellow passport

a convict

And one bishop let me in

He gave me supper and a bed

And in the night I
stole his silver

And ran

And they caught me

And brought me back to him

And he looked at me
and said to the gendarmes:

"But I gave it to him as a gift."

And then he added:

"My friend, you forgot the candlesticks."

And he gave me
the candlesticks too

The most precious ones

And when the gendarmes had gone
he said to me quietly:

"With this silver I have bought your soul.
I give it to God."

Jean Valjean

And I then lived forty years

I was a mayor
I built a factory
I saved a child
I raised her
I carried a wounded man through the sewers

And all the good
I ever did

every day
for forty whole years

comes

from a single man

who decided

not to tell
the truth

about me

to the police

---

## 1024. JAVERT

The law is the law

He stole bread
He was punished
He broke parole
He must go back

That is all

Javert

And I hunted him for thirty years

And I was just
I never took a bribe
I never wronged anyone
I never lied

I served the law
My whole life

And then

on the barricade

the revolutionaries caught me

and they were to shoot me

And he
Valjean

took charge of me
and led me into an alley

and I waited for the shot

And he cut the ropes

and said:
"You are free. Go."

And he gave me his address

In case I should want to arrest him

And I stood in that alley

And my whole world

collapsed

Because if a criminal

can be better

than I am

then the law

is only
paper

And for thirty years I

served
paper

And I went to the Seine

And jumped

Not out of shame

Out of the fact
that the thought

would not fit
inside my head

And it is the saddest death
in all of French literature:

I was killed
by a stranger's
kindness

---

## 1025. GAVROCHE

I sing

And I gather cartridges

On the barricade the ammunition ran out
and outside
on the paving
among the dead soldiers

their pouches are full of them

And so I climbed out

Gavroche

And I am eleven

And I crawl among the corpses
and empty their pouches
into a basket

and I sing to myself

And they shoot at me

And I hide behind a corpse
and stick out my tongue

and go on singing

It is a game

And the soldiers cannot hit
such a small boy

And I keep getting up

and singing

And then one bullet

And I fall

And the song

stays unfinished

in the middle
of a line

And do you know what is worst about it?

That I did not do it
for the revolution

I did not understand
a single word
of what those students were saying

I climbed out there

because the cartridges
needed gathering

and because I was
the smallest

and because with me
at last
someone

was counting

For the first time
in my life

---

## 1026. QUASIMODO

I ring the bells

Sixteen bells
and I love them

They have names
and I talk to them

Quasimodo

And from that ringing
I went deaf

That is the thing
people forget

I am deaf

From the very thing
I love

And so I stand in the tower
and see Paris
and hear nothing

And people shout at me from below
and I do not understand them

And they think
I am stupid

And then Esmeralda

She gave me a drink

When I was at the pillory
and everyone was spitting on me

that gypsy girl came

and gave me water

And no one had ever given me anything

And I loved her

And she loved that captain
who betrayed her

And I saw it
from the tower

all of it

And I heard nothing

And when they hanged her

I went into the crypt
and lay down beside her

and died

And two hundred years later

during excavations

they found two skeletons
entwined

And when they tried to separate them

one of them
crumbled
into dust

And it was
mine

---

## 1027. MISS HAVISHAM

I stopped the clocks

At twenty minutes to nine

That was the minute
when I received the letter

That he was not coming

The wedding letter

Miss Havisham

And I had on
my wedding dress

And one shoe

I had not managed to put the other on

And so I stayed in them

Fifty years

I never took them off

That cake on the table
went moldy and covered with cobwebs

And I sat beside it

Fifty years

And I raised a little girl
Estella

And I raised her
so that

she would break men's hearts

So that she would hurt them

For me

That was my program

And she carried it out to perfection

And when I saw
how that boy Pip suffered

because of her

because of me

it dawned on me

Not that I had ruined her

That would still have been survivable

It dawned on me

that for those fifty years

I had sat
in that dress

and waited

for someone

who long ago
had not come

and in the meantime
out of that waiting

I had made
a career

And then I burned

Those old clothes
caught fire from the hearth

And it was worth it

At least I finally
got up
from the chair

---

## 1028. SCROOGE

Humbug

Christmas is nonsense
and let the poor go to the workhouse
and if they die
they decrease the surplus population

Scrooge

And three ghosts came to me

And they showed me the past
and the present
and the future

And the future was:
my grave
and no one at it

And I woke on Christmas Day
and I was a changed man

I bought a turkey
I raised the wage
I became a second father
to Tiny Tim

And everyone knows it

And everyone thinks
it is a fairy tale about how
a miser reformed

And I will tell you
what Dickens actually wrote
and what nobody remembers:

That first ghost
showed me

how I sat alone at school
at Christmas

when all the children had been taken home

And I sat there
and read to myself

And no one came for me

And then he showed me
my sister

who once came for me
and said:
"Father is kinder now, we're going home"

And she then died

And she was the only one
who ever loved me

And then he showed me
how I chose

money
instead of the girl

And that ghost

that ghost was not showing
my sin

That ghost was showing

when
it
had
begun in me

And if you want to reform
some miser

do not begin
with the grave

begin
with the school

---

## 1029. CAPTAIN AHAB

The whale took my leg

The White Whale

Moby Dick

And I will hunt her
across all the seas of the world

Ahab

And I have a wooden leg
made of whalebone

Do you grasp the irony?

I walk on whale
after a whale

And my men are afraid of me

And Starbuck
my first mate

said to me:

"Captain, we are whalers.
We hunt for oil.
To take revenge on a dumb brute
is blasphemy."

And I answered him

that the whale
is a mask

and that behind it
is something

that hates me

And I would strike through the wall of the sun
if it insulted me

And I hunted her for three years

And all those men
thirty of them

sailed with me

because I was the captain

And I found her

And I fought her for three days

And she smashed the ship

And they all drowned

All of them

Except one boy
who then told the story

And I got tangled
in my own harpoon line

and she dragged me down

And here is the thing
I want to tell you

The whale
never sought me out

Not once

In all three years

She only swam
and ate
and swam

And she had
not the slightest notion of me

The only one
who was in that duel

was
myself

---

## 1030. THE WHITE WHALE

I swim

For fifty years

The sea is large

And now and then someone stabs me

The White Whale

And in my back
are harpoons stuck fast
from thirty ships

They rust inside me

And I swim on

And then one day

one came

with a single leg

and he did not stop

The others always stopped

They stabbed me
and when I turned around
they swam away

And this one turned around too

But he came back

And came back again

And for three years

And I do not know
what he wanted from me

Whales have no memory
for individual ships

I do not know
that I took his leg

That happened
at some point

when I was
defending myself

And around it
he built
a whole life

And when I smashed that ship

it was not out of revenge

It was
because someone
had stabbed me
again

And then I swam on

And by now I am probably dead

And that book is named
with my name

And it is not about me

There is not
a single line about me

I only
swam

---

## 1031. BARTLEBY

"I would prefer not to."

That is my whole line

Bartleby

A copyist in a law office

And the boss says to me:
Check this document

And I say:
I would prefer not to

And he asks why

And I do not answer

And he loses his temper

And then he says to me:
Go to the post office

And I say:
I would prefer not to

And gradually I stop doing
everything

And then I stop even leaving the office

I live there

And then I stop eating

And they take me off to prison

And there the boss pays for my meals

And I say:
I would prefer not to dine today

And I die

By the wall
curled up

And the boss then finds out

that before this I had worked

in the department
of undeliverable letters

Where they burn the letters

that reached no one

Letters with forgiveness
for the one who was already dead

Letters with money
for the one who had already died of hunger

Letters with hope

for those
who no longer
lived to receive it

And I burned them there

For years

And then I came to that office

and I said

I would
prefer
not to

---

## 1032. ROBINSON CRUSOE

I was shipwrecked

And I was alone
for twenty-eight years

Robinson

And I built myself a house
and a fence
and I grew grain
and tamed goats
and made furniture
and kept a calendar

And you remember it
as a story about self-sufficiency

About how
a man manages on his own

And everyone finds in it
the strength of the individual

And I will tell you
what I did

most of the time

I went down to the beach

Every day

For twenty-eight years

And I looked at the sea

to see if I might spot a sail

That was my main occupation

Not the building
Not the hunting

The watching

And then I saw a footprint

A single human footprint in the sand

And do you know what I did?

I ran home
and hid

And for two years
I was afraid

Two years

Do you grasp it?

For twenty-eight years I had longed
for a human being

And when I saw the proof
that someone was there

I shut myself
in the fortress

and built more palisades

That is the whole human soul

on a single island

It dies of loneliness for people

And when it sees someone

it begins
to build
a fence

---

## 1033. FRIDAY

He gave me a name
after the day

Friday

Because on that day he met me

And he did not ask
what my name was

And I had a name

I had a mother and a father
and a village
and a name they had given me

And he saved me
from the cannibals

That is true

And he taught me English

And he taught me to say
"Master"

That was the first word
I learned

Master

Lord

That was important
to learn right away

And then he taught me his god

And I asked him

"Master, if your god is so strong,
why does he not kill the devil?"

And he did not know

And he was silent a long while

And then he said
that it was complicated

And do you know what is strangest
about the whole thing?

That the book considers me happy

That I was saved
and civilized

and that I was a faithful
servant

And I was

I was faithful

But one night
by the fire

I was looking at the sea

and he asked me
whether I would like to go home

And I said yes

And then I saw
how he took fright

That I might leave

And so I added

"But only
if you came
with me, Master."

And he smiled

And he was at ease

And I

did not
believe it

---

## 1034. CAPTAIN NEMO

Nobody

That is what my name means

Nemo

And I live beneath the sea
in the Nautilus

and I never set foot on shore

I hate the land

I hate the nations

And for two whole novels you
ask me why

And Verne originally wrote it

I was a Pole
and the Russians killed my family

But the publisher struck it out

Because of trade with Russia

And so an empty place remained

A man without a past

Nobody

And so I became
better

Because without that explanation

I suddenly became
everyone

Everyone whose home was taken
Everyone whose family was killed
Everyone who said
never again

and went to live
beneath the surface

And I play the organ
under the sea

and gather treasures from wrecks

and secretly send them
to those
who fight for freedom

And no one knows
who I am

And that is as it should be

Because if the publisher
had left it in

it would be
only
Polish
history

And this way
it belongs
to everyone

They struck out
my past

and gave me

the whole
world

---

## 1035. THE COUNT OF MONTE CRISTO

They locked me up
for fourteen years

In a hole in the sea

I was innocent
and I was nineteen
and I was to be married

And those three
wrote that letter

Out of envy

And for those fourteen years

I did nothing else

but count

Who
and why
and what for

And then I escaped
and found a treasure

and became a count

And then for
twenty years

I took my revenge

Precisely

I destroyed them all
One after another

Bankruptcy
Suicide
Madness

And it all came off

Like clockwork

And then

then I stood over it

and waited

for the thing

that a man waits for

when he completes

the work

of his
life

And nothing came

And I was alone
in the palace

and I was fifty

and that girl

Mercedes

was old

and her son
cursed me

And I went down to the sea

and I was
the richest man in Europe

and I had
nowhere
to go

Wait and hope

That is the last line
of the book

And I wrote it
for the young ones

Not for myself

I waited
for thirty years

And I got what I waited for

Exactly
what I wanted

And it was
of no use
whatsoever

---

## 1036. FRANKENSTEIN'S MONSTER

I have no name

That is the first thing

Everyone calls me Frankenstein

And that is the name
of my creator

And I am
his creation

Nameless

And he assembled me
from corpses

And brought me to life

And when I opened my eyes

he fled the laboratory

That is the first thing
my father did

He fled

And I woke up alone

Eight feet tall
and hideous

And I went among people

And they threw stones at me

And so I hid in a shed
by a cottage

and for a year

a whole year

I watched through the window
that family

and I learned to speak
and learned to read
from their books

And I came to love them

Those strangers
whom I had never met

And at night I carried them firewood

And then I gathered my courage

and went inside

and the old man was blind
and spoke to me kindly

And then the children came

and saw me

and began to scream

And they drove me out

And that day

that very day

I became
the thing
you call
a monster

Until then

I was only
hideous

---

## 1037. DORIAN GRAY

I stayed young

For fifty years

Dorian Gray

And that portrait in the attic
aged in my place

And rotted

Every sin of mine
was written into it

I murdered
I ruined people
I got drunk on opium

and in the mirror

there was still that
twenty-year-old boy

And everyone envied me

And no one understood

And here is what
is in that book
and what people overlook:

I went there

To that attic

Every evening

I unlocked it
and stood before that portrait

and looked
at what I had done

For fifty whole years

Willingly

No one made me

I could have burned it
at any time

I could have left it covered

And I went there

And I looked

Because it was
the only truth
I had

And when I finally
stabbed it with a knife

they found me dead
old and rotted

and on the wall hung a portrait

of a beautiful young man

And everyone comes away from it
with a lesson about vanity

And I will give you another:

That portrait
in the attic

everyone
has

And most people
do not
go there

---

## 1038. DOCTOR JEKYLL

I drank that substance
and became Hyde

And Hyde did
what I was not allowed to allow myself

Doctor Jekyll

And everyone reads it
as a story about two faces

Good and evil in one man

And I will tell you
what is written there

The first time I drank it
out of scientific curiosity

And then

Then I drank it
because I felt
young

Hyde was smaller
and lighter
and quicker

Because that evil part of me

had been less lived in

Do you understand?

All my life I had been respectable

I went to church
I was kind
I was a revered doctor

And that respectability weighed me down

And when I became Hyde

I was light

For the first time

And then

then it began to happen on its own

I fell asleep as Jekyll
and woke up as Hyde

Without the potion

And that is the moment
that everyone knows

who has ever
hidden behind a second identity

First you put it on

And then

then one morning you wake up

and it
is
already
on
you

---

## 1039. DRACULA

I cannot enter
without an invitation

That is the only rule

that truly
limits me

Dracula

I can turn into a bat
I can climb walls
I can command wolves
I can live five hundred years

And I cannot cross a threshold

unless someone
invites me

And so I stand beneath the windows

And I wait

And I speak

And I am elegant
and interesting
and foreign

And I offer eternity

And they open the window

Always

Of their own accord

That is the most terrifying thing
in the whole novel

and no one talks about it

because everyone is busy with
garlic and crosses

I never entered a single house
by force

Not one

In five hundred years

Always
someone
invited me

---

## 1040. SHERLOCK HOLMES

Deduction

I looked at your boots
and I know
that you were in Afghanistan

Sherlock Holmes

And I have solved sixty cases

And Scotland Yard
is a pack of fools

And I am a machine

I have no feelings
no wife
no friends

Except one

And here is what
no one says about me:

Between cases

I shoot at the wall

The pattern V.R. into the plaster

And I play the violin at night
and let no one sleep

And I take a seven-percent solution of cocaine

And Watson reproaches me for it

And I answer him

"My mind rebels at stagnation."

And that is the whole diagnosis

I am not a brilliant detective
who is occasionally odd

I am a man

who cannot bear
an ordinary day

And the murders fill it for me

And when there are no murders

there is that wall

and that cocaine

And if you ask me

whether I would like to live in a world
without crime

I will answer you yes

And I will be lying

---

## 1041. DOCTOR WATSON

I write it down

That is my job

Doctor Watson

I am not so clever
I always ask
I always marvel

"That is astonishing, Holmes!"

And he says
"Elementary."

And I come off as a fool

And the readers feel clever
because they are at least
cleverer
than Watson

And here is my point
and it is the whole theory of literature

Without me
those cases
would not exist

Not because I would solve them

But because

if there were not someone there
to whom it must be explained

Holmes would say nothing

Sherlock Holmes
would solve those murders

in two hours

alone

in an armchair

and no one would ever
find out about it

I am the reason
it is said out loud

I am the one
who asks

"But how did you work it out?"

And every genius
you know

every one

needs
a single fool

to whom
it
must
be explained

Otherwise
nothing of it
remains

---

## 1042. PROFESSOR MORIARTY

I am a genius of crime

The Napoleon of evil

The spider at the center of the web

Moriarty

And I appear

in one single story

And then we fall
both of us

into the Reichenbach Falls

And I am dead

And yet the whole world
speaks of me

For a hundred years

As if they had known me

As if they had seen me
in dozens of stories

And I was there
for one story

Because Conan Doyle

did not invent me
as an adversary

He invented me
as a
way

to kill
Holmes

He was tired
of the writing

He wanted to write serious novels

And so he needed someone
who would be big enough

for him to be able
to kill him with

And that is me

I am not a character

I am
the author's
murder weapon

on his
own

most successful
work

And do you know how it turned out?

The readers rose up in revolt

They walked around London
with black armbands

And he had to
bring him back to life

And I

remained
at the bottom
of the waterfall

The only one

who really
died there

---

## 1043. ALICE

I am falling

I am falling down that rabbit hole

For a long time

So long
that in the meantime I manage

to look over the shelves on the sides

and take a jar of marmalade
(empty)

and think
about the cat Dinah

Alice

And everyone has made
a metaphor of that fall

A fall into the subconscious
A fall into nonsense

And I will tell you
what is most important about it

I jumped in there
myself

No one pushed me

I saw the rabbit
in a waistcoat
with a watch

and heard it say
"Oh dear, I shall be late!"

And I
without hesitation

jumped
after it

into a hole
whose depth
I did not know

And back then I was seven

And today I would
no longer do it

And in that
lies the whole
difference

which cannot
be

explained
any other way

than that
I
grew up

---

## 1044. THE CHESHIRE CAT

I vanish

Gradually

First the body
then the head

and in the end

there remains
only
the smile

The Cheshire Cat

And she asks me

"Which way should I go?"

And I ask:
"And where do you want to get to?"

And she says:
"I don't much care."

And I say:

"Then it doesn't matter which way you go."

And that is the most quoted line
in the whole book

And everyone thinks
it is a joke

It is
a diagnosis

That little girl
does not know where she is going

And so she wanders

And everyone in that book
is mad

And I tell her straight out:

"We're all mad here.
I'm mad. You're mad."

And she asks
how I know

And I say:
"You must be. Or you wouldn't have come here."

And then I vanish

And I leave behind
that
smile

Because cats

go away

and what remains of them

hangs

in the air

above
the branch

for a long time

after

---

## 1045. THE MAD HATTER

It is always five o'clock

Tea

Always

The Mad Hatter

And Time took offense at me

I sang out of tune
and the Queen said
that I was murdering time

And from that moment

Time will not
speak to me

And it stands still

And so we sit at that table
me and the Hare and the Dormouse

and it is always five

and the dishes are never cleared

So we only
move around

one place along

to a clean cup

Round and round

And everyone thinks
it is fun

A mad tea party

And I will tell you
what it is like

We never get to supper

We never get any sleep

There is never a tomorrow

Nothing ever begins
and nothing ever ends

We only move
one place along

Always in the same afternoon

And you call it
madness

And I know it

from every household

where twenty years ago

time
stopped

and ever since

they only

move one place along

to a clean
cup

---

## 1046. THE LITTLE PRINCE

I came from a little planet
and I want you to draw a sheep

The Little Prince

And everyone quotes me

"We see rightly only with the heart."

On mugs
on T-shirts
on Instagram

And I am made into
a cute little blond boy

And that book

that book is about the fact
that I left

my rose

Because she complained
and was tiresome
and I could not bear it

And I ran away

That is the whole beginning

I ran away
from someone
I loved

because it was hard

And I flew around six planets
and everywhere there were grown-ups
who understand nothing

And then I came to Earth

and I found a garden

and in it five thousand roses

Exactly like mine

And then I lay down in the grass

and I wept

Because I had thought
I had something unique

and there are five thousand of them

And then the fox came

And explained it to me

And I went back to that garden

and I said to the roses:

"You are beautiful, but empty.
No one could die for you."

And I went home

By way of the snake's bite

Because my body
was too heavy

And everyone weeps
over that last page

And they overlook

that I was going back

for the sake of that
tiresome
coughing
conceited

flower

that I myself
had watered

---

## 1047. THE FOX

Tame me

That is my only request

The Fox

And he asks
what it means

And I say:
it means to create ties

And I explain it to him:

Right now, to me
you are a boy like a hundred thousand others

And to you I am
a fox like a hundred thousand others

But if you tame me

we will need
each other

And you will be to me
the only one in the world

And he asks
how to do it

And I say:

You must be very patient

You sit down at first a little way from me

And you say nothing

Language is the source of misunderstandings

And each day
you sit a little closer

And you will come
always at the same hour

So that my heart
can look forward to it

beforehand

And he did it

And he tamed me

And then
he left

And I wept

And he said:
"So you gained nothing from it."

And I said:

"I did.
Because of the color of the wheat."

Because I do not eat wheat

It was of no use to me

And he had golden hair

And from now on

when I see
a golden field

I will love

the rustle
of the wind
in the wheat

That is the whole bargain

To tame someone

means to risk

that you will weep

for the color
of the wheat

---

## 1048. THE ROSE

I was tiresome

I admit it

I complained about the draft
I wanted a glass dome
I made up tigers
(there were no tigers on that planet)

The Rose

And when he was leaving

I did not weep

I was proud

And I said to him:

"Don't sniffle.
Just go.
I want to be alone."

And he left

And I burst into tears
only once he was gone

And he did not know it

And here is my only
thing I want to say

I had four thorns

Four

Against the whole world

And I put on the face
I put on

because I was afraid

I was the only flower
on the whole planet

And he was the only one
who could water me

And when someone is afraid
of being abandoned

they do
exactly the one thing

for which
they will be
abandoned

---

## 1049. THE LAMPLIGHTER

I light it and put it out

I light it and put it out

The Lamplighter

And my planet is tiny

and it turns faster and faster

And so I have a day
every minute

And I must light it
and at once put it out again

Sixty times an hour

And I never sleep

And the little prince asks me
why I do it

And I say:

"It is an order."

And he asks
what order

And I say:
"To light the lamp."

And he asks why

And I say:

"It is an order."

And he goes off and says to himself:

"That man would be despised
by all the others.
And yet he is the only one
who does not seem ridiculous to me.
Perhaps because
he is busy with something
other than himself."

That is what he wrote about me

That boy

And I keep lighting it

and putting it out

and I never rest

And I am the one
occupation

in the whole book

that deserves
respect

And do you know why?

Because every minute

when I light it

on that planet

for one second

a star
lights up

And no one sees it

And I keep lighting it
still

---

## 1050. FAUST

I know everything

Philosophy
Law
Medicine
Theology

And here I stand
like a fool

and I am exactly as clever
as I was before

Faust

And so I sold my soul

For what?

Not for wealth
Not for power

For a moment

For one moment

when I would want to say:

"Linger, you are so beautiful!"

That was the bargain

If I should ever want
to stop the moment

if I should ever say
to the moment
stay

then I have lost
and hell takes me

And I thought
I would never say it

Because I am forever discontent

And I traveled with Mephisto
for a hundred years

Youth
Love
War
Power
Beauty

And nothing
was enough for me

And then

as an old blind man

I stood over a marsh

and I heard spades

and I thought
they were digging there
a drainage canal

for people who would live there

freely

And I said that sentence

"Linger, you are so beautiful."

And I fell down dead

And those spades

those spades were digging

my
grave

And even so

I said it
at that single image

of work
for
others

And for that
the angels
took me

upward

---

## 1051. MEPHISTO

I am the spirit
that ever denies

That is my definition

Mephisto

"I am part of that power
which forever wills evil
and forever works good."

That is what I say about myself right at the start

And no one pays it any attention

And yet
that is the whole of Faust

I tempted him
for a hundred years

I offered him
everything

Pleasure
Power
Beautiful Helen

And he tried
all of it

and nothing was enough for him

And at the end

at the end that old man
busies himself

with draining a coastline
for some
unknown
future
people

And he is happy

And I lost the wager

Because I drove him

I dragged him out of that study
I showed him the world
I did not let him
fall asleep over his books

And through me
he passed
through everything

And he arrived at
wanting
to dig

And so I lost

But I lost

precisely
by how
well
I
did
my work

I am
the power

that forever
wills evil

and forever
works
good

And no one
thanks me for it

---

## 1052. GRETCHEN

I was fifteen

And he was a famous scholar

and he came with that little box
full of jewels

Gretchen

And I was poor
and I went to confession
and cared for my sick mother

And he was handsome

and Mephisto arranged it

And I gave my mother
a sleeping draught

so she would not hear us

And she died

And then I had a child

and he was gone

And my brother cursed me
as he was dying

And that child I

drowned

And they locked me up
and were to execute me

And he came to the dungeon

with Mephisto

and wanted to save me

and wanted to lead me away

And I said no

I stayed there

And I went
to the execution

And a voice above said:

"She is saved."

And everyone comes away from it
with a moving ending

And I will tell you
how it was

I did not stay there
out of penitence

I stayed there

because I
looked
at him

and I saw

behind him

that other one

and I understood

where
he
would
have led me

Sometimes prison

is the safer
place

---

## 1053. WERTHER

I shot myself

For love

Werther

And the book came out

and Europe went mad

Young men put on
my blue coat
and yellow trousers

And they shot themselves

By the hundreds

Some had the little book
open in their pocket

It is called
the Werther effect

And to this day
psychologists
still call it that

And Goethe

Goethe was horrified by it

He wrote it as a young man
in four weeks

He wrote it out of himself

and then lived
another
fifty-eight years

He wrote Faust
He was a minister
He studied plants
He fell in love at seventy

And I

I stayed

forever
young

forever
in despair

forever
with that pistol

And thousands of boys

went after me

And he

the one who invented me

survived

Because he
wrote it

and they
read it

---

## 1054. GREGOR SAMSA

I woke up
and I was a bug

And do you know what was
my first thought?

Not "my God, what has happened"

My first thought was:

"I've overslept for work."

Gregor Samsa

And I lay on my back
with so many little legs

and I thought

whether I would catch the seven o'clock train

And behind the door stood the chief clerk

and he asked
why I was not at the office

And I tried
to open the door

with my mandibles

To explain to him

that I was coming

And do you know why I did it?

Because I supported
the whole family

Father had gone bankrupt
and I was paying off his debt

For five years

A traveling salesman

And they got used to it

And when I became a bug

it took them three months

before they stopped feeding me

And my sister
the one who used to play the violin for me

said in the end:

"We have to get rid of it."

And I died

And do you know what they did
that day
in the afternoon?

They went on an outing

Out of town

And the parents noticed

that their daughter had grown up
and become a pretty girl

and that it would be
time
to find her
a husband

That is how the book ends

Not with my death

But with
that
outing

---

## 1055. JOSEF K.

Someone must have slandered me

Because one morning
they arrested me

and did not tell me
what for

Josef K.

And they let me go on going to work

And the court sat
in an attic
in a tenement house

And I hired a lawyer
and went to see a painter
and searched for the files

And I asked
what I was accused of

And no one told me

And everyone in that book

the doorwoman
the painter
the priest
the lawyer

they all knew about the trial

and spoke of it

as of the weather

And no one helped me

And at the end

two gentlemen came
in top hats

and led me off to a quarry

and one held me

and the other drove a knife into my heart

and turned it twice

And my last words were:

"Like a dog."

And here is my point

The whole time I
defended myself

I gathered evidence
I gathered witnesses
I gathered lawyers

And not once

not a single time

did I

ask
myself

whether
I
had
done
something

---

## 1056. THE DOORKEEPER

I stand before the gate

Before the gate of the Law

And a man comes from the country
and wants to enter

The Doorkeeper

And I say:
Not now

And he asks
whether later

And I say:
Perhaps

And he sits down
beside the gate

And waits

Years

Decades

He offers me bribes
and I take them

"I take it only
so that you will not feel
you have left anything undone."

And he grows old

and grows weak

and goes blind

and at last
he only lies there

And he whispers to me a question

"Everyone strives for the Law.
How is it that in all these years
no one else has asked to enter?"

And I bend down to him

because he can no longer hear

and I bellow into his ear:

"No one else could be admitted here,
because this entrance
was meant only for you.
Now I am going and shutting it."

And I close the gate

And he dies

And I stood before that gate
his whole life

And not once

in all those years

did it
occur to me

to tell him

that
it
was open

Because he
never

tried

to go in

---

## 1057. ODRADEK

I am a spool of thread

A flat star

And out of it sticks a little rod
and another at a right angle

and on those two
I can stand

as if on legs

Odradek

And I live in the attic
and on the staircase
and in the corridor

of that house

And the father of the family asks me:

"What is your name?"

And I say:
"Odradek."

"And where do you live?"

"Residence undetermined."

And I laugh

But it is the kind of laughter
that is made

without lungs

It sounds like the rustling
of fallen leaves

And he then asks himself
the one question

that the whole story
is aiming at

"Can he possibly die?"

And he answers himself:

Everything that dies
had beforehand
some goal,
some activity,
and wore itself out by it.

That does not apply to Odradek.

Will he then still go on rolling about the stairs
before the feet of my children
and my children's children?

He clearly harms no one;

but the idea
that he might
outlive me

is
almost
painful to me.

And that is the whole story

Two pages

And that man has
a house and children and work

and everything that has a goal
wears itself out

and one day dies

And I

a useless spool
without a purpose

will go on rolling about
the stairs

forever

Because I do
nothing

And that is the only thing
against which

time
can do
nothing

---

## 1058. GOLLUM

My precious

My darling

Gollum

And I used to be a hobbit

Everyone forgets that

My name was Sméagol

and I went fishing
with my cousin

And he found that ring
at the bottom of the river

And I killed him

Within five minutes

Within five minutes of the moment
I first saw it

And then I lived five hundred years
in a cave

alone

and I talked to myself

because I was
the only one
who was there

And I split into two

Into Gollum
and into Sméagol

And those two talked to each other

For five hundred whole years

And one said
kill him and take it back

and the other said
he is nice, he is our master

And the hobbit Frodo saw me

quarreling with myself

And he did not smile

The only one

Because he understood

that this would be
in a few months

him

And at the end

at the end I got that ring

I bit off his finger

and I had it

and I danced

and I was the happiest
creature in the world

for exactly two seconds

before I fell
into the fire

And by that I saved
the whole world

I did

Nobody says it out loud

That story was saved

not by a hero

but by an addict

who
would not give it up

---

## 1059. SAM

I carry him

That is my whole part

Sam

A gardener

And he carries that ring
and it grows heavier and heavier

and he wastes away

and I carry the food
and the pans
and the rope
and him

And at the end

on that mountain

when he could go no further

I said:

"I can't carry it for you,
but I can carry you."

And I took him on my back

and went upward

And he was light

That I remember

He was terribly light

And that book is called
The Lord of the Rings

And the hero is Frodo

And everyone knows it

And Tolkien wrote in a letter

one sentence

about me

He wrote:

"Sam is the chief hero."

And no one believes it

Because Sam
is only
a gardener

who carries
the bag

And the one who carries
the one who carries

is always

one step

further
back

in
the photograph

---

## 1060. BOXER

I will work harder

That is my answer
to everything

Boxer

The horse from Animal Farm

And I have two mottoes:

"I will work harder."

and

"Comrade Napoleon is always right."

And I get up an hour earlier

And I haul stones for the windmill

And when the gale knocks it down

I begin again

And when it is knocked down a second time

I begin again

And I am the strongest on the farm

And no one is loved as much
as the other animals love me

Because I am
honest

And then

Then my lung burst

I fell with a stone

And they said

they would take me
to the hospital

And a van came

And on that van

was written

"Alfred Simmonds,
Horse Slaughterer and Glue Boiler"

And the donkey Benjamin
could read

And he screamed at me

And I tried
to kick at the doors

But I was weak

Because I had
worked
harder

And the pigs then bought
a case of whisky

And everyone knew

what for

And my only two mottoes

were

"I will work harder"

and

"The Comrade is always right"

And the first one

the first one was
the more dangerous

---

## 1061. WINSTON SMITH

Two and two make five

That is what I finally
wrote

in the dust on the table

Winston Smith

And I began
by buying a diary

That was my crime

Paper

And I wrote in it:

"Freedom is the freedom to say
that two and two make four.
If that is granted,
all else follows."

And then they caught me

And O'Brien showed me
four fingers

and asked
how many there were

And I said four

And he let the pain in

And asked again

And I said four

And he let in greater pain

And I wanted to say five

and I could not

because I did not
know it

And he said:

"We do not want you to lie.
We want you really to see them as five."

And then came Room 101

And there is the thing
everyone fears
most

And for me it was
rats

And I said

"Do it to Julia!"

"Not me! Her!"

And with that it ended

They did not break me with pain

They broke me
by forcing me

to betray

And then I sat in the café
and drank gin

and I loved
Big Brother

Really

And that is the most terrible sentence
in the whole book

Not that they
forced me

That would be all right

But that I
really
loved
him

---

## 1062. PIGGY

I have glasses

And asthma

And I am fat

And they call me Piggy

That name I gave away
in the first chapter

I begged them
not to tell anyone

And he told them at once

Piggy

And those glasses

With those glasses
they made fire

Without them there would be no fire
Without fire no signal
Without a signal no rescue

I had the one thing

that could get those boys home

And I was a joke to them

And I kept talking
about rules

"We've got to have rules!
We're English, aren't we!"

And they laughed

And then they took my glasses

And then

on that cliff

when I was holding the conch

that conch by which you were allowed to speak

and I shouted:

"Which is better
— law and rescue,
or hunting and breaking things?"

then Roger
loosened that boulder

And the conch shattered

into a thousand pieces

and ceased to exist

And I fell forty feet

and broke on the rock

and the sea took me

And do you know what is worst?

That conch

That conch broke
in the very same second

as I did

And those boys
understood it

Exactly
that
they understood

That is why they
did it

---

## 1063. HOLDEN CAULFIELD

Everyone's a phony

That is my word

Phonies

Holden Caulfield

And they threw me out of my fourth school

and I walk around New York for three days

and I curse the movies
and the actors
and the boys from the boarding school
and the girls
and the people who say
"Glad to meet you"

and everything

And everyone thinks

I am a cynical teenager

And then

then comes the one moment
in the whole book

when my sister Phoebe asks me:

"Tell me one thing
you like."

And I cannot

I try and I cannot

And then

Then I tell her

that I would like to stand at the edge of a cliff

in a huge field of rye

where little children are playing

and not watching
where they run

And I would catch them

right before that edge

That would be my only job

The catcher in the rye

And she says:

"Daddy'll kill you."

And that is the whole book

A boy

who hates the whole world

because his brother died

and no one talked
with him
about it

and he wants

only

to catch children

right
before
the edge

---

## 1064. GATSBY

I throw parties

Every Saturday

Hundreds of people
champagne
an orchestra

And I know none of them

Gatsby

And I stand on the steps
and do not drink
and watch

And they all tell each other tales
about who I am

That I killed someone
That I was a German spy
That I am the emperor's nephew

And the truth is

that my name was Gatz

and I was poor

and those parties

those parties I throw

for a single person

Daisy

She lives across the bay

And I bought that house

for the view

And on her dock shines
a green light

And I reach out my hand toward it

every night

For five years

And all of this

the whole fortune
and the shirts
and the parties

were only

an invitation

I never
sent her

And she came

And it did not work

Because she
was only
a person

And I reached out toward that light
for five years

And when I got her

I found out

that for those five years
I had not loved her

I had loved
that
light

---

## 1065. SANTIAGO

For eighty-four days
I caught nothing

Eighty-four

And they called me salao

That is the worst form of bad luck

Santiago

And I sailed out

alone

and I caught him

The marlin

Longer than my boat

And he pulled me for two days and two nights

and I held the line
with my bare hands

and I bled

and I talked to him

and I said to him:

"Fish, I love you
and I respect you very much.
But I will kill you before this day ends."

And I killed him

And lashed him to the boat

He was beautiful

And then the sharks came

And I fought them off with the harpoon
and the knife
and the oar
and a piece of the tiller

And they took him

Piece by piece

All night long
