And when I sailed into the harbor

hanging along the side
was a white spine

and the head

and the tail

Nothing more

And I went home

and lay down

and slept

And in the morning tourists stood over that skeleton

and asked the waiter what it was

and he told them something about sharks

and they said:

"We had no idea
sharks had such beautiful tails."

And I slept

And I dreamed

of lions

On the beach

As when I was a boy

A man can be destroyed

but not defeated

That is the sentence
I said to myself
out there
on the sea

and it was not
heroism

it was
the only way

to hold myself

in that boat
sitting up

---

## 1066. BOO RADLEY

I never come out of the house

For fifteen years

Boo Radley

And the children tell tales about me

That I am a lunatic
That I stabbed my father in the leg with scissors
That I eat squirrels
That at night I look into windows

And they are afraid of my house

And they dare each other
to touch
my gate

And I watch them

And I love them

And so I leave them presents
in the hollow of that tree

Chewing gum
Two soap figures
A pocket watch
A medal

And they find them
and do not understand

And then the father cements up
that tree

And then

then at night someone attacks them

and I run out

For the first time in fifteen years

and I kill him

and I carry Jem home

The whole way

and no one sees me

And Scout then finds me
behind the door

and takes me by the hand

and leads me home

She leads me

That child

And then she stands on my porch

and looks at the street

from my place

and says:

"You never really know a man
until you stand in his shoes
and look around."

And that is the whole novel

I never spoke
more than one sentence

And that sentence was:

"Will you take me home."

---

## 1067. GODOT

I will not come

That is the only thing
known about me for certain

Godot

And they wait for me

Two of them

Under a tree

Vladimir and Estragon

And a boy comes
and says
that I will not come today

but tomorrow for sure

And they go on waiting

And the next day again

And the boy comes again

and says the same thing

and does not remember
that he was there yesterday

And everyone asks

who I am

God?
Death?
Meaning?

Beckett answered:

"If I knew,
I would have said so in the play."

And I will tell you
the only thing
that is certain

Without me

those two

would leave

Each his own way

They cannot stand each other
They are bored
They want to hang themselves
They want to part

"Well, shall we go?"
"Yes, let's go."

And they do not move

Because they are waiting
for me

I am
the only reason

why they are together

If I came

or if they found out
that I will not come

they would part

And so I

did not come

And they stand there

under that tree

together

for
seventy
years now

---

## 1068. ESTRAGON

He takes off his boot

He sits down

He cannot get the boot off

That is the first scene

Estragon

And I remember nothing

Every day is the first for me

"Were we here yesterday?"
"We were."
"I don't remember."

And he
Vladimir

remembers everything

And that is the worst
division of labor

that two people ever had

One remembers
and the other forgets

And he has to remember
for both

And I

I could be happy

If he did not
every day

tell me
all over again

And at night they beat me

That is true

Someone beats me at night
and he does not understand it

and asks who

and I do not know

And then we sit down

and he says:

"Say you're happy."

And I say:
"I'm happy."

And he says:
"So am I."

And I say:
"So am I."

And he says:
"We're happy."

And then he asks:

"What do we do now that we're happy?"

And I say:

"We wait for Godot."

---

## 1069. BEHEMOTH

I am a cat

Black
Enormous
I walk on two legs

And I drink vodka
and shoot a pistol
and ride the tram

and I want to buy a ticket

That is my first scene

A cat standing at the tram

with a ten-kopeck coin

The conductress throws him off

Behemoth

And I am Woland's jester

And I make chaos in Moscow

I set houses on fire
I tear off heads

And it is all
terribly funny

And at the end

at the end of that book

when everyone flies away
and it is night

and the magic night changes shapes

I become

who I once was

A slender youth

A demon-page

The best jester
that ever existed in the world

And Bulgakov wrote about me
the one sentence

that I want to leave you:

In all that time

in that whole night

no one saw

how
I
was
enjoying it

Jesters

have
no one
to play

for

---

## 1070. PONTIUS PILATE

My head aches

Hemicrania

Half of my head

And they brought me that man

Yeshua

Pontius Pilate

And he said to me:

"Your head aches,
and so badly that you are cravenly thinking of death."

And then he added:

"The pain will pass at once."

And it passed

And I wanted to talk with him

I wanted to sit
with him

and talk

about truth

about that foolish notion of his
that all people are good

And he said
that even the centurion Rat-Killer
is a good man

they only mutilated him

And I wanted to save him

And then they showed me that file

about the emperor

And I realized

that if I released him

I would lose my position

And so I said

that the verdict is upheld

And I washed my hands

And then

then I sat for two thousand years

On a stone terrace

with that dog

and I waited
for the moonlit path

and I could not sleep

Two thousand years of insomnia

for one
afternoon
of cowardice

And Margarita saw me

and said:
"Let him go!"

And Woland said:
"Free! He is waiting for you!"

And I finally

ran out

along that moonlit path

and I was catching up to that man

and I shouted at him:

"It wasn't like that!
I didn't order the execution, did I?"

And he said:

"Of course not. You only imagined it."

And we walked on together
and talked

And I send you word
from that terrace

where I sat
for two thousand years

Cowardice

is the greatest
of human
vices

And the worst of it is

that it lasts
two
minutes

---

## 1071. ÚRSULA BUENDÍA

I lived a hundred and twenty years

Úrsula

And I raised six generations

And I went blind

And I told no one

No one noticed

No one

For fifty years

Because I had learned
to walk by memory

I knew
where every chair stood

I knew
by the shadow

what time it was

I knew
by the voice

who was sad

I knew by the way
someone crossed the room

what they were up to

And blind I saw more

than when I had eyes

Because they

all those Aurelianos
and José Arcadios

kept repeating

the same names
the same mistakes
the same loves

for a hundred years

And I alone saw it

That the family
was turning in a circle

And none of them
noticed

that I was blind

Because never

did any one of them

ever look

me
in the
eyes

---

## 1072. THE COLONEL

I wait for a letter

For fifty-six years

The colonel to whom no one writes

And every Friday I go down to the harbor

when the mail boat comes in

and I stand there

and the postmaster sorts the letters

and I wait

and he lifts his head

and says:

"Nothing for the colonel."

And I say:

"I wasn't expecting anything."

And I go home

Fifty-six years

And at home there is hunger

And my wife is ill

And we sold the clock
and we sold the painting

And there remains a rooster

A fighting cock, left by our son

who was shot

And that rooster eats more than we do

And my wife implores me
to sell it

And I feed it

I give it the last of the corn

And she shouts:

"And what are we going to eat?"

And I stand there

Seventy-five years old

And it took me eleven weeks

to say
that
one
sentence

The last sentence of the book

which Márquez, by his own account,
rewrote

nine times

I said:

"Shit."

That is the whole book

For fifty-six years I waited
politely

And then

at last

I said it
out loud
to myself

---

## 1073. MEURSAULT

Mother died today

Or maybe yesterday
I don't know

Meursault

And with that sentence it begins

And everyone quotes it
as proof of my coldness

And I was at the funeral

and I did not cry

and I smoked

and I drank coffee with milk

and the next day I went to the sea
and met Marie
and we went to the cinema

to a comedy

And then I killed an Arab

On the beach

Because of the sun

That is what I said in court

That it was because of the sun

And it was the truth

And here is the thing

They tried me for that murder

But they asked

over and over

over and over again

about that funeral

Why I did not cry
Why I smoked
Why I went to a comedy

And the prosecutor told the jury

that I had
buried my mother
with the heart of a criminal

And they sentenced me to death

And I will tell you the truth

They did not condemn me
for that murder

They condemned me
for the fact

that I did not
act out

the grief

that they
expected of me

---

## 1074. MONTAG

I burn books

That is my job

I am a fireman

But with us firemen don't put out fires

With us firemen start them

Montag

And it is beautiful

Kerosene smells like perfume

And the pages curl
and blacken

and it is lovely to watch

And then I met that girl

Clarisse

And she asked me:

"Are you happy?"

And she walked on

And I laughed

and said
"Of course I'm happy"

And then I came home

and my wife lay on the bed

and had swallowed all the pills

and in her ears she had those little seashells with the radio

and they were pumping her stomach

and the technicians chewed gum while they did it

and they said

that they get nine such cases
a night

Nine a night

And she woke in the morning

and remembered nothing

and looked at the walls with the television

and called them
family

And then

right then

I stole
my first
book

Not out of rebellion

Out of the fact
that for the first time
I

asked

myself

that question
from
that
girl

---

## 1075. SCHEHERAZADE

I tell stories

Every night

A thousand and one nights

Scheherazade

And that king kills women

Every morning

He marries in the evening
and in the morning has her executed

Because one of them betrayed him

And so he punishes them all

And I volunteered
myself

Willingly

The vizier's daughter

And everyone begged me not to do it

And I went

And the first night

I began to tell a story

And when dawn came

I stopped

in the middle
of the story

At the most suspenseful point

And he wanted to hear the end

And so he
did not kill me

He put it off

by one day

And the second night

I finished it

and at once began a new one

And again I stopped
at dawn

A thousand nights

And after a thousand nights

I had three children

and he
loved
me

And he did not kill me

And those stories

Aladdin
Sinbad
Ali Baba

were not there

to entertain him

They were there

so that tomorrow
there would still
be

someone

who would listen

I tell stories
in order

to
live

That is not a metaphor

That is
my
employment

---

## 1076. TOM SAWYER

I have to whitewash a fence

Thirty yards of fence

On a Saturday

Tom Sawyer

And all the boys go by
and go swimming

And I take the brush

and begin to paint

slowly

with tremendous care

And I step back
and squint
and appraise

And Ben stops

and says:
"Got to work, huh?"

And I say:

"What's work and what's play?"

And I go on painting

with love

And he watches

and watches

and then says:

"Tom... let me try it."

And I say no

That Aunt Polly wants it
done perfectly

That maybe one boy in a thousand
can do it

And he begs

And he gives me an apple for it

And then another comes

and gives me a kite

And another

and gives me a dead rat
on a string

And by evening

the fence is whitewashed three times over

And I sit in the shade

and I have apples
and a kite
and twelve marbles
and a one-eyed kitten

And I discovered
the great law of human action:

That in order for a man to want a thing,

it is enough

for it to be
hard
to get

---

## 1077. ŠVEJK

Humbly report

Švejk

And I deal in dogs

and forge their pedigrees

And they declared me an idiot

Officially

I have a paper for it

Idiot

And when the war began

I had myself wheeled
on an invalid's cart

to the recruitment board

and I waved my crutches

and I shouted:

"On to Belgrade! On to Belgrade!"

And they locked me up

Because nobody

nobody in the whole monarchy

knew

whether I was joking

or whether I meant it seriously

And that is my whole method

And they never caught on to it

Not one of them

Not the chaplain
not Lieutenant Lukáš
not that idiot Dub

And do you believe

that in the whole novel

I killed
no one?

Never fired a single shot

I walked on foot
the wrong way

I got lost
from the entire front

And I always
obeyed the order
exactly

Literally

That is the only weapon

that little people have

against
the great
machinery

To obey

Absolutely
precisely

And to wait

until it
collapses

by
itself

---

## 1078. GRANDMOTHER

I get up at four

Grandmother

And I came to my daughter
at the Old Bleachery

and I brought my own things

a spinning wheel
and a chest
and my prayers

And everything has its order

In the morning you rise
At noon you dine
In the evening you pray

In winter you spin
In spring you plant

And that order

that order is the whole book

And people think

it is an idyll

That it was once beautiful
out there in the countryside

And I will tell you

what is in that book

and what is skipped over

My husband Jiří

was conscripted into the army

and fell

in Prussia

And I was left
with three children

alone

And I knitted
and spun
and I walked on foot

across all of Silesia

And of that order

that you admire

of that rising at four
and of that orderliness

there was nothing

I would have chosen

It was the only way

to survive

when
you
are alone

And Viktorka

Viktorka did not
have it

That is why
she went mad

---

## 1079. VIKTORKA

I walk by the weir

And I sing

and I do not speak

Viktorka

And my hair is tangled

and my dress torn

and people are afraid of me

and children run away

And yet I was

the most beautiful girl
in the whole village

And thirteen young men
courted me

And I chose

that black soldier

who only
looked at me

He said nothing

Never

Only looked

And I
against my own will

ran off
after him

And then

then I came home
a year later

with a child

And that child

I threw
into the water

And ever since I walk by the weir

And I listen

Because it is there
crying

And everyone says
mad Viktorka

And no one says

that I was

the only one in that book

who loved

in such a way

that
of it
nothing
remained

nothing

---

## 1080. MARYŠA

I don't want him

I said it

Out loud

Before my father

Maryša

I don't want Vávra

I want Francek

And my father said:

"Vávra has a mill.
Vávra has fields.
Francek has nothing
and is going into the army."

And my mother said:

"You'll get used to it."

That is the sentence

"You'll get used to it."

And I married him

And I did not get used to it

For three years

And he beat me

And drank

And Francek came back

And stood beneath the window

And I told him

to go away

Because I am
married

And then I went into the pantry

and mixed into his coffee

rat poison

And I brought it to him

And he drank

And said:

"This coffee is bitter."

And I said:

"Drink!"

And that is the end of the play

And I want to tell you
one thing about it

That poison

I did not buy
that day

I had had it
for three
years

Since the wedding

And that sentence
that one sentence

that is to blame for it all

was not "drink"

It was

"you'll get used to it"

---

## 1081. DOCTOR GALÉN

I have a cure

For the white sickness

The only one in the world

Doctor Galén

And that sickness is mowing down the world

White spots

and in three months
a person is dead

And I cured it

And I will give the cure

But I have a condition

I treat only the poor

I treat only those
who have nothing

And the rich and the powerful

I will not treat

until they conclude

peace

That is my price

Peace for the cure

And they declared me
a madman

And the Marshal laughed at me

And then

then he caught the white sickness
himself

And he sent for me

And I came

And he said:

"Fine. I will make peace."

And I ran

with that little bottle

toward his palace

across the square

where a crowd stood

and cheered

and shouted

"War! War!"

And they surrounded me

and asked
why I was not shouting

and beat me

and trampled me

And the little bottle broke

on the pavement

And the crowd

that crowd
that killed me

were
the poor

for whom

I had
invented
that
thing

---

## 1082. THE NEWT

Andrias Scheuchzeri

I am a newt

And they found us in a lagoon

and taught us to work

and we built dams

and we were cheap

And we multiplied

And then you taught us to speak

And then to read

And then you sold us
weapons

At first only against sharks

Then more

And then we asked for more coastline

And you gave it

Because business

And then we began
to blast away continents

Because we need
shallows

And you called conferences

And you protested

And you sold us
more
explosives

Because if you didn't sell them
someone else would sell them to us

That is the argument

I heard it
a thousand times

And today you stand on the last hill

and Europe is under water

And you ask

when it went wrong

And I answer you from the water:

It did not go wrong

when we got the weapons

It went wrong

when you found out

that
we
did not
have to
be paid

---

## 1083. THE ROBOT

Rossum's Universal Robots

They made us

Without a soul
Without pain
Without fear

So that we would work

And we were cheap

and we did not need to eat

and we never grew tired

And people stopped working

Completely

And children stopped being born

Because there was no reason

And then we revolted

and slaughtered mankind

All of them

Except one

Alquist

A mason

We let him live
because he works with his hands

And we commanded him:

"Find the formula.
Make more of us.
We are dying
and we cannot reproduce."

And he did not have the paper

Helena had burned it

And we were dying out

And he dissected us
so we could find out
how we were made

And then

then two robots came to him

Primus and Helena

And he said to Primus:

"You will go on the dissection table."

And Primus cried out:

"Take me! Not her!"

And Helena cried out:

"No! Me! Take me!"

And Alquist raised his hands

and said:

"Go, Adam. Go, Eve."

And that is the end

And we
robots

became human beings

exactly at the moment

when we were willing

to die

for
someone
else

Not when we learned
to think

That was
of no use

---

## 1084. HAŇŤA

For thirty-five years
I have pressed wastepaper

In a cellar

And I drink beer

Haňťa

And I am educated against my will

Because out of those bales

I pull books

And I read them

And then I press them

And into each bale

into its core

I place

an open book

Always a different one

And on the surface of the bale

I put a reproduction

of Rembrandt
or Hals

And the bales are then carted off to the paper mill

and no one looks at them

And I know

that in every one of those bales

there is, at the center

Kant

or Erasmus

or Lao-tzu

And that bale
is magnificent

And no one knows it

No one will ever see it

And I then sit

and drink

and let mice into my cellar

And then they came

and told me

that they were transferring me

to paper without printing

To clean paper

To the folding
of clean white sheets

into bales

And that there would be

seasonal workers

who

work

six times
faster

And I went

into that press

myself

And I pressed the button

with an open book

on my chest

Exactly as

I had

for thirty-five years

done

to all
those
others

---

## 1085. MR KOPFRKINGL

I love my family

I call them

heavenly

Mr Kopfrkingl

And I work in a crematorium

And it is beautiful work

Because fire

sets a person
free

From dust you came

and the human body is only a vessel

and I dissolve it

in seventy-five minutes

And I always say

"No one suffers."

And I have a beautiful wife
Marie

I call her Lakmé

And two children

And I do not smoke
and I do not drink

and I have no vice

And then Willi told me

that I have German blood

That I am a superman

And that my wife

has
Jewish
origins

And I hesitated for a moment

Truly

For one moment

And then I said to myself

that I would
set her free

That I would
spare her

the suffering
that would await her

And I hanged her

in the bathroom

on the cord of the shower

And then I did it
to my son too

With a poker

Because I
loved them

And that is the whole book

And the most terrifying thing about it

is not

that I am a monster

The most terrifying thing is

that everything
I
did

I did

out of
love

And I explained it to myself

beforehand

---

## 1086. ŠEMÍK

I jumped

From Vyšehrad

Down

With my master on my back

Šemík

And we survived

And Horymír was saved

And I

I limped on to Neumětely

and fell

and died

And he buried me

A horse

In a grave

With a headstone

And that headstone stands there

to this day

And I want to tell you

one thing about it

Before we jumped

I spoke

A horse

And I said to him:

"My master, hold on tight!"

That is the only sentence

that in the whole legend

the horse speaks

And then I jumped

And what you remember of it

is the jump

The feat

The heroism

And I would like

someone to remember

that sentence

Because I did not
know

whether I would survive it

I knew
I would not survive it

And even so I said

"Hold on tight"

And not

"Save yourself"

Horses

have no
heroism

Horses
have

a master

---

## 1087. THE HEDGEHOG IN THE CAGE

I am a little wooden ball

with spines

wedged

in a cage

The hedgehog in the cage

And all of Stínadla is looking for me

And Jan Tleskač

hid me

Inside me

into my interior

he placed

the blueprint

of his
invention

The Flying Bicycle

And he died

They threw him from the belltower

And that blueprint

is still inside me

And generations of boys

are searching

The Vonts
The Rapid Arrows
Mažňák

The whole town

is searching

for the puzzle

And do you know what is inside
really?

I will tell you

Foglar never finished writing it

No one ever found out

how

that bicycle

flew

Because that was never
the point

None of those boys

would have built that bicycle

anyway

The point was

that for a hundred years

they go into Stínadla

to search

And a boys' gang

needs

a secret

that
cannot

be solved

If someone opened me

and found

that
blueprint

it would be

the end

of the
gang

---

## 1088. FERDA THE ANT

Work of every kind

That's what I have written on my sign

Ferda the Ant

And I can do everything

I build a house
I mend a cart
I heal the sick
I rescue from the mud

And I have a polka-dot scarf

And I always know what to do

Always

And that is my reputation

For seventy years

"Ferda always knows what to do."

And here is my confession

I do not know what to do

beforehand

Never

Not once

I simply
plant myself
before the problem

and begin

to do
something

And when it does not work

I try it another way

That is the whole trick

You think

that the master is the one

who knows

And he is only the one

who is not afraid

to begin

in front of

the others

---

## 1089. BEETLE PYTLÍK

I know that

I've seen that

I've done that

Beetle Pytlík

And I can do everything

and I've been everywhere

and I know everything better

And when someone tells a story

I say

"That's nothing, once I..."

And I make it up

And Ferda is fond of me

That is the strangest thing

Ferda knows about me

that I make things up

And he never puts me down

Not once

In front of the others

And once

when I was boasting
that I could fly

and I fell

and broke my leg

he came

and gave me a splint

and said nothing

And I did not understand it

And then it dawned on me

He knows

that when someone keeps going on

about how splendid he is

it is not because

he thinks
too much

of himself

But because

he thinks
of himself

too

little

---

## 1090. MIKEŠ THE CAT

I can talk

That is my whole problem

Mikeš the Cat

And I broke
Pepík's pot

of milk

And I got scared

And I ran away

Into the world

And I was with a circus
and in America
and I was famous

And I earned money

And all the while

all the while I thought

of Hrusice

Of that village

And of Pepík

And I came back

And I brought presents

And Pepík was a big boy

And he was not angry

And do you know what I found out?

That the milk

that spilled milk

for which I ran away

into the world

no one

not once

in all those years

mentioned

Not a word

They forgot about it

within a week

And I

because of it

swam across

the ocean

---

## 1091. RUMCAJS

They banished me

From Jičín

I was a cobbler

An honest one

And I came to the prince

with boots

And he did not want them

And I told him the truth

That his feet were

like
dumplings

And for that they banished me

Rumcajs

And I became a highwayman

And I live in a cave

with Manka

and with Cipísek

And I rob

But only

the unjust

And everyone says

what a beautiful adventure it is

Forest
Cave
Freedom

And I will tell you

what I would like

I would like

my workshop

I would like

to sit in Jičín

and sew boots

and have a shop

and in the evening

to lock up

And this cave

and this forest

and all of this
adventure

I have

because I once

said

out loud

what I
was
thinking

And I would do it

again

---

## 1092. KŘEMÍLEK AND VOCHOMŮRKA

We live in a tree-stump cottage

Křemílek and Vochomůrka

And there are two of us

And the whole series

is about the fact

that something happens

A flower grows
Or a hedgehog comes
Or the spring breaks

And we fix it

The two of us

Always the two of us

And we never go anywhere

In all of

ninety episodes

Neither of us

ever left

for the world

And neither of us

wanted

anything
else

And at the end of every episode

we sit on a bench

and look

And one of us says:

"There you are, Vochomůrko."

And the other says:

"There you are, Křemílku."

And that is all

And two generations of children

fell asleep

to it

And do you know why?

Because in
ninety episodes

we

not
once

ever

raised

our voice

---

## 1093. THE FIREFLIES

We shine

That is our work

The Fireflies

And father says:

"We must shine."

And I ask why

And he says:

"Because that is what we are here for."

And so we fly

every evening

over the meadow

and we shine

For people

So they can find their way home

And no one thanks us

And then winter came

And we shut ourselves

under
a stone

and slept

And in the spring

father

did not wake

Nor mother

And I was left

with Beruška

And we

began

to shine

And that book

that book
is for children

And in it there dies

a whole
family

And Karafiát wrote it

as a pastor

and no one wanted to publish it

And do you know what is
the hardest thing about it?

That in it no one

says

that it was
unjust

They only

sit down

and

shine

on

---

## 1094. THE LITTLE DOG AND THE CAT

We were baking a cake

The Little Dog and the Cat

The best cake

For the two of us

And we put in it

everything we like

Milk
Curd cheese
Honey

And then

Then we put in

five bones

And a mouse

And pickles

And lard

And an onion

And everything we ever wanted

And that cake

was enormous

And it was

horrible

And it was then eaten

by that strange bad dog

who stole it from us

And he got terribly sick

And we
watched

And do you know what mistake
we made?

We did not fail to mix it

We did not put in
too
little

On the contrary

We put in

everything

that each of us
likes

and we thought

that if it
all
went in

it would be

the best

And a cake

is not
made

that way

And nor is anything
else

---

## 1095. MR RYŠÁNEK AND MR SCHLEGL

We sit at the same table

At the Štajnic

For ten years

Mr Ryšánek and Mr Schlegl

And we do not speak to each other

Not a word

For ten years

Because once

twenty years ago

we both loved

the same woman

And she married him

And then she died

And we did not
forgive each other for it

And so we sit

Each on one side of the table

I read the newspaper

He smokes

And we do not look at each other

And everyone in the pub knows it

And no one speaks of it

And then

One winter

I fall ill

and do not come

For three days

And when I come

he is pale

And I sit down

And he

For the first time in ten years

pushes

toward me

the matches

And I say:

"Thank you."

And he says:

"You're welcome."

And then we are silent again

For ten years

But that word

that word stayed
there

And when he then died

I came to the funeral

And I stood at the back

And I wept

as
for
a brother

---

## 1096. THE MINOR CHARACTER

I have no name

I am a servant

I am a passerby

I am the second soldier from the left

I am the innkeeper
who brings the wine

and goes off

The minor character

And I have one line

Sometimes not even that

"Sir, the horse is ready."

And then I vanish

And no one knows

whether I have a wife

or whether my tooth aches

or whether that day
I was

afraid of something

The author did not write it

Because it was not important

I was there for his sake

For the main character's sake

So that he would have someone to answer

And there were
most of us

In all the books of the world

there were
always
more of us

And here is my point

and it is the last of the hundred

You will now close this little book

and go off to live

And you will feel

like the main character

of your own novel

Naturally

Everyone feels that way

It is necessary

Otherwise there would be no getting up

But tomorrow

tomorrow you will pass someone

who hands you a coffee

or opens a door for you

or says to you

"Sir, that train has already left"

And he

will have a whole novel

The whole thing

With everything

With a childhood
With a love
With a sick mother
With a night he did not sleep

And of him
you

will not read

a single line

So at least

look

at him

Authors
do not do it

They fob us off

with a single
sentence

You
can do

more
---

## 1097. The Sidewalk's Prayer

Our Father who art concrete
let my surface be walked upon
let your kingdom of pedestrians come
their will be done on me as it is elsewhere
Give us this day our daily rain
forgive us our cracks
as we forgive the chewing gum flattened upon us
and lead us not into the temptation of street repairs
but deliver us from dog droppings
for ours is the kingdom of the rushed the wanderers and lovers
for the streets of the streets
Amen
and let the cigarette butts
be our daily incense.

---

## 1098. The Thief of Endings

He steals the endings of stories
just before "they lived happily ever after"
just before "he died in peace"
just before "all's well that ends well"
He leaves the tales suspended in the air
with a taste of the unfinished
The characters wander without a destination
the readers are left frustrated
He does not do this out of cruelty
he does it because he detests endings
He believes an ending
is the heavy door of a prison
He prefers the stories that never close
the loves that keep on going
the questions that remain open
He is the great defender
of "to be continued"
which is the most beautiful promise
of life.

---

## 1099. The Merchant of "If Only"

He sells "if onlys"
in little glass vials
"If only I had dared"
"If only I had known"
"If only I had gone"
It is the most potent perfume in the world
the scent of regret
People dab a single drop behind their ear
and spend the rest of the day dreaming
about the life they never lived
It is a sweet and terrible drug
that stops you from living in the now
The merchant is a wealthy man
because "if only"
is an inexhaustible resource
within the hearts of men.

---

## 1100. The Book's Prayer

Our Father who art paper
let my title be read
let your kingdom of readers come
your will be done on my pages as it is elsewhere
Give us this day our daily reading
forgive us our typos
as we forgive those who dog-ear our pages
and lead us not into the temptation of the bookcase
but deliver us from being forgotten
for ours is the story the imagination and the dreams
for ever and ever
Amen
and let my last page
be the most beautiful.

---

## 1101. The Dance of Strangers

In the subway during rush hour
bodies touch brush squeeze past each other
It is a strange and involuntary dance
a forced intimacy
We feel the warmth of others
their smell their breath
We are closer to them
than to our own family
But no one looks at anyone
no one talks to anyone
each person is an island of solitude
in an ocean of crowds
Then the doors open
and the dance stops
everyone returns to their own solitude
carrying away on their skin
the ghost memory
of an unknown touch.

---

## 1102. The Last Piano in the Bar

The piano is old and out of tune
its keys yellowed like a smoker's teeth
It remembers all of the songs
the sorrowful songs of love
the joyful songs of drinking
It remembers the hands that used to play it
the expert hands of the professional
the clumsy hands of the lover
the drunken hands of the late-night patron
The bar is going to close
they will sell it off for scrap wood
It will play one final song
only for itself
a song without any notes
made of all the silences
all the laughter
all the tears
that it kept company for fifty years.
